714: install_qwen() {
715:   local src="$INTEGRATIONS/qwen/agents"
716:   local dest="$PWD/.qwen/agents"
717:   local count=0 f base
718: 
719:   check_tool_integration "qwen" || return 1
720: 
721:   if $DRY_RUN; then
722:     while IFS= read -r -d '' f; do
723:       base="$(basename "$f")"
724:       printf '  [dry-run] would copy: %s -> %s/%s\n' "$f" "$dest" "$base"
725:       count=$(( count + 1 ))
726:     done < <(find "$src" -maxdepth 1 -name '*.md' -type f -print0 2>/dev/null | sort -z)
727:     ats_ok "Qwen: $count skills (dry-run, project-scoped to $PWD)"
728:     return 0
729:   fi
730: 
731:   mkdir -p "$dest"
732:   while IFS= read -r -d '' f; do
733:     base="$(basename "$f")"
734:     install_file "$f" "$dest/$base"
735:     count=$(( count + 1 ))
736:   done < <(find "$src" -maxdepth 1 -name '*.md' -type f -print0 2>/dev/null | sort -z)
737: 
738:   ats_ok "Qwen Code: $count skills -> $dest"
739:   ats_warn "Qwen: project-scoped. Run from your project root."
740: }
741: 
742: install_kimi() {
743:   local src="$INTEGRATIONS/kimi"
744:   local dest="${HOME}/.config/kimi/agents"
745:   local count=0 skill_dir skill_name
746: 
747:   check_tool_integration "kimi" || return 1
748: 
749:   while IFS= read -r -d '' skill_dir; do
750:     skill_name="$(basename "$skill_dir")"
751:     if $DRY_RUN; then
752:       printf '  [dry-run] would install: %s -> %s/%s/{agent.yaml,system.md}\n' \
753:         "$skill_dir" "$dest" "$skill_name"
754:       count=$(( count + 1 ))
755:       continue
756:     fi
757:     mkdir -p "$dest/$skill_name"
758:     install_dir "$skill_dir" "$dest/$skill_name"
759:     count=$(( count + 1 ))
760:   done < <(find "$src" -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -type d -print0 2>/dev/null | sort -z)
761: 
762:   ats_ok "Kimi Code: $count skills -> $dest"
763: }
764: 
765: install_aider() {
766:   local src="$INTEGRATIONS/aider/CONVENTIONS.md"
767:   local dest="$PWD/CONVENTIONS.md"
768: 
769:   check_tool_integration "aider" || return 1
770:   if [[ ! -f "$src" ]]; then
771:     ats_err "integrations/aider/CONVENTIONS.md missing. Run convert.sh --tool aider first."
772:     return 1
773:   fi
774: 
775:   if $DRY_RUN; then
776:     printf '  [dry-run] would copy: %s -> %s\n' "$src" "$dest"
777:     ats_ok "Aider: (dry-run, project-scoped to $PWD)"
778:     return 0
779:   fi
780: 
781:   # Per contract: refuse to overwrite existing file (user-edited project file)
782:   if [[ -f "$dest" ]]; then
783:     ats_err "$dest exists; remove or rename before install"
784:     return 1
785:   fi
786: 
787:   cp "$src" "$dest"
788:   ats_ok "Aider: installed -> $dest"
789:   ats_warn "Aider: project-scoped. Run from your project root."
790: }
791: 
792: install_windsurf() {
793:   local src="$INTEGRATIONS/windsurf/.windsurfrules"
794:   local dest="$PWD/.windsurfrules"
795: 
796:   check_tool_integration "windsurf" || return 1
797:   if [[ ! -f "$src" ]]; then
798:     ats_err "integrations/windsurf/.windsurfrules missing. Run convert.sh --tool windsurf first."
799:     return 1
800:   fi
801: 
802:   if $DRY_RUN; then
803:     printf '  [dry-run] would copy: %s -> %s\n' "$src" "$dest"
804:     ats_ok "Windsurf: (dry-run, project-scoped to $PWD)"
805:     return 0
806:   fi
807: 
808:   # Per contract: refuse to overwrite existing file (user-edited project file)
809:   if [[ -f "$dest" ]]; then
810:     ats_err "$dest exists; remove or rename before install"
811:     return 1
812:   fi
813: 
814:   cp "$src" "$dest"
815:   ats_ok "Windsurf: installed -> $dest"
816:   ats_warn "Windsurf: project-scoped. Run from your project root."
817: }
818: 
819: install_tool() {
820:   local t="$1"
821:   case "$t" in
822:     claude-code)  install_claude_code  ;;
823:     copilot)      install_copilot      ;;
824:     antigravity)  install_antigravity  ;;
825:     gemini-cli)   install_gemini_cli   ;;
826:     opencode)     install_opencode     ;;
827:     cursor)       install_cursor       ;;
828:     openclaw)     install_openclaw     ;;
829:     qwen)         install_qwen         ;;
830:     kimi)         install_kimi         ;;
831:     aider)        install_aider        ;;
832:     windsurf)     install_windsurf     ;;
833:     *)            ats_err "Unknown tool: $t"; return 1 ;;
834:   esac
835: }
836: 
837: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
838: # Worker entry point (parallel mode)
839: # When ATS_INSTALL_WORKER=1, run a single tool and exit, suppressing TUI.
840: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
841: if [[ "${ATS_INSTALL_WORKER:-}" == "1" && -n "${ATS_INSTALL_TOOL:-}" ]]; then
842:   install_tool "${ATS_INSTALL_TOOL}"
843:   exit 0
844: fi
845: 
846: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
847: # Main
848: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
849: main() {
850:   local interactive_mode="auto"
851:   local use_parallel=false
852:   local parallel_jobs
853:   parallel_jobs="$(nproc_count)"
854:   local explicit_tools=()
855:   local use_all=false
856:   local use_detected=false
857: 
858:   while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
859:     case "$1" in
860:       --tool)          explicit_tools+=("${2:?'--tool requires a value'}"); shift 2 ;;
861:       --all)           use_all=true; shift ;;
862:       --detected)      use_detected=true; shift ;;
863:       --interactive)   interactive_mode="yes"; shift ;;
864:       --no-interactive) interactive_mode="no"; shift ;;
865:       --parallel)      use_parallel=true; shift ;;
866:       --jobs)          parallel_jobs="${2:?'--jobs requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
867:       --dry-run)       DRY_RUN=true; shift ;;
868:       --wire-hooks)    WIRE_HOOKS=true; shift ;;
869:       --help|-h)       usage ;;
870:       -*)              ats_err "Unknown option: $1"; exit 2 ;;
871:       *)               explicit_tools+=("$1"); shift ;;
872:     esac
873:   done
874: 
875:   # Validate explicit tools
876:   local t valid
877:   for t in "${explicit_tools[@]+"${explicit_tools[@]}"}"; do
878:     valid=false
879:     local v
880:     for v in "${ALL_TOOLS[@]}"; do [[ "$v" == "$t" ]] && valid=true && break; done
881:     if ! $valid; then
882:       ats_err "Unknown tool '$t'. Valid: ${ALL_TOOLS[*]}"
883:       exit 2
884:     fi
885:   done
886: 
887:   check_integrations
888: 
889:   if $DRY_RUN; then
890:     ats_header "Skill Madness -- Skill Installer (DRY RUN)"
891:   else
892:     ats_header "Skill Madness -- Skill Installer"
893:   fi
894: 
895:   SELECTED_TOOLS=()
896: 
897:   # Determine selection mode
898:   if [[ ${#explicit_tools[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then
899:     SELECTED_TOOLS=("${explicit_tools[@]}")
900:   elif $use_all; then
901:     SELECTED_TOOLS=("${ALL_TOOLS[@]}")
902:   elif $use_detected; then
903:     for t in "${ALL_TOOLS[@]}"; do
904:       is_detected "$t" 2>/dev/null && SELECTED_TOOLS+=("$t")
905:     done
906:   else
907:     # Auto-mode: TUI if stdin+stdout are TTY, else detected
908:     if [[ "$interactive_mode" == "yes" ]] || \
909:        [[ "$interactive_mode" == "auto" && -t 0 && -t 1 ]]; then
910:       interactive_select
911:     else
912:       printf '\n  Scanning for installed tools...\n\n'
913:       for t in "${ALL_TOOLS[@]}"; do
914:         if is_detected "$t" 2>/dev/null; then
915:           SELECTED_TOOLS+=("$t")
916:           printf '  %s[*]%s  %s  %sdetected%s\n' \
917:             "${C_GREEN}" "${C_RESET}" "$(tool_label "$t")" "${C_DIM}" "${C_RESET}"
918:         else
919:           printf '  %s[ ]  %s  not found%s\n' "${C_DIM}" "$(tool_label "$t")" "${C_RESET}"
920:         fi
921:       done
922:     fi
923:   fi
924: 
925:   if [[ ${#SELECTED_TOOLS[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
926:     ats_warn "No tools selected or detected. Nothing to install."
927:     printf '\n'
928:     ats_dim "  Tip: use --tool <name> to force-install a specific tool."
929:     ats_dim "  Available: ${ALL_TOOLS[*]}"
930:     exit 0
931:   fi
932: 
933:   printf '\n'
934:   printf '  Repo:       %s\n' "$REPO_ROOT"
935:   printf '  Installing: %s\n' "${SELECTED_TOOLS[*]}"
936:   if $DRY_RUN; then
937:     printf '  Mode:       DRY RUN (no files will be written)\n'
938:   fi
939:   printf '\n'
940: 
941:   local installed=0 errors=0
942:   local n_selected=${#SELECTED_TOOLS[@]}
943: 
944:   if $use_parallel && (( n_selected > 1 )); then
945:     local install_out_dir
946:     install_out_dir="$(ats_mktemp_dir)"
947:     local tools_list
948:     tools_list="$(ats_mktemp_file)"
949:     for t in "${SELECTED_TOOLS[@]}"; do printf '%s\n' "$t"; done > "$tools_list"
950: 
951:     # Propagate the opt-in wiring choice: workers bypass main's arg parsing and
952:     # read WIRE_HOOKS from ATS_WIRE_HOOKS at load time.
953:     if $WIRE_HOOKS; then export ATS_WIRE_HOOKS=1; fi
954:     export ATS_INSTALL_WORKER=1 INTEGRATIONS DRY_RUN REPO_ROOT
955:     # shellcheck disable=SC2016 # single quotes: xargs shell expansion
956:     xargs -P "$parallel_jobs" -I {} sh -c \
957:       'ATS_INSTALL_TOOL="{}" ATS_INSTALL_WORKER=1 '"$SCRIPT_DIR"'/install.sh --tool "{}" --no-interactive > "'"$install_out_dir"'/{}" 2>&1' \
958:       < "$tools_list"
959:     unset ATS_INSTALL_WORKER
960: 
961:     for t in "${SELECTED_TOOLS[@]}"; do
962:       [[ -f "$install_out_dir/$t" ]] && cat "$install_out_dir/$t"
963:     done
964:     rm -rf "$install_out_dir" "$tools_list"
965:     installed=$n_selected
966:   else
967:     local i=0
968:     for t in "${SELECTED_TOOLS[@]}"; do
969:       (( i++ )) || true
970:       progress_bar "$i" "$n_selected"
971:       printf '\n'
972:       printf '  %s[%s/%s]%s %s\n' "${C_DIM}" "$i" "$n_selected" "${C_RESET}" "$t"
973:       local rc=0
974:       install_tool "$t" || rc=$?
975:       if (( rc != 0 )); then
976:         errors=$(( errors + 1 ))
977:       else
978:         installed=$(( installed + 1 ))
979:       fi
980:     done
981:   fi
982: 
983:   printf '\n'
984:   box_top
985:   if $DRY_RUN; then
986:     box_row "${C_BOLD}  Dry run complete. No files written.${C_RESET}"
987:   else
988:     box_row "${C_GREEN}${C_BOLD}  Done! Installed ${installed} tool(s).${C_RESET}"
989:   fi
990:   box_bot
991:   printf '\n'
992: 
993:   if (( errors > 0 )); then
994:     printf '[install] %d error(s) during install\n' "$errors" >&2
995:     exit 1
996:   fi
997: }
998: 
999: main "$@"

=== scripts/install-plan.sh ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env bash
2: #
3: # install-plan.sh — Pure resolution of an install into a serializable JSON plan.
4: #
5: # Resolves selected tools x profile-selected skills into a list of file
6: # operations, reading destinations from the install-locations matrix. Computes
7: # each operation's action (create|overwrite|skip) by comparing the source
8: # content hash to any existing destination file. NO WRITES are performed.
9: #
10: # Usage:
11: #   scripts/install-plan.sh --tool NAME[,NAME...] [--profile NAME] \
12: #                           [--root DIR] [--integrations DIR] [--out FILE]
13: #
14: # Options:
15: #   --tool NAME       Tool(s) to plan for. Comma-separated or repeatable. Required.
16: #   --profile NAME    Named profile from manifests/profiles.json (default: full).
17: #   --root DIR        Override ~ (HOME) and $PWD bases for destinations
18: #                     (default: $HOME). Tests pass a temp dir here.
19: #   --integrations DIR  Source integrations dir (default: <repo>/integrations).
20: #   --out FILE        Write the plan JSON here (default: stdout).
21: #   --help            Show this help and exit.
22: #
23: # Emits: { schema_version, generated_at, profile, tools,
24: #          operations:[ {tool, source, dest, action, sha256} ] }
25: #
26: # Exit codes: 0 success, 2 argument/resolution error.
27: 
28: set -euo pipefail
29: 
30: SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
31: LIB_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/lib"
32: REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
33: 
34: # shellcheck source=scripts/lib/term.sh
35: . "$LIB_DIR/term.sh"
36: # shellcheck source=scripts/lib/install-state.sh
37: . "$LIB_DIR/install-state.sh"
38: 
39: PROFILES_JSON="$REPO_ROOT/manifests/profiles.json"
40: SKILLS_ROOT="$REPO_ROOT/skills"
41: SCHEMA_VERSION=1
42: 
43: ALL_TOOLS="claude-code copilot antigravity gemini-cli opencode cursor openclaw qwen kimi aider windsurf"
44: 
45: usage() {
46:   sed -n '3,30p' "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'
47:   exit 0
48: }
49: 
50: main() {
51:   local tools_csv=""
52:   local profile="full"
53:   local root="${HOME:-/tmp}"
54:   local integrations="$REPO_ROOT/integrations"
55:   local out=""
56: 
57:   while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
58:     case "$1" in
59:       --tool)         tools_csv="${tools_csv:+$tools_csv,}${2:?'--tool requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
60:       --profile)      profile="${2:?'--profile requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
61:       --root)         root="${2:?'--root requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
62:       --integrations) integrations="${2:?'--integrations requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
63:       --out)          out="${2:?'--out requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
64:       --help|-h)      usage ;;
65:       *)              ats_err "Unknown option: $1"; exit 2 ;;
66:     esac
67:   done
68: 
69:   if [[ -z "$tools_csv" ]]; then
70:     ats_err "--tool is required"
71:     exit 2
72:   fi
73:   if [[ ! -d "$integrations" ]]; then
74:     ats_err "integrations dir not found: $integrations (run scripts/convert.sh first)"
75:     exit 2
76:   fi
77: 
78:   # Resolve profile categories (space-separated).
79:   local cats
80:   if ! cats="$(profile_categories "$profile" "$PROFILES_JSON")"; then
81:     ats_err "Unknown profile: $profile"
82:     exit 2
83:   fi
84:   cats="$(printf '%s' "$cats" | tr '\n' ' ')"
85: 
86:   # Normalize tool list, validate, dedupe (preserve order).
87:   local tools="" t valid
88:   local IFS_OLD="$IFS"; IFS=','
89:   # shellcheck disable=SC2086
90:   set -- $tools_csv
91:   IFS="$IFS_OLD"
92:   for t in "$@"; do
93:     [[ -z "$t" ]] && continue
94:     valid=0
95:     for v in $ALL_TOOLS; do [[ "$v" == "$t" ]] && valid=1 && break; done
96:     if [[ "$valid" -eq 0 ]]; then ats_err "Unknown tool: $t"; exit 2; fi
97:     case " $tools " in *" $t "*) : ;; *) tools="${tools:+$tools }$t" ;; esac
98:   done
99: 
100:   # Build category->1 membership lookup file for the python resolver.
101:   # Pass everything to python which enumerates sources + computes hashes/actions.
102:   local generated_at
103:   generated_at="$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
104: 
105:   # Pre-compute the canonical slug->category table once (TSV) for python.
106:   _ats_build_slug_cat "$SKILLS_ROOT"
107:   local slugcat_tmp; slugcat_tmp="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-slugcat.XXXXXX")"
108:   printf '%s\n' "$_ATS_SLUG_CAT" > "$slugcat_tmp"
109: 
110:   # Detect hash tool once and export the resolved command name for python.
111:   _ats_detect_hash
112: 
113:   local plan_json rc=0
114:   plan_json="$(
115:     ATS_INTEG="$integrations" \
116:     ATS_ROOT="$root" \
117:     ATS_CATS="$cats" \
118:     ATS_TOOLS="$tools" \
119:     ATS_PROFILE="$profile" \
120:     ATS_GENERATED_AT="$generated_at" \
121:     ATS_SCHEMA="$SCHEMA_VERSION" \
122:     ATS_SLUGCAT="$slugcat_tmp" \
123:     ATS_HASH_CMD="$_ATS_HASH_CMD" \
124:     python3 - <<'PYEOF'
125: import hashlib, json, os, sys
126: 
127: integ   = os.environ["ATS_INTEG"]
128: root    = os.environ["ATS_ROOT"]
129: cats    = set(os.environ["ATS_CATS"].split())
130: tools   = os.environ["ATS_TOOLS"].split()
131: profile = os.environ["ATS_PROFILE"]
132: gen_at  = os.environ["ATS_GENERATED_AT"]
133: schema  = int(os.environ["ATS_SCHEMA"])
134: slugcat_path = os.environ["ATS_SLUGCAT"]
135: 
136: # Canonical slug -> category map.
137: slug_cat = {}
138: with open(slugcat_path) as f:
139:     for line in f:
140:         line = line.rstrip("\n")
141:         if not line:
142:             continue
143:         parts = line.split("\t")
144:         if len(parts) == 2:
145:             slug_cat[parts[0]] = parts[1]
146: 
147: def sha256(path):
148:     h = hashlib.sha256()
149:     with open(path, "rb") as fh:
150:         for chunk in iter(lambda: fh.read(65536), b""):
151:             h.update(chunk)
152:     return h.hexdigest()
153: 
154: # Per-tool source root subdir (matches resolve_dest expectations).
155: SRC_SUBDIR = {
156:     "opencode": "agents",
157:     "qwen": "agents",
158:     "gemini-cli": "skills",
159:     "cursor": "rules",
160: }
161: 
162: def dests_for(tool, relpath):
163:     """Return the list of destination paths for a source file. Most tools map
164:     to a single dest; copilot mirrors into BOTH ~/.github/agents/ and
165:     ~/.copilot/agents/ (install-locations.md:17 + install.sh install_copilot)."""
166:     if tool == "claude-code":
167:         return [os.path.join(root, ".claude", "skills", relpath)]
168:     if tool == "copilot":
169:         return [os.path.join(root, ".github", "agents", relpath),
170:                 os.path.join(root, ".copilot", "agents", relpath)]
171:     if tool == "antigravity":
172:         return [os.path.join(root, ".gemini", "antigravity", "skills", relpath)]
173:     if tool == "gemini-cli":
174:         return [os.path.join(root, ".gemini", "extensions", "alltheskills", relpath)]
175:     if tool == "opencode":
176:         return [os.path.join(root, ".opencode", "agents", relpath)]
177:     if tool == "cursor":
178:         return [os.path.join(root, ".cursor", "rules", relpath)]
179:     if tool == "openclaw":
180:         return [os.path.join(root, ".openclaw", "alltheskills", relpath)]
181:     if tool == "qwen":
182:         return [os.path.join(root, ".qwen", "agents", relpath)]
183:     if tool == "kimi":
184:         return [os.path.join(root, ".config", "kimi", "agents", relpath)]
185:     if tool == "aider":
186:         return [os.path.join(root, "CONVENTIONS.md")]
187:     if tool == "windsurf":
188:         return [os.path.join(root, ".windsurfrules")]
189:     return []
190: 
191: def slug_of(tool, src_root, abspath):
192:     """Derive the canonical skill slug for a source file, for category filtering."""
193:     rel = os.path.relpath(abspath, src_root)
194:     parts = rel.split(os.sep)
195:     if tool in ("claude-code", "gemini-cli"):
196:         # <category>/<slug>/... (claude-code) or <slug>/... (gemini-cli skills/)
197:         if tool == "claude-code":
198:             return parts[1] if len(parts) >= 2 else None
199:         return parts[0] if parts else None
200:     if tool == "antigravity" or tool == "openclaw" or tool == "kimi":
201:         # <slug>/...
202:         return parts[0] if parts else None
203:     if tool in ("copilot", "opencode", "qwen", "cursor"):
204:         # flat: <slug>.<ext> or <slug>-references/...
205:         base = parts[0]
206:         if base.endswith("-references"):
207:             return base[: -len("-references")]
208:         return os.path.splitext(base)[0]
209:     return None
210: 
211: operations = []
212: # Tools whose convert.sh output is absent. Warn loudly per tool (naming the
213: # path that was missing); if EVERY requested tool is missing, exit non-zero so
214: # a silent empty plan can never masquerade as success (SR23).
215: missing_sources = []
216: for tool in tools:
217:     tool_root = os.path.join(integ, tool)
218:     sub = SRC_SUBDIR.get(tool)
219:     src_root = os.path.join(tool_root, sub) if sub else tool_root
220:     if not os.path.isdir(src_root):
221:         # Single-file tools (aider/windsurf) live as files under tool_root.
222:         if tool == "aider":
223:             f = os.path.join(tool_root, "CONVENTIONS.md")
224:             src_root = tool_root
225:             if os.path.isfile(f):
226:                 files = [f]
227:             else:
228:                 sys.stderr.write(
229:                     "[plan] WARN missing source for tool '%s': %s "
230:                     "(run scripts/convert.sh --tool %s)\n" % (tool, f, tool))
231:                 missing_sources.append(tool)
232:                 continue
233:         elif tool == "windsurf":
234:             f = os.path.join(tool_root, ".windsurfrules")
235:             src_root = tool_root
236:             if os.path.isfile(f):
237:                 files = [f]
238:             else:
239:                 sys.stderr.write(
240:                     "[plan] WARN missing source for tool '%s': %s "
241:                     "(run scripts/convert.sh --tool %s)\n" % (tool, f, tool))
242:                 missing_sources.append(tool)
243:                 continue
244:         else:
245:             sys.stderr.write(
246:                 "[plan] WARN missing source for tool '%s': %s "
247:                 "(run scripts/convert.sh --tool %s)\n" % (tool, src_root, tool))
248:             missing_sources.append(tool)
249:             continue
250:     else:
251:         files = []
252:         for dirpath, _dirs, fnames in os.walk(src_root):
253:             for fn in fnames:
254:                 files.append(os.path.join(dirpath, fn))
255:     files.sort()
256: 
257:     for src in files:
258:         # Category filter (per-skill tools). aider/windsurf are whole-repo
259:         # consolidated files: include iff the profile selects any category that
260:         # exists on disk (i.e. non-empty profile) — they cannot be subset.
261:         if tool in ("aider", "windsurf"):
262:             if not cats:
263:                 continue
264:         else:
265:             slug = slug_of(tool, src_root, src)
266:             cat = slug_cat.get(slug)
267:             if cat is None or cat not in cats:
268:                 continue
269:         rel = os.path.relpath(src, src_root)
270:         dests = dests_for(tool, rel)
271:         if not dests:
272:             continue
273:         digest = sha256(src)
274:         for dest in dests:
275:             if os.path.isfile(dest):
276:                 action = "skip" if sha256(dest) == digest else "overwrite"
277:             else:
278:                 action = "create"
279:             operations.append({
280:                 "tool": tool,
281:                 "source": src,
282:                 "dest": dest,
283:                 "action": action,
284:                 "sha256": digest,
285:             })
286: 
287: # Every requested tool lacked a source → refuse to emit an empty plan (SR23).
288: if missing_sources and len(missing_sources) == len(tools):
289:     sys.stderr.write(
290:         "[plan] ERROR all requested tools have missing sources: %s\n"
291:         % " ".join(missing_sources))
292:     sys.exit(3)
293: 
294: operations.sort(key=lambda o: (o["tool"], o["dest"]))
295: plan = {
296:     "schema_version": schema,
297:     "generated_at": gen_at,
298:     "profile": profile,
299:     "tools": tools,
300:     "operations": operations,
301: }
302: print(json.dumps(plan, indent=2))
303: PYEOF
304:   )" || rc=$?
305:   rm -f "$slugcat_tmp"
306:   if [[ "$rc" -eq 3 ]]; then
307:     ats_err "no installable sources for any requested tool (see warnings above); run scripts/convert.sh"
308:     exit 2
309:   elif [[ "$rc" -ne 0 ]]; then
310:     ats_err "plan resolution failed"
311:     exit 2
312:   fi
313: 
314:   if [[ -n "$out" ]]; then
315:     mkdir -p "$(dirname "$out")"
316:     printf '%s\n' "$plan_json" > "$out"
317:     ats_ok "Wrote plan: $out"
318:   else
319:     printf '%s\n' "$plan_json"
320:   fi
321: }
322: 
323: main "$@"

=== scripts/install-apply.sh ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env bash
2: #
3: # install-apply.sh — Execute a plan produced by install-plan.sh + record state.
4: #
5: # Consumes a serializable plan, performs the file operations (respecting
6: # --dry-run), then writes install-state to <root>/.claude/.ats-install-state.json
7: # with the content hash of every installed file. Idempotent: re-applying an
8: # unchanged plan is a no-op.
9: #
10: # Usage:
11: #   scripts/install-apply.sh --plan FILE [--root DIR] [--dry-run]
12: #
13: # Options:
14: #   --plan FILE   Plan JSON from install-plan.sh. Required.
15: #   --root DIR    Destination base (default: $HOME). The plan's dests already
16: #                 encode the root; --root is used to locate the state file and
17: #                 must match the root the plan was resolved against.
18: #   --dry-run     Print actions; write nothing.
19: #   --help        Show this help and exit.
20: #
21: # install-state shape:
22: #   { schema_version, applied_at, profile, tools,
23: #     files:[ {dest, sha256, tool} ] }
24: #
25: # Exit codes: 0 success, 2 argument/plan error.
26: 
27: set -euo pipefail
28: 
29: SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
30: LIB_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/lib"
31: 
32: # shellcheck source=scripts/lib/term.sh
33: . "$LIB_DIR/term.sh"
34: # shellcheck source=scripts/lib/install-state.sh
35: . "$LIB_DIR/install-state.sh"
36: 
37: usage() {
38:   sed -n '3,26p' "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'
39:   exit 0
40: }
41: 
42: main() {
43:   local plan=""
44:   local root="${HOME:-/tmp}"
45:   local dry_run=0
46: 
47:   while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
48:     case "$1" in
49:       --plan)    plan="${2:?'--plan requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
50:       --root)    root="${2:?'--root requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
51:       --dry-run) dry_run=1; shift ;;
52:       --help|-h) usage ;;
53:       *)         ats_err "Unknown option: $1"; exit 2 ;;
54:     esac
55:   done
56: 
57:   if [[ -z "$plan" ]]; then ats_err "--plan is required"; exit 2; fi
58:   if [[ ! -f "$plan" ]]; then ats_err "plan not found: $plan"; exit 2; fi
59: 
60:   local state_file="$root/.claude/.ats-install-state.json"
61:   local applied_at
62:   applied_at="$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
63: 
64:   local rc=0
65:   ATS_PLAN="$plan" \
66:   ATS_ROOT="$root" \
67:   ATS_STATE="$state_file" \
68:   ATS_APPLIED_AT="$applied_at" \
69:   ATS_DRY_RUN="$dry_run" \
70:   python3 - <<'PYEOF' || rc=$?
71: import hashlib, json, os, shutil, sys
72: 
73: plan_path  = os.environ["ATS_PLAN"]
74: state_path = os.environ["ATS_STATE"]
75: applied_at = os.environ["ATS_APPLIED_AT"]
76: dry_run    = os.environ["ATS_DRY_RUN"] == "1"
77: 
78: with open(plan_path) as f:
79:     plan = json.load(f)
80: 
81: def sha256(path):
82:     h = hashlib.sha256()
83:     with open(path, "rb") as fh:
84:         for chunk in iter(lambda: fh.read(65536), b""):
85:             h.update(chunk)
86:     return h.hexdigest()
87: 
88: ops = plan.get("operations", [])
89: created = updated = skipped = 0
90: files = []
91: 
92: for op in ops:
93:     src = op["source"]
94:     dest = op["dest"]
95:     tool = op["tool"]
96:     want = op["sha256"]
97: 
98:     if not os.path.isfile(src):
99:         sys.stderr.write("[apply] WARN missing source: %s\n" % src)
100:         continue
101: 
102:     # Recompute the on-disk action at apply time (the plan may be stale).
103:     if os.path.isfile(dest):
104:         action = "skip" if sha256(dest) == want else "overwrite"
105:     else:
106:         action = "create"
107: 
108:     if action == "skip":
109:         skipped += 1
110:     else:
111:         if not dry_run:
112:             os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dest), exist_ok=True)
113:             # Replace symlinks with regular files (do not follow).
114:             if os.path.islink(dest):
115:                 os.unlink(dest)
116:             shutil.copyfile(src, dest)
117:         if action == "create":
118:             created += 1
119:         else:
120:             updated += 1
121: 
122:     files.append({"dest": dest, "sha256": want, "tool": tool})
123: 
124: state = {
125:     "schema_version": plan.get("schema_version", 1),
126:     "applied_at": applied_at,
127:     "profile": plan.get("profile"),
128:     "tools": plan.get("tools", []),
129:     "files": sorted(files, key=lambda x: x["dest"]),
130: }
131: 
132: if not dry_run:
133:     os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(state_path), exist_ok=True)
134:     with open(state_path, "w") as f:
135:         json.dump(state, f, indent=2)
136:         f.write("\n")
137: 
138: sys.stderr.write("[apply] created=%d updated=%d skipped=%d dry_run=%s\n"
139:                  % (created, updated, skipped, dry_run))
140: print("created=%d updated=%d skipped=%d" % (created, updated, skipped))
141: PYEOF
142: 
143:   if [[ "$rc" -ne 0 ]]; then ats_err "apply failed"; exit 2; fi
144:   if [[ "$dry_run" -eq 1 ]]; then
145:     ats_info "dry-run: no files written, no state recorded"
146:   else
147:     # Stash a copy of the plan beside the state so `install-state.sh repair`
148:     # can re-resolve source paths without re-running install-plan.
149:     mkdir -p "$(dirname "$state_file")"
150:     cp "$plan" "$(dirname "$state_file")/.ats-install-plan.json"
151:     ats_ok "Applied plan; state: $state_file"
152:   fi
153: }
154: 
155: main "$@"

=== spec/PSFS.md ===
1: # Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec (PSFS)
2: 
3: **Version:** 1.1.0
4: **Status:** Published
5: 
6: The Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec (PSFS) is a named, versioned standard for the YAML
7: frontmatter that a skill declares in its `SKILL.md`. It defines a small, tool-agnostic
8: set of fields and two conformance tiers — **Core** and **Extended** — so that skill
9: collections written by different teams, in different languages and toolchains, can
10: validate against the *same* contract without adopting any one repository's tooling. PSFS
11: Core is a compatibility-preserving profile of Anthropic's open Agent Skills frontmatter:
12: a Core-conformant skill uploads to Claude.ai and runs on Claude Code and the Agent SDK
13: unchanged. The optional Extended fields add multi-agent coordination metadata that
14: non-understanding parsers ignore safely.
15: 
16: > **Motivation (non-normative).** Portability and convergence matter because skills are
17: > increasingly shared across ecosystems. A single normative spec plus a portable
18: > machine-readable validator lets any collection claim conformance and interoperate,
19: > rather than each vendor inventing a divergent frontmatter dialect.
20: 
21: ## Conformance Tiers
22: 
23: The key words MUST, MUST NOT, REQUIRED, SHALL, SHALL NOT, SHOULD, SHOULD NOT,
24: RECOMMENDED, MAY, and OPTIONAL in this document are to be interpreted as described in
25: RFC 2119.
26: 
27: ### Core
28: 
29: A skill claiming **Core** conformance:
30: 
31: - MUST declare a `name` that is kebab-case, at most 64 characters, and matches its
32:   containing folder name.
33: - MUST declare a `version` that is valid semantic versioning (`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`).
34: - MUST declare a `description` that is present, at most 1024 characters, and contains no
35:   `<` or `>` characters.
36: - MUST NOT include `<` or `>` characters anywhere in any frontmatter string value (see
37:   the angle-bracket rule under Field Catalog for the rationale).
38: - MAY include any of the Anthropic Agent Skills optional fields (`compatibility`,
39:   `license`, `allowed-tools`, `metadata`, `argument-hint`, `disable-model-invocation`),
40:   and where present those fields MUST be used with the correct type and semantics defined
41:   in the field catalog below.
42: - SHOULD keep `description` at or under a 200-character target so triggers stay tight and
43:   scannable; the 1024-character ceiling is the hard limit.
44: - SHOULD NOT use a `name` beginning with `claude-` or `anthropic-`; these prefixes are
45:   reserved by Anthropic for first-party skills.
46: - **No Extended field is REQUIRED.** Core is the tier that vendor-neutral collections —
47:   including ECC (Everything Claude Code) — can claim.
48: 
49: > **Reserved-prefix exception (non-normative).** A skill that precisely targets a
50: > corresponding Anthropic product or feature (for example, a skill named
51: > `claude-design-brief` for Claude's design canvas) may reasonably use a reserved prefix.
52: > This is author judgement, not a validator-checkable constraint; reference
53: > implementations treat a reserved-prefix `name` as a warning, not a failure.
54: 
55: ### Extended
56: 
57: A skill claiming **Extended** conformance:
58: 
59: - MUST satisfy every Core requirement.
60: - MUST use this standard's multi-agent extension fields (`requires_agent_teams`,
61:   `requires_claude_code`, `min_plan`, `owns`, `composes_with`, `spawned_by`) correctly
62:   where present, with the types and semantics defined in the field catalog below.
63: - MUST claim Extended conformance if it participates in orchestrated, multi-agent builds,
64:   because ownership and composition metadata is required for zero-conflict parallel work.
65: - MAY omit any individual Extended field that does not apply; Extended conformance
66:   constrains the *correctness* of these fields, not their presence.
67: 
68: ### Parser requirements
69: 
70: - A PSFS parser operating at Core tier MUST accept and ignore frontmatter keys it does
71:   not recognize. This is what lets an Extended skill load unchanged under a Core-only
72:   parser, and what lets future PSFS versions add fields without breaking older parsers.
73: - When both `allowed-tools` (canonical) and `allowed_tools` (deprecated alias) are
74:   present, a parser MUST treat `allowed-tools` as authoritative and SHOULD emit a warning
75:   about the redundant alias.
76: 
77: ## Field Catalog
78: 
79: This catalog is normative. Field names, types, and constraints are part of PSFS v1.1.0.
80: 
81: **Angle-bracket rule.** Any string-typed field MUST NOT contain `<` or `>`. Frontmatter
82: is injected verbatim into the model's system prompt, where `<...>` sequences can be
83: interpreted as control or tool tags, or abused as a prompt-injection vector. The ban is
84: on the literal characters and is intentionally strict: it also rejects otherwise-benign
85: uses such as `n <= 5` or `a > b`. Authors should phrase these as `at most` / `greater
86: than`, or use the Unicode forms `≤` / `≥`. Reliably distinguishing a benign angle bracket
87: from an injection vector inside a system-prompt context is not worth the risk, so PSFS
88: forbids the literal characters outright rather than attempting to allow "safe" ones.
89: 
90: This constraint applies to every string value **and key**, at any depth — including
91: those nested inside the free-form `metadata` object. Enforcement is split: the JSON
92: Schema constrains the named typed fields, and the bash reference validator walks the
93: entire parsed frontmatter to cover arbitrary leaves (such as `metadata` values), a
94: constraint JSON Schema cannot express over a free-form object. The check runs against
95: parsed YAML *values*, so block-scalar indicators like `description: >` or
96: `description: |` are structural and never trip the rule.
97: 
98: ### Core Fields
99: 
100: | Field | Required | Type | Constraints | Semantics |
101: |-------|----------|------|-------------|-----------|
102: | `name` | MUST | string | kebab-case `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`, ≤64 chars, matches folder name, unique within the collection | Stable identifier for the skill. |
103: | `version` | MUST | string | semver `^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$` | Top-level semantic version. See Skill Versioning below. |
104: | `description` | MUST | string | present, ≤1024 chars, no `<`/`>`; ≤200 chars RECOMMENDED | Primary trigger text. Claude reads this to decide whether to invoke the skill: `[what it does] + [when to use] + [key capabilities/keyword variants]`. |
105: | `compatibility` | MAY | string | 1–500 chars, no `<`/`>` | Human-readable declaration of host, required packages, network, and MCP servers. For programmatic gating use the `requires_*` booleans. |
106: | `license` | MAY | string | no `<`/`>` | Per-skill SPDX license override (e.g. `MIT`, `Apache-2.0`). Usually unnecessary; the repo-level license applies. |
107: | `allowed-tools` | MAY | string[] | each item no `<`/`>` | **Canonical.** Whitelist of tools the skill may invoke. |
108: | `allowed_tools` | MAY | string[] | each item no `<`/`>` | **Deprecated alias** of `allowed-tools` (underscore form). Accepted for back-compat; new skills SHOULD use `allowed-tools`. If both appear, `allowed-tools` wins (see Parser requirements). |
109: | `argument-hint` | MAY | string | short, no `<`/`>` | Hint shown in Claude Code's slash-command UI for skills taking a positional argument. |
110: | `disable-model-invocation` | MAY | boolean | default `false` | When `true`, the skill is invocable only by an orchestrator or explicit slash-command, not by Claude's model-driven auto-trigger. Recognized by the Claude Code runtime. |
111: | `metadata` | MAY | object | free-form key/value | Attribution and cataloging. See the metadata note below. |
112: 
113: **metadata note.** `metadata` is an OPTIONAL free-form object. The keys `author`,
114: `category`, `tags`, `mcp-server`, `documentation`, and `support` are RECOMMENDED
115: conventional keys with the obvious meanings; additional keys MAY appear. PSFS does not
116: constrain `metadata` values beyond the angle-bracket rule on any string within them. This
117: mirrors Anthropic's nested-metadata form.
118: 
119: ### Extended Fields
120: 
121: These fields are this standard's multi-agent extensions. They are not part of Anthropic's
122: Agent Skills spec; parsers that do not understand them MUST be able to ignore them safely
123: (see Parser requirements). They are evaluated for correctness only under Extended
124: conformance.
125: 
126: | Field | Type | Constraints | Semantics |
127: |-------|------|-------------|-----------|
128: | `requires_agent_teams` | boolean | default `false` | `true` if the skill requires `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1`. Such skills SHOULD degrade gracefully when teams are unavailable. |
129: | `requires_claude_code` | boolean | default `false` | `true` if the skill requires the Claude Code CLI (bash, filesystem access). |
130: | `min_plan` | enum | one of `starter`, `pro`, `team`, `enterprise`; default `starter` | Minimum plan tier required to run the skill. |
131: | `owns` | object | OPTIONAL object; see owns note below | Ownership declaration for agent role skills. |
132: | `composes_with` | string[] | each item no `<`/`>` | Other skill names this one naturally works with (informational). Plugin-external refs use a `plugin:name` prefix. |
133: | `spawned_by` | string[] | each item no `<`/`>` | Which skills spawn this one. Plugin-external refs use a `plugin:name` prefix. |
134: 
135: **owns note.** `owns` is an OPTIONAL object with three OPTIONAL properties, each an array
136: of strings (no other properties are permitted):
137: 
138: - `owns.directories` — directories this agent skill owns exclusively.
139: - `owns.patterns` — file glob patterns this agent owns.
140: - `owns.shared_read` — directories this agent reads but does not own.
141: 
142: An `owns` object MAY be empty (`owns: {}`) and any single property MAY be omitted; for
143: example, a skill MAY declare `owns.patterns` without `owns.directories`. The non-overlap
144: of `owns.directories` between agent skills is a cross-file rule (see Validator-only
145: checks), not a per-file one.
146: 
147: **Ownership resolution.** When ownership of a path is contested, resolve in this order:
148: (1) directory ownership takes precedence over pattern ownership — a file matching agent
149: A's glob but living under agent B's owned directory belongs to B; (2) a more specific
150: subdirectory carve-out overrides a parent directory — an agent owning `tests/performance/`
151: keeps it even if another owns `tests/`; (3) any conflict unresolved by (1)–(2) is
152: **undefined behavior at the PSFS layer** — a runtime that consumes PSFS metadata SHOULD
153: provide a deterministic resolution mechanism and MUST surface any conflict it cannot
154: resolve. (An orchestrated multi-agent build, for example, resolves at spawn time and
155: escalates an unresolvable conflict to a human; a static analyzer might simply report it.)
156: 
157: **Reference resolution.** A `composes_with` or `spawned_by` entry that names an in-collection
158: skill SHOULD resolve to a skill that exists in the collection; an unresolved in-collection
159: reference is a warning, not an error (these fields are informational and a dangling
160: reference does not make the skill malformed). A plugin-namespaced reference — any value
161: containing a `:` (e.g. `superpowers:brainstorming`), matching the prevailing Anthropic
162: plugin `plugin:name` convention — is external by definition: it is out of scope for
163: in-collection resolution and MUST NOT be flagged as unresolved.
164: 
165: ## Skill Versioning
166: 
167: `version` is the skill's own semantic version (distinct from the PSFS spec version; see
168: Spec Versioning and Stability). Start at `1.0.0`. For a skill, treat as **breaking**
169: (MAJOR) any change to behavior a caller or a composing skill could depend on: renaming the
170: skill (its `name`/folder), removing a tool from `allowed-tools`, narrowing `owns`,
171: changing `spawned_by`/`composes_with` relationships, or altering `description` such that
172: the skill's trigger semantics shift materially. *Broadening* `owns` is also breaking at
173: the collection level — it can claim territory another skill relied on or create an
174: ownership overlap — even though it is additive from the broadening skill's own
175: perspective. Bump MINOR for additive capability and
176: PATCH for fixes that change nothing a caller relies on. When in doubt, defer to
177: [semver.org](https://semver.org); "breaking" means "a dependent could observe the
178: difference."
179: 
180: ## Reference Validators
181: 
182: PSFS v1.1.0 has two reference validators:
183: 
184: 1. **`spec/frontmatter.schema.json`** — the canonical, portable machine-readable
185:    validator. It is a JSON Schema 2020-12 document, runnable in any language with a
186:    conforming JSON Schema implementation, and is the authoritative encoding of per-file
187:    structural conformance.
188: 2. **`scripts/lint-skills.sh --standard`** — the bash reference implementation. It binds
189:    the linter to the named standard (reporting `Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec (PSFS)
190:    v1.1.0` and the canonical schema path) and, where `python3` and the `jsonschema`
191:    package are available, cross-checks each parsed frontmatter object against the
192:    canonical schema. It additionally performs the cross-file checks below, which a per-file
193:    schema cannot express.
194: 
195: The two validators are kept in agreement by a regression test (`tests/standard/`) that
196: asserts every skill in the collection validates identically under both, run in CI by the
197: `Frontmatter Standard / PSFS` job.
198: 
199: ### Schema-expressible checks (per-file structure)
200: 
201: The JSON Schema expresses everything verifiable by inspecting a single frontmatter object
202: in isolation:
203: 
204: - Required fields present: `name`, `version`, `description`.
205: - `name` is kebab-case (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`) and at most 64 characters.
206: - `version` matches the semver pattern `^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$`.
207: - `description` is at most 1024 characters and contains no `<`/`>`.
208: - No `<`/`>` in any constrained string field.
209: - Correct enum values (e.g. `min_plan` is one of `starter`, `pro`, `team`, `enterprise`).
210: - Correct types for every documented optional field, including the `owns` sub-object shape.
211: 
212: ### Validator-only / cross-file checks
213: 
214: These require knowledge beyond a single file and are out of scope for JSON Schema. They
215: live in `scripts/lint-skills.sh`:
216: 
217: - **Name uniqueness within the collection** — each `name` appears in exactly one skill in
218:   the validated collection. (Global uniqueness across all PSFS collections is not claimed:
219:   it would require a registry PSFS does not define.)
220: - **`name` equals directory name** — the `name` field MUST match the skill's folder name
221:   on disk.
222: - **`owns.directories` non-overlap between agents** — no two agent skills declare ownership
223:   of the same directory, resolved by the Ownership resolution rules above.
224: - **In-collection reference resolution** — each in-collection `composes_with` / `spawned_by`
225:   name resolves to an existing skill (WARN if not); plugin-namespaced refs (containing `:`)
226:   are external and not checked. See Reference resolution above.
227: 
228: ## Conformance
229: 
230: A collection declares its conformance level explicitly:
231: 
232: - A collection claims **Core conformance** when every skill it ships satisfies all Core
233:   requirements above. Vendor-neutral collections that do not use the multi-agent
234:   extensions — such as ECC — claim Core. Core conformance is verified end-to-end by
235:   running `spec/frontmatter.schema.json` against each skill's frontmatter (per-file
236:   structure) plus the cross-file checks listed under Reference Validators.
237: - A collection claims **Extended conformance** when, in addition to Core, every skill that
238:   uses an Extended field uses it correctly per the field catalog, and the cross-file
239:   `owns.directories` non-overlap rule holds across all agent skills.
240: 
241: A skill or collection states the tier and the standard version it targets — for example,
242: `Conforms to PSFS v1.1.0 (Core)` — and validates that claim with the reference validators
243: named above. A collection SHOULD declare this claim in its root README. A machine-readable
244: conformance manifest (for example a `psfs.json` file a registry could consume) is
245: intentionally deferred: its shape is reserved for the version that introduces a consuming
246: registry, rather than being specified speculatively here.
247: 
248: ## Relationship to Anthropic's Agent Skills Spec
249: 
250: PSFS Core is a compatibility-preserving profile of Anthropic's open Agent Skills
251: frontmatter standard:
252: 
253: - A Core-conformant skill uses only `name`, `version`, `description`, and the Anthropic
254:   Agent Skills optional fields (`compatibility`, `license`, `allowed-tools`, `metadata`,
255:   `argument-hint`, `disable-model-invocation`). Such a skill uploads to Claude.ai and runs
256:   on Claude Code and the Agent SDK without modification.
257: - The Extended fields (`requires_agent_teams`, `requires_claude_code`, `min_plan`, `owns`,
258:   `composes_with`, `spawned_by`) are additive metadata. Spec-compliant parsers ignore
259:   unrecognized frontmatter keys, so a skill carrying Extended fields remains loadable
260:   everywhere Core is loadable.
261: 
262: In short: Core does not diverge from Anthropic's spec, and Extended never breaks
263: compatibility with it.
264: 
265: ## Examples
266: 
267: A minimal **Core** skill (no Extended fields):
268: 
269: ```yaml
270: ---
271: name: simplify
272: version: 1.2.0
273: description: |
274:   Refactor code for readability without changing behavior. Use when the user asks to
275:   simplify, clean up, or reduce complexity in a function or module.
276: allowed-tools: ["Read", "Edit"]
277: metadata:
278:   author: jane-doe
279:   category: workflows
280:   tags: [refactoring, readability]
281:   documentation: https://example.com/skills/simplify
282: ---
283: ```
284: 
285: An **Extended** skill (an orchestrated agent that declares ownership):
286: 
287: ```yaml
288: ---
289: name: backend-agent
290: version: 2.0.1
291: description: |
292:   Build and own backend services in an orchestrated multi-agent build. Use when the
293:   orchestrator dispatches API, service, or data-layer work.
294: allowed-tools: ["Read", "Write", "Edit", "Bash"]
295: requires_claude_code: true
296: min_plan: starter
297: owns:
298:   directories: ["src/api/", "src/services/"]
299:   patterns: ["Dockerfile*"]
300:   shared_read: ["contracts/"]
301: composes_with: ["contract-author"]
302: spawned_by: ["orchestrator"]
303: ---
304: ```
305: 
306: ## Spec Versioning and Stability
307: 
308: This versions the **standard itself**, not the skills that conform to it.
309: 
310: - **Canonical identifier.** The standard is `spec/PSFS.md` in the Skill-Madness
311:   repository; its machine-readable form is `spec/frontmatter.schema.json`, whose `$id`
312:   carries the same version. The document `Version` header and the schema `title`/`$id` are
313:   bumped in lockstep.
314: - **Versioning policy.** PSFS uses semantic versioning. A MAJOR bump removes or
315:   incompatibly redefines a field or constraint; a MINOR bump adds an optional field or
316:   relaxes a constraint in a backward-compatible way (existing conformant skills stay
317:   conformant); a PATCH bump is editorial. Because Core parsers MUST ignore unrecognized
318:   keys, additive MINOR releases do not break older parsers.
319: - **Deprecation.** A field is marked deprecated at least one MINOR release before any
320:   MAJOR release removes it (as `allowed_tools` is deprecated in favor of `allowed-tools`).
321:   Deprecated fields continue to validate until the removing MAJOR release.
322: 
323: ## Changelog
324: 
325: This section tracks every released version of the standard.
326: 
327: - 1.1.0 — Editorial and normative clarifications from spec review; backward-compatible
328:   (no field added or removed, no constraint changed, every previously-conformant skill
329:   stays conformant). Folded across two review passes:
330:   - *Initial 1.1.0 changes.* Added a **Parser Requirements** section (Core parsers MUST
331:     accept and ignore unrecognized keys; `allowed-tools` is authoritative when the
332:     deprecated `allowed_tools` alias is also present); documented the angle-bracket threat
333:     model and its intentional strictness; added **Skill Versioning** and **Spec Versioning
334:     and Stability** sections; defined the `owns` object shape and the ownership-resolution
335:     order; added Core and Extended worked examples; scoped `name` uniqueness to the
336:     collection (not a global "ecosystem"); moved the rationale into a non-normative
337:     Motivation note.
338:   - *Second review pass.* Clarified that the angle-bracket rule applies to every string
339:     value and key at any depth (including `metadata`) and documented the split enforcement
340:     (schema for typed fields, bash validator walks the whole frontmatter) — the reference
341:     validator was updated to enforce this. Made ownership-resolution rule (3) tool-agnostic
342:     (undefined at the PSFS layer; runtimes SHOULD resolve deterministically and MUST surface
343:     unresolvable conflicts). Added a **Reference resolution** rule for
344:     `composes_with`/`spawned_by` (in-collection refs WARN if unresolved, plugin-namespaced
345:     refs are external and not flagged) — the validator was updated to stop flagging
346:     `plugin:` refs. Noted that *broadening* `owns` is breaking at the collection level.
347:     Recommended declaring the conformance claim in the collection README (machine-readable
348:     manifest deferred).
349: - 1.0.0 — initial publication.

=== spec/frontmatter.schema.json ===
1: {
2:   "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
3:   "$id": "https://github.com/the-hive-ecosystem/Skill-Madness/spec/frontmatter.schema.json",
4:   "title": "Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec (PSFS) v1.1.0",
5:   "description": "Canonical, portable, tool-agnostic JSON Schema for SKILL.md frontmatter, conforming to the Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec (PSFS) v1.1.0 (see spec/PSFS.md). This schema expresses PER-FILE STRUCTURE ONLY: required fields, types, patterns, and enumerations for a single SKILL.md frontmatter object. CROSS-FILE checks are explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for JSON Schema and remain in scripts/lint-skills.sh: name uniqueness within the collection, name == directory name, and owns.directories non-overlap between role skills. Core fields (name, version, description plus Anthropic optionals) define Core conformance; the multi-agent extension fields define Extended conformance.",
6:   "type": "object",
7:   "required": ["name", "version", "description"],
8:   "properties": {
9:     "name": {
10:       "type": "string",
11:       "description": "Skill identifier. Kebab-case, must match the skill folder name (folder match enforced by lint-skills.sh, not this schema).",
12:       "pattern": "^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$",
13:       "maxLength": 64
14:     },
15:     "version": {
16:       "type": "string",
17:       "description": "Semantic version MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.",
18:       "pattern": "^\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+$"
19:     },
20:     "description": {
21:       "type": "string",
22:       "description": "Primary trigger text. No XML angle brackets ('<' or '>') — frontmatter loads into Claude's system prompt.",
23:       "maxLength": 1024,
24:       "pattern": "^[^<>]*$"
25:     },
26: 
27:     "compatibility": {
28:       "type": "string",

30:       "minLength": 1,

32:       "pattern": "^[^<>]*$"
33:     },

45:       }

47:     "allowed_tools": {

58:       "pattern": "^[^<>]*$"
59:     },

63:     },

=== scripts/lint-skills.sh ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env bash
2: #
3: # lint-skills.sh — Validate all skills/**/SKILL.md files.
4: #
5: # Checks frontmatter schema, body quality, and cross-skill invariants.
6: # This is the CI gate — errors block PR merges.
7: #
8: # Usage:
9: #   scripts/lint-skills.sh [OPTIONS] [PATH ...]
10: #
11: # Options:
12: #   --quiet          Suppress WARN output
13: #   --verbose        Show INFO output
14: #   --fix-trivial    Auto-fix trivial issues (prompts before each fix)
15: #   --format FORMAT  Output format: text (default) or junit
16: #   --standard       Print the PSFS standard + version this linter implements
17: #                    and the canonical schema path, then exit 0
18: #   --changed [REF]  Version-drift guard (git-based): ERROR when a skill's
19: #                    SKILL.md body changed vs REF (default origin/main, then
20: #                    main) but its frontmatter version: did not. Ignores PATH
21: #                    args. Not part of the static lint; wired into CI as a PR
22: #                    gate in .github/workflows/lint-skills.yml (DV-2).
23: #   --help           Print this and exit
24: #
25: # PATH may be a directory (recurse) or an individual SKILL.md.
26: # If no PATH given, lints all skills/**/SKILL.md.
27: #
28: # Exit codes: 0 no errors, 1 at least one error, 2 argument failure
29: 
30: set -euo pipefail
31: 
32: SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
33: LIB_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/lib"
34: 
35: # shellcheck source=scripts/lib/term.sh
36: . "$LIB_DIR/term.sh"
37: # shellcheck source=scripts/lib/frontmatter.sh
38: . "$LIB_DIR/frontmatter.sh"
39: 
40: REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
41: SKILLS_ROOT="$REPO_ROOT/skills"
42: 
43: # Named standard this linter implements (see spec/PSFS.md).
44: PSFS_NAME="Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec (PSFS)"
45: PSFS_VERSION="1.1.0"
46: SCHEMA_PATH="$REPO_ROOT/spec/frontmatter.schema.json"
47: 
48: # print_standard — bind this bash impl to the named, versioned standard.
49: print_standard() {
50:   printf '%s v%s\n' "$PSFS_NAME" "$PSFS_VERSION"
51:   printf 'Canonical schema: %s\n' "$SCHEMA_PATH"
52:   printf 'Reference implementation: scripts/lint-skills.sh (bash)\n'
53: }
54: 
55: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
56: # CLI state
57: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
58: QUIET=false
59: VERBOSE=false
60: # shellcheck disable=SC2034  # FIX_TRIVIAL: reserved for future auto-fix implementation
61: FIX_TRIVIAL=false
62: FORMAT="text"
63: LINT_PATHS=()
64: # --changed mode (SR13 version-drift guard): opt-in, git-based. CHANGED_BASE=""
65: # means "auto-detect base ref" (origin/main, then main).
66: CHANGED_MODE=false
67: CHANGED_BASE=""
68: 
69: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
70: # Issue tracking
71: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
72: ERRORS=()    # "FILE:LINE:MESSAGE"
73: WARNS=()
74: INFOS=()
75: 
76: # Emit the "jsonschema absent" advisory at most once across the whole run.
77: JSONSCHEMA_ADVISED=false
78: 
79: record_error() { ERRORS+=("$1"); }
80: record_warn()  { WARNS+=("$1"); }
81: record_info()  { INFOS+=("$1"); }
82: 
83: # emit_issue <severity> <file> [line] <message>
84: emit_issue() {
85:   local sev="$1" file="$2" line_or_msg="$3"
86:   local msg="${4:-}"
87:   local line=""
88: 
89:   if [[ -n "$msg" ]]; then
90:     line="$line_or_msg"
91:   else
92:     msg="$line_or_msg"
93:   fi
94: 
95:   local loc="$file"
96:   [[ -n "$line" ]] && loc="${file}:${line}"
97: 
98:   case "$sev" in
99:     ERROR) record_error "${loc}:${msg}" ;;
100:     WARN)  record_warn  "${loc}:${msg}" ;;
101:     INFO)  record_info  "${loc}:${msg}" ;;
102:   esac
103: }
104: 
105: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
106: # YAML-aware python3 parser for lint checks
107: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
108: _lint_py3() {
109:   local file="$1"
110:   local schema="${2:-}"
111:   python3 - "$file" "$schema" <<'PYEOF'
112: import sys, json, re
113: 
114: path = sys.argv[1]
115: schema_path = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else ''
116: 
117: with open(path) as f:
118:     content = f.read()
119: 
120: lines = content.split('\n')
121: result = {
122:     "ok": False,
123:     "error": None,
124:     "fm_end_line": None,
125:     "data": {},
126:     "body_lines": [],
127:     "body_word_count": 0,
128:     "schema_errors": [],
129:     "jsonschema_missing": False,
130: }
131: 
132: # Check opening ---
133: if not lines or lines[0].rstrip() != '---':
134:     result["error"] = "missing opening ---"
135:     print(json.dumps(result))
136:     sys.exit(0)
137: 
138: fm_end = None
139: for i in range(1, min(101, len(lines))):
140:     if lines[i].rstrip() == '---':
141:         fm_end = i
142:         break
143: 
144: if fm_end is None:
145:     result["error"] = "no closing --- within 100 lines"
146:     print(json.dumps(result))
147:     sys.exit(0)
148: 
149: result["fm_end_line"] = fm_end + 1  # 1-based
150: 
151: fm_text = '\n'.join(lines[1:fm_end])
152: 
153: try:
154:     import yaml
155:     try:
156:         data = yaml.safe_load(fm_text) or {}
157:     except yaml.YAMLError as e:
158:         result["error"] = f"YAML parse error: {e}"
159:         print(json.dumps(result))
160:         sys.exit(0)
161: except ImportError:
162:     result["error"] = "install python3 pyyaml for full YAML validation"
163:     print(json.dumps(result))
164:     sys.exit(0)
165: 
166: result["ok"] = True
167: result["data"] = data
168: 
169: # Angle-bracket safety: '<' and '>' are forbidden in ANY frontmatter string
170: # value or key, at any depth — including inside the free-form `metadata` object.
171: # Frontmatter is injected verbatim into the model's system prompt, where '<...>'
172: # can be read as control/tool tags or abused for prompt injection. Checked
173: # against parsed VALUES (not raw text), so YAML block-scalar indicators such as
174: # 'description: >' or 'description: |' are never false positives.
175: def _angle_hits(obj, path=""):
176:     hits = []
177:     if isinstance(obj, str):
178:         if "<" in obj or ">" in obj:
179:             hits.append(path or "(root)")
180:     elif isinstance(obj, dict):
181:         for k, v in obj.items():
182:             ks = str(k)
183:             kp = f"{path}.{ks}" if path else ks
184:             if "<" in ks or ">" in ks:
185:                 hits.append(f"{kp} (key)")
186:             hits += _angle_hits(v, kp)
187:     elif isinstance(obj, list):
188:         for i, v in enumerate(obj):
189:             hits += _angle_hits(v, f"{path}[{i}]")
190:     return hits
191: 
192: result["angle_hits"] = _angle_hits(data)
193: 
194: # Body analysis
195: body_lines = lines[fm_end + 1:]
196: result["body_lines"] = body_lines
197: result["body_word_count"] = len(' '.join(body_lines).split())
198: result["body_line_count"] = len([l for l in body_lines if l.strip()])
199: 
200: # Description char count (collapsed)
201: desc = data.get('description', '')
202: if isinstance(desc, str):
203:     result["desc_len"] = len(' '.join(desc.split()))
204: else:
205:     result["desc_len"] = 0
206: 
207: # Check if description starts with action verb (first 5 words)
208: action_verbs = {
209:     'use', 'apply', 'generate', 'validate', 'coordinate', 'author', 'review',
210:     'audit', 'create', 'build', 'analyze', 'check', 'extract', 'prepare',
211:     'design', 'manage', 'track', 'convert', 'install', 'lint', 'fix',
212:     'refactor', 'test', 'run', 'write', 'read', 'update', 'set', 'add',
213:     'remove', 'deploy', 'debug', 'implement', 'scaffold', 'plan', 'parse',
214:     'emit', 'walk', 'scan', 'detect', 'verify', 'enforce', 'produce',
215:     'output', 'sync', 'migrate', 'query', 'clean', 'prune', 'stage',
216:     'commit', 'push', 'pull', 'merge', 'rebase', 'branch', 'tag',
217:     'profile', 'monitor', 'alert', 'log', 'trace', 'instrument', 'bootstrap',
218:     'configure', 'format', 'render', 'compile', 'bundle', 'optimize',
219:     'benchmark', 'measure', 'report', 'summarize', 'document', 'draft',
220:     'lead', 'coordinate', 'orchestrate', 'spawn', 'dispatch', 'gate',
221:     'consolidate', 'research', 'improve', 'deep', 'guide', 'chart',
222: }
223: first_words = ' '.join(desc.split()).split()[:5]
224: first_words_lower = [w.lower().strip('.,;:!?') for w in first_words]
225: result["has_action_verb"] = any(w in action_verbs for w in first_words_lower)
226: 
227: # Check trigger context hint (when/for/whenever/if)
228: result["has_trigger_context"] = bool(re.search(r'\b(when|for|whenever|if)\b', desc, re.IGNORECASE))
229: 
230: # Optional schema cross-check (PSFS frontmatter.schema.json).
231: # Only attempts when a schema path was passed AND jsonschema is importable.
232: # Missing jsonschema is an environment advisory (WARN), mirroring pyyaml.
233: if schema_path:
234:     try:
235:         import jsonschema
236:         try:
237:             with open(schema_path) as sf:
238:                 schema = json.load(sf)
239:             validator = jsonschema.Draft202012Validator(schema)
240:             for err in sorted(validator.iter_errors(data), key=str):
241:                 loc = '/'.join(str(p) for p in err.absolute_path) or '(root)'
242:                 result["schema_errors"].append(f"{loc}: {err.message}")
243:         except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — never block lint on schema-read issues
244:             result["schema_errors"].append(f"schema validation error: {e}")
245:     except ImportError:
246:         result["jsonschema_missing"] = True
247: 
248: print(json.dumps(result))
249: PYEOF
250: }
251: 
252: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
253: # Collect SKILL.md files to lint
254: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
255: 
256: # _drop_gitignored — filter stdin, dropping paths git ignores. Gitignored
257: # SKILL.md files (skill-creator / skill-review eval workspaces, `*-workspace/`
258: # debris) are not part of the tree, but the discovery find used to pick them
259: # up and fail the lint (+ the bats regression test) after every local
260: # skill-creator run. Outside a git repo this is a pass-through. Explicitly
261: # passed files are NOT filtered — asking for a file means lint that file.
262: _drop_gitignored() {
263:   local all ignored
264:   all="$(cat)"
265:   [[ -z "$all" ]] && return 0
266:   if git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree >/dev/null 2>&1; then
267:     ignored="$(printf '%s\n' "$all" | git -C "$REPO_ROOT" check-ignore --stdin 2>/dev/null || true)"
268:     if [[ -n "$ignored" ]]; then
269:       printf '%s\n' "$all" | grep -vxF -f <(printf '%s\n' "$ignored") || true
270:       return 0
271:     fi
272:   fi
273:   printf '%s\n' "$all"
274: }
275: 
276: collect_files() {
277:   if [[ ${#LINT_PATHS[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
278:     find "$SKILLS_ROOT" -name "SKILL.md" -type f | sort | _drop_gitignored
279:     return
280:   fi
281:   local p
282:   for p in "${LINT_PATHS[@]}"; do
283:     if [[ -f "$p" ]]; then
284:       printf '%s\n' "$p"
285:     elif [[ -d "$p" ]]; then
286:       find "$p" -name "SKILL.md" -type f | sort | _drop_gitignored
287:     else
288:       printf '%s: not a file or directory\n' "$p" >&2
289:     fi
290:   done | sort -u
291: }
292: 
293: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
294: # lint_one <file>
295: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
296: lint_one() {
297:   local file="$1"
298: 
299:   # Basic file check
300:   if [[ ! -f "$file" ]]; then
301:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "" "file not found"
302:     return
303:   fi
304: 
305:   # Run python3 analysis (passing the schema path enables the optional
306:   # PSFS schema cross-check when the jsonschema package is available)
307:   local schema_arg=""
308:   [[ -f "$SCHEMA_PATH" ]] && schema_arg="$SCHEMA_PATH"
309:   local json_out
310:   json_out="$(_lint_py3 "$file" "$schema_arg")"
311:   if [[ -z "$json_out" ]]; then
312:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "" "python3 lint helper returned no output"
313:     return
314:   fi
315: 
316:   # Extract fields via python3 json parsing (avoids jq dependency)
317:   local ok fm_error fm_end_line desc_len body_wc body_lc has_verb has_trigger
318:   ok="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d['ok'])")"
319:   fm_error="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d['error'] or '')" 2>/dev/null || echo '')"
320: 
321:   if [[ "$ok" != "True" ]]; then
322:     # Missing pyyaml is an environment advisory, not a content defect — WARN, not ERROR.
323:     if [[ "$fm_error" == *"install python3 pyyaml"* ]]; then
324:       emit_issue WARN "$file" "1" "$fm_error"
325:     else
326:       emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "$fm_error"
327:     fi
328:     return
329:   fi
330: 
331:   fm_end_line="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d['fm_end_line'])")"
332:   desc_len="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d.get('desc_len',0))")"
333:   body_wc="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d['body_word_count'])")"
334:   body_lc="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d.get('body_line_count',0))")"
335:   has_verb="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d.get('has_action_verb',False))")"
336:   has_trigger="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d.get('has_trigger_context',False))")"
337: 
338:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
339:   # Optional PSFS schema cross-check (mirrors the pyyaml-absent advisory above)
340:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
341:   local jsonschema_missing
342:   jsonschema_missing="$(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d.get('jsonschema_missing',False))")"
343:   if [[ "$jsonschema_missing" == "True" ]]; then
344:     if ! $JSONSCHEMA_ADVISED; then
345:       emit_issue WARN "$file" "1" "install python3 jsonschema for schema validation"
346:       JSONSCHEMA_ADVISED=true
347:     fi
348:   else
349:     # Emit each schema violation as an ERROR (structural defect).
350:     while IFS= read -r schema_err; do
351:       [[ -z "$schema_err" ]] && continue
352:       emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "schema: $schema_err"
353:     done < <(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; [print(e) for e in json.load(sys.stdin).get('schema_errors',[])]")
354:   fi
355: 
356:   # Angle-bracket safety (always-on, independent of jsonschema). This is the
357:   # enforcement home for the no-'<'/'>' rule: the JSON Schema can only pattern
358:   # the named typed fields, not arbitrary string leaves under the free-form
359:   # `metadata` object, and the schema check is skipped when jsonschema is absent.
360:   while IFS= read -r angle_path; do
361:     [[ -z "$angle_path" ]] && continue
362:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "$fm_end_line" \
363:       "frontmatter contains '<' or '>' in '$angle_path' (forbidden — frontmatter is injected into the system prompt)"
364:   done < <(printf '%s' "$json_out" | python3 -c "import sys,json; [print(e) for e in json.load(sys.stdin).get('angle_hits',[])]")
365: 
366:   # Derive slug and category from path
367:   local skill_dir slug category
368:   skill_dir="$(dirname "$file")"
369:   slug="$(basename "$skill_dir")"
370:   local parent_dir
371:   parent_dir="$(basename "$(dirname "$skill_dir")")"
372:   if [[ "$parent_dir" == "skills" ]]; then
373:     category="$slug"
374:   else
375:     category="$parent_dir"
376:   fi
377: 
378:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
379:   # Required field: name
380:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
381:   local name
382:   name="$(get_field "name" "$file")"
383:   if [[ -z "$name" ]]; then
384:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "required field 'name' is missing or empty"
385:   else
386:     # kebab-case check
387:     if ! printf '%s' "$name" | grep -qE '^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$'; then
388:       emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "name '$name' is not kebab-case (must match ^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$)"
389:     fi
390:     # length check
391:     if (( ${#name} > 64 )); then
392:       emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "name '$name' exceeds 64 characters"
393:     fi
394:     # name must match directory
395:     if [[ "$name" != "$slug" ]]; then
396:       emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "name '$name' does not match directory '$slug'"
397:     fi
398:   fi
399: 
400:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
401:   # Required field: version
402:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
403:   local version
404:   version="$(get_field "version" "$file")"
405:   if [[ -z "$version" ]]; then
406:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "required field 'version' is missing or empty"
407:   elif ! printf '%s' "$version" | grep -qE '^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$'; then
408:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "version '$version' is not valid semver (expected X.Y.Z)"
409:   fi
410: 
411:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
412:   # Required field: description
413:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
414:   if (( desc_len == 0 )); then
415:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "1" "required field 'description' is missing or empty"
416:   elif (( desc_len > 950 )); then
417:     # Ceiling-approach band (SR12): the schema hard-fails description >1024 chars.
418:     # WARN once a description crosses 950 so it gets trimmed BEFORE one more edit
419:     # pushes it over and turns a clean tree red. WARN-only — never touches exit.
420:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "description is ${desc_len} chars — within $(( 1024 - desc_len )) of the 1024 hard ceiling; trim trigger text (move detail to body/references) before an edit pushes it over and FAILs the schema check"
421:   elif (( desc_len > 500 )); then
422:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "description is ${desc_len} chars (consider whether some trigger context belongs in body/references)"
423:   elif (( desc_len > 200 )); then
424:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "description is ${desc_len} chars (soft target ≤200; pushy descriptions are OK per CLAUDE.md)"
425:   fi
426: 
427:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
428:   # Body: must exist
429:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
430:   if (( body_wc == 0 )); then
431:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "$(( fm_end_line + 1 ))" "body is empty (≥1 non-frontmatter line required)"
432:   elif (( body_wc < 50 )); then
433:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "body is ${body_wc} words (likely a stub; target ≥50)"
434:   fi
435: 
436:   # Body line count
437:   if (( body_lc > 500 )); then
438:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "body is ${body_lc} non-blank lines (target ≤500; move detail to references/)"
439:   fi
440: 
441:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
442:   # $ARGUMENTS token guard (WC-1). convert.sh ships the body of every skill
443:   # verbatim to 10 non-Claude-Code hosts (Cursor/Windsurf/Gemini/etc.) — none
444:   # of those converters translate Claude Code's slash-command `$ARGUMENTS`
445:   # placeholder, so a skill using it would silently ship a magic token those
446:   # hosts don't understand. Fail loudly rather than translate silently: with
447:   # zero skills using the token, there is no real host-specific behavior to
448:   # translate against yet, and a loud ERROR here is what forces that design
449:   # conversation the first time a skill actually needs it.
450:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
451:   if tail -n "+$(( fm_end_line + 1 ))" "$file" | grep -qF '$ARGUMENTS'; then
452:     emit_issue ERROR "$file" "" "body contains '\$ARGUMENTS' — this Claude-Code-only slash-command token is shipped verbatim (untranslated) to every other host by convert.sh; rewrite in host-neutral prose or gate the reference behind a Claude-Code-only note"
453:   fi
454: 
455:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
456:   # Description quality heuristics (WARN)
457:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
458:   if [[ "$has_verb" != "True" ]]; then
459:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "description may not start with an action verb (check first 5 words)"
460:   fi
461:   if [[ "$has_trigger" != "True" ]]; then
462:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "description lacks a trigger context (consider adding 'when', 'for', 'if', or 'whenever')"
463:   fi
464: 
465:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
466:   # Recommended fields for role skills
467:   # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
468:   if [[ "$category" == "roles" ]]; then
469:     local owns_dirs
470:     owns_dirs="$(get_owns_dirs "$file")"
471:     if [[ -z "$owns_dirs" ]]; then
472:       emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "role skill missing recommended 'owns.directories' (agent roles need exclusive ownership)"
473:     fi
474: 
475:     local allowed_tools
476:     allowed_tools="$(get_field "allowed-tools" "$file")"
477:     if [[ -z "$allowed_tools" ]]; then
478:       emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "role skill missing recommended 'allowed-tools' (prevents agents reaching outside their domain)"
479:     fi
480:   fi
481: 
482:   # composes_with recommended for all
483:   local composes_with_val
484:   composes_with_val="$(get_field "composes_with" "$file")"
485:   if [[ -z "$composes_with_val" ]]; then
486:     emit_issue WARN "$file" "" "missing recommended 'composes_with' (helps orchestrator understand skill relationships)"
487:   fi
488: }
489: 
490: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
491: # _glob_intersections <patterns_map_file> <allowlist>
492: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
493: # owns.patterns glob-intersection detector for cross_validate (SR18). Reads a
494: # TSV of "pattern<TAB>skill-name" lines and prints one line per cross-owner pair
495: # of patterns that can both match a single filename, EXCLUDING pairs whose two
496: # owners are in <allowlist> (newline-separated, sorted '::'-joined name pairs
497: # with a documented tiebreak). Output columns: ownerA<TAB>ownerB<TAB>patA<TAB>patB
498: # (owners sorted; each owner/pattern pair emitted once).
499: #
500: # Heuristic, NOT full glob algebra — it understands exactly two glob shapes:
501: #   extension  `*.EXT`      (e.g. *.tsx)      -> matches files ending .EXT
502: #   infix      `*.TOKEN.*`  (e.g. *.test.*)   -> matches files containing .TOKEN.
503: # and flags a pair as intersecting when: the patterns are identical; an
504: # extension meets an infix (a file like x.TOKEN.EXT matches both — the
505: # frontend `*.tsx` vs qe `*.test.*` case); or two infixes meet (x.A.B.y matches
506: # both). Any pattern that is NOT one of those two shapes (concrete filenames,
507: # prefix globs like `Dockerfile*`, char classes, `?`, braces, path-scoped
508: # globs) is conservatively treated as non-intersecting: this can miss real
509: # overlaps (false negatives) but never invents one (no false positives).
510: _glob_intersections() {
511:   local map_file="$1" allow="$2"
512:   python3 - "$map_file" "$allow" <<'PYEOF'
513: import sys, re
514: 
515: map_file = sys.argv[1]
516: allow = set(x for x in sys.argv[2].split('\n') if x.strip())
517: 
518: pairs = []
519: with open(map_file) as f:
520:     for line in f:
521:         line = line.rstrip('\n')
522:         if '\t' not in line:
523:             continue
524:         pat, owner = line.split('\t', 1)
525:         if pat and owner:
526:             pairs.append((pat, owner))
527: 
528: def ext(p):
529:     m = re.match(r'^\*\.([A-Za-z0-9]+)$', p)
530:     return m.group(1) if m else None
531: 
532: def infix(p):
533:     m = re.match(r'^\*\.([A-Za-z0-9]+)\.\*$', p)
534:     return m.group(1) if m else None
535: 
536: def intersect(p1, p2):
537:     if p1 == p2:
538:         return True
539:     e1, e2 = ext(p1), ext(p2)
540:     f1, f2 = infix(p1), infix(p2)
541:     if (e1 and f2) or (e2 and f1):   # extension meets infix
542:         return True
543:     if f1 and f2:                    # infix meets infix
544:         return True
545:     return False
546: 
547: seen = set()
548: n = len(pairs)
549: for i in range(n):
550:     for j in range(i + 1, n):
551:         p1, o1 = pairs[i]
552:         p2, o2 = pairs[j]
553:         if o1 == o2:
554:             continue
555:         if not intersect(p1, p2):
556:             continue
557:         key = '::'.join(sorted([o1, o2]))
558:         if key in allow:
559:             continue
560:         dedup = (key, tuple(sorted([p1, p2])))
561:         if dedup in seen:
562:             continue
563:         seen.add(dedup)
564:         a, b = sorted([o1, o2])
565:         print('\t'.join([a, b, p1, p2]))
566: PYEOF
567: }
568: 
569: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
570: # Cross-skill validation (requires all files)
571: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
572: cross_validate() {
573:   local files=("$@")
574: 
575:   # Build name→file map and collect owns.directories
576:   # Use temp files (bash 3.2 has no associative arrays)
577:   local name_map_file
578:   name_map_file="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-lint-names.XXXXXX")"
579:   local owns_map_file
580:   owns_map_file="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-lint-owns.XXXXXX")"
581:   local patterns_map_file
582:   patterns_map_file="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-lint-pats.XXXXXX")"
583:   # shellcheck disable=SC2064 # capture current values of temp files
584:   trap "rm -f '$name_map_file' '$owns_map_file' '$patterns_map_file'" RETURN
585: 
586:   local f name owns_dirs dir owns_pats pat
587: 
588:   # First pass: collect names and ownership
589:   for f in "${files[@]}"; do
590:     name="$(get_field "name" "$f")"
591:     [[ -z "$name" ]] && continue
592:     # Append "name TAB file" to map
593:     printf '%s\t%s\n' "$name" "$f" >> "$name_map_file"
594: 
595:     owns_dirs="$(get_owns_dirs "$f")"
596:     if [[ -n "$owns_dirs" ]]; then
