597:       while IFS= read -r dir; do
598:         [[ -z "$dir" ]] && continue
599:         printf '%s\t%s\n' "$dir" "$f" >> "$owns_map_file"
600:       done <<< "$owns_dirs"
601:     fi
602: 
603:     # Collect owns.patterns keyed by skill NAME (for the glob-intersection check)
604:     owns_pats="$(get_owns_patterns "$f")"
605:     if [[ -n "$owns_pats" ]]; then
606:       while IFS= read -r pat; do
607:         [[ -z "$pat" ]] && continue
608:         printf '%s\t%s\n' "$pat" "$name" >> "$patterns_map_file"
609:       done <<< "$owns_pats"
610:     fi
611:   done
612: 
613:   # Check name uniqueness
614:   while IFS= read -r name; do
615:     local count
616:     count="$(grep -c "^${name}	" "$name_map_file" || true)"
617:     if (( count > 1 )); then
618:       local files_with_name
619:       files_with_name="$(grep "^${name}	" "$name_map_file" | awk -F'\t' '{print $2}' | tr '\n' ' ')"
620:       emit_issue ERROR "(cross-skill)" "" "name '$name' is not unique: $files_with_name"
621:     fi
622:   done < <(awk -F'\t' '{print $1}' "$name_map_file" | sort | uniq)
623: 
624:   # Check owns.directories uniqueness (no overlap between role skills)
625:   while IFS= read -r dir; do
626:     local count
627:     count="$(grep -c "^${dir}	" "$owns_map_file" || true)"
628:     if (( count > 1 )); then
629:       local owners
630:       owners="$(grep "^${dir}	" "$owns_map_file" | awk -F'\t' '{print $2}' | tr '\n' ' ')"
631:       emit_issue ERROR "(cross-skill)" "" "owns.directories '$dir' claimed by multiple skills: $owners"
632:     fi
633:   done < <(awk -F'\t' '{print $1}' "$owns_map_file" | sort | uniq)
634: 
635:   # Check owns.patterns glob intersections across DIFFERENT skills (SR18). The
636:   # directory/uniqueness checks above only compare pattern strings for equality;
637:   # two DIFFERENT strings can still both match one file (e.g. frontend `*.tsx`
638:   # and qe `*.test.*` both match Button.test.tsx). WARN, don't fail — an
639:   # intersection is a design smell, not always a defect, and some are resolved
640:   # by a documented tiebreak.
641:   #
642:   # Documented-tiebreak allowlist: newline-separated, sorted '::'-joined skill
643:   # name pairs whose overlap is intentional and stated in
644:   # skills/orchestrator/references/file-ownership.md. Seed = frontend/qe:
645:   # test/spec files (qe-agent) win over UI-extension patterns (frontend-agent),
646:   # so Button.test.tsx belongs to qe-agent. Add a pair here only after recording
647:   # the tiebreak in that ownership map.
648:   local glob_tiebreak_allow="frontend-agent::qe-agent"
649:   if [[ -s "$patterns_map_file" ]]; then
650:     while IFS=$'\t' read -r g_owner_a g_owner_b g_pat_a g_pat_b; do
651:       [[ -z "$g_owner_a" ]] && continue
652:       emit_issue WARN "(cross-skill)" "" \
653:         "owns.patterns overlap — '$g_pat_a' ($g_owner_a) and '$g_pat_b' ($g_owner_b) can both match one file; assign the overlap to exactly one skill or document a tiebreak"
654:     done < <(_glob_intersections "$patterns_map_file" "$glob_tiebreak_allow")
655:   fi
656: 
657:   # Check composes_with references exist
658:   local all_names
659:   all_names="$(awk -F'\t' '{print $1}' "$name_map_file" | sort)"
660: 
661:   # Known bare external refs: skills that live outside this collection (a global
662:   # ~/.claude/skills/ skill, or a bare-invoked plugin skill) or Claude Code
663:   # built-in slash commands. Bare is their invocable form, so they are legitimate
664:   # and must not flag as "unknown". This is the allowlist mechanism for
665:   # non-namespaced externals in BOTH composes_with and spawned_by (colon-namespaced
666:   # refs like `plugin:name` are skipped separately, by design). Only add
667:   # genuinely-verified externals. See FA6, SR24.
668:   #   artifact-design, dataviz — host-global design skills that artifact-publish
669:   #   composes_with; they ship with the Claude Code host, not this collection.
670:   local known_external=" ux-review ui-ux-pro-max claude-api loop schedule artifact-design dataviz "
671: 
672:   for f in "${files[@]}"; do
673:     while IFS= read -r ref_name; do
674:       [[ -z "$ref_name" ]] && continue
675:       # Plugin-namespaced refs (plugin:name) are external by definition — out of
676:       # scope for in-collection resolution, so they are never flagged.
677:       [[ "$ref_name" == *:* ]] && continue
678:       # Known bare externals (global skills / built-in commands) are also fine.
679:       [[ "$known_external" == *" $ref_name "* ]] && continue
680:       if ! printf '%s\n' "$all_names" | grep -qxF "$ref_name"; then
681:         emit_issue WARN "$f" "" "composes_with references unknown skill '$ref_name'"
682:       fi
683:     done < <(get_array "composes_with" "$f")
684: 
685:     while IFS= read -r ref_name; do
686:       [[ -z "$ref_name" ]] && continue
687:       [[ "$ref_name" == *:* ]] && continue
688:       [[ "$known_external" == *" $ref_name "* ]] && continue
689:       if ! printf '%s\n' "$all_names" | grep -qxF "$ref_name"; then
690:         emit_issue WARN "$f" "" "spawned_by references unknown skill '$ref_name'"
691:       fi
692:     done < <(get_array "spawned_by" "$f")
693:   done
694: }
695: 
696: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
697: # lint_changed [base-ref]   (SR13: version-bump-drift guard)
698: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
699: # Opt-in, git-based check: ERROR when a skill's SKILL.md BODY (everything after
700: # the closing frontmatter ---) changed versus a base ref but its frontmatter
701: # `version:` did NOT. Frontmatter-only edits (e.g. a description tweak) do not
702: # require a bump — only body changes do.
703: #
704: # Compares the base ref against the WORKING TREE (staged + unstaged), so it
705: # catches local edits before they are committed. New files (absent in the base)
706: # and deletions are skipped: drift needs a prior version to compare against.
707: # base-ref defaults to origin/main, then main. This is NOT part of the static
708: # lint (it needs git); it runs as its own CI step — see the usage block at the
709: # top of this file and .github/workflows/lint-skills.yml (DV-2).
710: # bash-3.2-safe. Exits 0 (no drift) / 1 (drift) / 2 (git/base-ref error).
711: lint_changed() {
712:   local base_ref="$1"
713: 
714:   if ! git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree >/dev/null 2>&1; then
715:     ats_err "--changed: not inside a git work tree"
716:     exit 2
717:   fi
718: 
719:   if [[ -z "$base_ref" ]]; then
720:     if git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse --verify -q origin/main >/dev/null 2>&1; then
721:       base_ref="origin/main"
722:     elif git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse --verify -q main >/dev/null 2>&1; then
723:       base_ref="main"
724:     else
725:       ats_err "--changed: no base ref found (tried origin/main, main); pass one explicitly"
726:       exit 2
727:     fi
728:   fi
729: 
730:   if ! git -C "$REPO_ROOT" rev-parse --verify -q "${base_ref}^{commit}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
731:     ats_err "--changed: base ref '$base_ref' does not resolve to a commit"
732:     exit 2
733:   fi
734: 
735:   # Compare against the MERGE BASE, not the base ref's tip. On a branch that is
736:   # behind the base, a two-way diff also reports whatever landed on the base
737:   # since the fork — files this branch never touched — and any of those carrying
738:   # an unbumped body change would fail the branch for someone else's drift. The
739:   # CI job runs on push to any branch, not only on PR merge commits, so that
740:   # shape is reachable. Falls back to the ref itself if there is no merge base
741:   # (unrelated histories), which restores the old two-way behaviour.
742:   local base_rev
743:   base_rev="$(git -C "$REPO_ROOT" merge-base HEAD "$base_ref" 2>/dev/null || true)"
744:   [[ -z "$base_rev" ]] && base_rev="$base_ref"
745: 
746:   # Changed SKILL.md paths (repo-relative). Filter with grep rather than relying
747:   # on git pathspec magic, so nested paths are always matched.
748:   local changed
749:   changed="$(git -C "$REPO_ROOT" diff --name-only "$base_rev" 2>/dev/null | grep -E '(^|/)SKILL\.md$' || true)"
750: 
751:   local n_drift=0 n_checked=0
752:   local rel nowfile basecontent basetmp vbase vnow bbase bnow
753:   if [[ -n "$changed" ]]; then
754:     while IFS= read -r rel; do
755:       [[ -z "$rel" ]] && continue
756:       nowfile="$REPO_ROOT/$rel"
757:       [[ -f "$nowfile" ]] || continue   # deleted in the work tree — skip
758:       basecontent="$(git -C "$REPO_ROOT" show "${base_rev}:${rel}" 2>/dev/null || true)"
759:       [[ -z "$basecontent" ]] && continue   # new file (absent in base) — no prior version
760:       basetmp="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-drift.XXXXXX")"
761:       printf '%s\n' "$basecontent" > "$basetmp"
762:       vbase="$(get_field version "$basetmp")"
763:       vnow="$(get_field version "$nowfile")"
764:       bbase="$(get_body "$basetmp")"
765:       bnow="$(get_body "$nowfile")"
766:       rm -f "$basetmp"
767:       n_checked=$(( n_checked + 1 ))
768:       if [[ "$bbase" != "$bnow" && "$vbase" == "$vnow" ]]; then
769:         emit_issue ERROR "$rel" "" \
770:           "SKILL.md body changed since ${base_ref} but its version stayed ${vnow:-unset} — bump the frontmatter version field"
771:         n_drift=$(( n_drift + 1 ))
772:       fi
773:     done <<< "$changed"
774:   fi
775: 
776:   printf 'Version-drift check vs %s: %d changed SKILL.md file(s), %d drift(s).\n' \
777:     "$base_ref" "$n_checked" "$n_drift"
778:   local issue loc msg
779:   for issue in "${ERRORS[@]+"${ERRORS[@]}"}"; do
780:     msg="${issue##*:}"
781:     loc="${issue%:*}"
782:     printf '%-6s %s  %s\n' 'ERROR' "$loc" "$msg"
783:   done
784: 
785:   if (( n_drift > 0 )); then
786:     printf '\nFAILED: bump the version: of each skill whose body changed.\n'
787:     exit 1
788:   fi
789:   printf 'PASSED: no version-bump drift.\n'
790:   exit 0
791: }
792: 
793: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
794: # Output formatters
795: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
796: 
797: # Print issues in text format to stdout
798: print_text_report() {
799:   local n_files="$1"
800:   local n_errors=${#ERRORS[@]}
801:   local n_warns=${#WARNS[@]}
802: 
803:   printf 'Linting %s skills...\n\n' "$n_files"
804: 
805:   local issue
806:   for issue in "${ERRORS[@]+"${ERRORS[@]}"}"; do
807:     local loc msg
808:     # Split on last colon-prefixed message: "file:line:msg" or "file:msg"
809:     # We stored as "loc:msg" with loc possibly containing ":"
810:     # Strategy: last field after ":" is the message if it starts uppercase, else join
811:     msg="${issue##*:}"
812:     loc="${issue%:*}"
813:     printf '%-6s %s  %s\n' 'ERROR' "$loc" "$msg"
814:   done
815: 
816:   if ! $QUIET; then
817:     for issue in "${WARNS[@]+"${WARNS[@]}"}"; do
818:       msg="${issue##*:}"
819:       loc="${issue%:*}"
820:       printf '%-6s %s  %s\n' 'WARN' "$loc" "$msg"
821:     done
822:   fi
823: 
824:   if $VERBOSE; then
825:     for issue in "${INFOS[@]+"${INFOS[@]}"}"; do
826:       msg="${issue##*:}"
827:       loc="${issue%:*}"
828:       printf '%-6s %s  %s\n' 'INFO' "$loc" "$msg"
829:     done
830:   fi
831: 
832:   printf '\nResults: %d error(s), %d warning(s) across %d skills.\n' \
833:     "$n_errors" "$n_warns" "$n_files"
834: 
835:   if (( n_errors > 0 )); then
836:     printf 'FAILED: fix the errors above before merging.\n'
837:   else
838:     printf 'PASSED\n'
839:   fi
840: }
841: 
842: # Print issues in JUnit XML format to stdout
843: print_junit_report() {
844:   local n_files="$1"
845:   local n_errors=${#ERRORS[@]}
846: 
847:   printf '<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>\n'
848:   printf '<testsuites name="skill-lint" tests="%s" failures="%s" errors="0">\n' \
849:     "$n_files" "$n_errors"
850: 
851:   # Group by file
852:   local all_issues=()
853:   local issue
854:   for issue in "${ERRORS[@]+"${ERRORS[@]}"}"; do
855:     all_issues+=("ERROR:$issue")
856:   done
857:   if ! $QUIET; then
858:     for issue in "${WARNS[@]+"${WARNS[@]}"}"; do
859:       all_issues+=("WARN:$issue")
860:     done
861:   fi
862: 
863:   # Collect unique files mentioned
864:   local files_seen=()
865:   for issue in "${all_issues[@]+"${all_issues[@]}"}"; do
866:     local raw="${issue#*:}"
867:     local loc="${raw%:*}"
868:     # strip line number if present (loc might be "file:lineno")
869:     local fpart
870:     fpart="$(printf '%s' "$loc" | sed 's/:[0-9]*$//')"
871:     local already=false fs
872:     for fs in "${files_seen[@]+"${files_seen[@]}"}"; do
873:       [[ "$fs" == "$fpart" ]] && already=true && break
874:     done
875:     $already || files_seen+=("$fpart")
876:   done
877: 
878:   local fpart
879:   for fpart in "${files_seen[@]+"${files_seen[@]}"}"; do
880:     local suite_name="${fpart#"$REPO_ROOT"/}"
881:     local f_errors=0 f_warns=0
882:     for issue in "${ERRORS[@]+"${ERRORS[@]}"}"; do
883:       [[ "$issue" == "${fpart}"* ]] && f_errors=$(( f_errors + 1 ))
884:     done
885:     for issue in "${WARNS[@]+"${WARNS[@]}"}"; do
886:       [[ "$issue" == "${fpart}"* ]] && f_warns=$(( f_warns + 1 ))
887:     done
888:     local f_tests=$(( f_errors + f_warns ))
889:     (( f_tests == 0 )) && f_tests=1
890: 
891:     printf '  <testsuite name="%s" tests="%s" failures="%s">\n' \
892:       "$suite_name" "$f_tests" "$f_errors"
893: 
894:     for issue in "${ERRORS[@]+"${ERRORS[@]}"}"; do
895:       if [[ "$issue" == "${fpart}"* ]]; then
896:         local msg="${issue##*:}"
897:         local loc="${issue%:*}"
898:         local lineno="${loc##*:}"
899:         [[ "$lineno" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || lineno=""
900:         printf '    <testcase classname="frontmatter" name="%s">\n' \
901:           "$(printf '%s' "$msg" | sed 's/&/\&amp;/g;s/</\&lt;/g;s/>/\&gt;/g;s/"/\&quot;/g')"
902:         printf '      <failure message="%s"/>\n' \
903:           "$(printf '%s' "$msg" | sed 's/&/\&amp;/g;s/</\&lt;/g;s/>/\&gt;/g;s/"/\&quot;/g')"
904:         printf '    </testcase>\n'
905:       fi
906:     done
907: 
908:     printf '  </testsuite>\n'
909:   done
910: 
911:   printf '</testsuites>\n'
912: }
913: 
914: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
915: # Main
916: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
917: usage() {
918:   sed -n '3,25p' "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'
919:   exit 0
920: }
921: 
922: main() {
923:   while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
924:     case "$1" in
925:       --quiet)        QUIET=true; shift ;;
926:       --verbose)      VERBOSE=true; shift ;;
927:       --fix-trivial)  FIX_TRIVIAL=true; shift ;;  # future: implement trivial auto-fixes
928:       --format)       FORMAT="${2:?'--format requires a value'}"; shift 2 ;;
929:       --standard)     print_standard; exit 0 ;;
930:       --changed)
931:         CHANGED_MODE=true
932:         # Optional base ref immediately follows if it is not another flag.
933:         # --changed ignores PATH args, so consuming one non-flag token is safe.
934:         if [[ $# -ge 2 && "$2" != -* ]]; then
935:           CHANGED_BASE="$2"; shift 2
936:         else
937:           shift
938:         fi
939:         ;;
940:       --help|-h)      usage ;;
941:       -*)             ats_err "Unknown option: $1"; exit 2 ;;
942:       *)              LINT_PATHS+=("$1"); shift ;;
943:     esac
944:   done
945: 
946:   # --changed is a distinct, git-based mode (SR13): run the version-drift guard
947:   # and exit. It deliberately does not run the static per-file / cross-skill lint.
948:   if $CHANGED_MODE; then
949:     lint_changed "$CHANGED_BASE"
950:     # lint_changed always exits; this is unreachable but keeps intent explicit.
951:     exit $?
952:   fi
953: 
954:   case "$FORMAT" in
955:     text|junit) ;;
956:     *) ats_err "Unknown format '$FORMAT'. Use: text or junit"; exit 2 ;;
957:   esac
958: 
959:   # Collect files
960:   local files=()
961:   while IFS= read -r f; do
962:     [[ -n "$f" ]] && files+=("$f")
963:   done < <(collect_files)
964: 
965:   if [[ ${#files[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
966:     printf 'No SKILL.md files found.\n' >&2
967:     exit 1
968:   fi
969: 
970:   # Per-skill lint (--fix-trivial is reserved for a future interactive pass)
971:   if $FIX_TRIVIAL; then
972:     ats_warn "--fix-trivial: auto-fix not yet implemented; running lint-only"
973:   fi
974:   local f
975:   for f in "${files[@]}"; do
976:     lint_one "$f"
977:   done
978: 
979:   # Cross-skill checks
980:   cross_validate "${files[@]}"
981: 
982:   local n_errors=${#ERRORS[@]}
983:   local n_files=${#files[@]}
984: 
985:   # Print report
986:   if [[ "$FORMAT" == "junit" ]]; then
987:     print_junit_report "$n_files"
988:   else
989:     print_text_report "$n_files"
990:   fi
991: 
992:   if (( n_errors > 0 )); then
993:     exit 1
994:   fi
995:   exit 0
996: }
997: 
998: main "$@"

=== scripts/catalog.sh ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env bash
2: #
3: # catalog.sh — Catalog-as-CI-invariant for Skill Madness.
4: #
5: # The filesystem is the single source of truth for the skill catalog. This
6: # script computes the true inventory from disk and either checks every derived
7: # assertion against it (--check), rewrites them to match (--sync), or prints the
8: # table (--text). It covers:
9: #   - the count phrases in README.md, plugin.json, marketplace.json,
10: #     CLAUDE.md, PLAN.md, and START-HERE.md (live phrasings only — historical
11: #     "47 skills" notes are left alone), including per-category phrases
12: #     ("N role agents", "N meta-skills", the marketplace category breakdown,
13: #     CLAUDE.md's "skills/<cat>/ (N)" lines);
14: #   - the README one-row-per-skill catalog table: row count + skill names must
15: #     equal disk (--check only; the table's descriptions are hand-maintained so
16: #     --sync deliberately does not rewrite it — fix reported rows by hand);
17: #   - the README architecture-diagram boxes: named nodes + the "+ N more"
18: #     overflow must equal disk per category, node ids unique per graph
19: #     (--sync repairs only the overflow number);
20: #   - the plugin.json `skills` array itself (every on-disk skill registered,
21: #     no stale entries) — delegated to scripts/sync-catalog-skills.py.
22: #
23: # Contract: contracts/installer/catalog-invariant.md v1.2.0
24: #
25: # Usage:
26: #   scripts/catalog.sh [--check | --sync | --text] [--help]
27: #
28: # Options:
29: #   --check   (default) compare every asserted count + the plugin listing to
30: #             disk; exit 1 on any mismatch.
31: #   --sync    rewrite all counts + reconcile the plugin.json skills array.
32: #   --text    print total + per-category table to stdout.
33: #   --help    print this and exit.
34: #
35: # A skill = a directory directly under a category (skills/<cat>/<skill>/SKILL.md)
36: # or the top-level orchestrator (skills/orchestrator/SKILL.md). Anything under
37: # skills/archive/, skills/in-progress/, or node_modules/ — or nested deeper than
38: # a skill root (a bundled node_modules SKILL.md) — is NOT a skill; the -maxdepth
39: # scan and the python reconciler both enforce that.
40: #
41: # Exit codes: 0 ok / clean, 1 drift detected (--check), 2 argument failure.
42: #
43: # Bash 3.2 portable: no associative arrays, no mapfile.
44: 
45: set -euo pipefail
46: 
47: SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
48: LIB_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/lib"
49: REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
50: SKILLS_ROOT="$REPO_ROOT/skills"
51: 
52: # shellcheck source=scripts/lib/term.sh
53: . "$LIB_DIR/term.sh"
54: 
55: README="$REPO_ROOT/README.md"
56: PLUGIN_JSON="$REPO_ROOT/.claude-plugin/plugin.json"
57: MARKETPLACE_JSON="$REPO_ROOT/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json"
58: CLAUDE_MD="$REPO_ROOT/CLAUDE.md"
59: PLAN_MD="$REPO_ROOT/PLAN.md"
60: START_HERE="$REPO_ROOT/START-HERE.md"
61: 
62: # Companion that reconciles the plugin.json `skills` array with disk (registers
63: # new skills, drops stale entries). Delegated to from --check / --sync.
64: SKILLS_SYNC="$SCRIPT_DIR/sync-catalog-skills.py"
65: 
66: # The seven known categories, fixed order for stable output (matches plugin.json).
67: CATEGORIES="orchestrator roles contracts git meta workflows loops"
68: 
69: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
70: # Disk inventory
71: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
72: 
73: # _inventory <skills_root> — one find+awk pass over the tree, emitting
74: # "category|skill-name" per active skill ("orchestrator|orchestrator" for the
75: # top-level skill). Cached per process: every count/list below derives from
76: # this single pass instead of re-walking the tree with per-file subshells
77: # (the old per-file dirname/basename loop cost ~3 forks per skill per call,
78: # which multiplied badly once per-category assertions landed).
79: _INVENTORY_CACHE=""
80: _inventory() {
81:   local root="$1"
82:   if [[ -z "$_INVENTORY_CACHE" ]]; then
83:     _INVENTORY_CACHE="$(find "$root" -maxdepth 3 -name SKILL.md -type f 2>/dev/null \
84:       | grep -v "$root/archive/" \
85:       | grep -v "$root/in-progress/" \
86:       | awk -F/ '{
87:           if ($(NF-2) == "skills") print $(NF-1) "|" $(NF-1)
88:           else                     print $(NF-2) "|" $(NF-1)
89:         }')"
90:   fi
91:   [[ -n "$_INVENTORY_CACHE" ]] && printf '%s\n' "$_INVENTORY_CACHE"
92:   return 0
93: }
94: 
95: # count_total <skills_root>  — total active skills.
96: count_total() {
97:   _inventory "$1" | grep -c . || true
98: }
99: 
100: # count_category <skills_root> <category> — active skills in one category.
101: count_category() {
102:   _inventory "$1" | grep -c "^$2|" || true
103: }
104: 
105: # count_convertible <skills_root> — active skills that convert to non-Claude-
106: # Code hosts: requires_claude_code absent or false (the criterion convert.sh
107: # implements). Guards the README's hand-drifting "N of the M skills" phrases.
108: count_convertible() {
109:   local root="$1" n=0 f
110:   while IFS= read -r f; do
111:     grep -qE '^requires_claude_code:[[:space:]]*true' "$f" || n=$(( n + 1 ))
112:   done < <(find "$root" -maxdepth 3 -name SKILL.md -type f 2>/dev/null \
113:     | grep -v "$root/archive/" \
114:     | grep -v "$root/in-progress/")
115:   printf '%s\n' "$n"
116: }
117: 
118: # list_skills <skills_root> — print each active skill's name (the directory
119: # basename, or "orchestrator" for the top-level skill), one per line, sorted.
120: # Derives from the SAME inventory pass as count_total so the README-table
121: # check sees exactly the inventory the counts are derived from.
122: list_skills() {
123:   _inventory "$1" | cut -d'|' -f2 | LC_ALL=C sort
124: }
125: 
126: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
127: # --text
128: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
129: cmd_text() {
130:   local total cat n sum=0
131:   total="$(count_total "$SKILLS_ROOT")"
132:   ats_header "Skill catalog (disk truth)"
133:   printf '  %-16s %s\n' "CATEGORY" "COUNT"
134:   printf '  %-16s %s\n' "----------------" "-----"
135:   for cat in $CATEGORIES; do
136:     n="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" "$cat")"
137:     sum=$(( sum + n ))
138:     printf '  %-16s %s\n' "$cat" "$n"
139:   done
140:   printf '  %-16s %s\n' "----------------" "-----"
141:   printf '  %-16s %s\n' "TOTAL" "$total"
142:   if (( sum != total )); then
143:     ats_err "category sum ($sum) != total ($total)"
144:     return 1
145:   fi
146:   return 0
147: }
148: 
149: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
150: # Mismatch reporting / file rewriting
151: #
152: # We use sed for in-place rewrites. To stay portable across BSD (macOS) and GNU
153: # sed we write to a temp file and move it back (no -i flag differences).
154: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
155: 
156: # sed_replace <file> <sed-expr...> — apply one or more -e exprs portably.
157: # A missing target is a no-op: not every repo (or test fixture) carries every
158: # asserted-count file, and a count that isn't present simply can't drift.
159: sed_replace() {
160:   local file="$1"; shift
161:   [[ -f "$file" ]] || return 0
162:   local tmp
163:   tmp="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-catalog.XXXXXX")"
164:   sed "$@" "$file" > "$tmp"
165:   cat "$tmp" > "$file"
166:   rm -f "$tmp"
167: }
168: 
169: # MISMATCHES accumulates human-readable drift lines for --check.
170: MISMATCHES=()
171: 
172: # check_pattern <file> <label> <grep-ERE> <expected-number> <capture-sed>
173: # Finds every line matching grep-ERE in file, extracts the embedded number with
174: # capture-sed, and records a mismatch if it differs from expected. capture-sed
175: # must turn a matching line into just the number.
176: check_pattern() {
177:   local file="$1" label="$2" ere="$3" expected="$4" capture="$5"
178:   local line got
179:   while IFS= read -r line; do
180:     [[ -z "$line" ]] && continue
181:     got="$(printf '%s\n' "$line" | sed -E "$capture")"
182:     [[ -z "$got" ]] && continue
183:     if [[ "$got" != "$expected" ]]; then
184:       MISMATCHES+=("$label: found $got, disk says $expected  ($(basename "$file"))")
185:     fi
186:   done < <(grep -nE "$ere" "$file" 2>/dev/null || true)
187: }
188: 
189: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
190: # README skill-catalog TABLE check.
191: #
192: # The README carries a one-row-per-skill catalog table:
193: #     | # | Skill | Category | What it does |
194: #     |---|-------|----------|--------------|
195: #     | 1 | `orchestrator` | coordinator | ... |
196: #     ...
197: # This is hand-maintained and has drifted silently before (a new skill ships
198: # but no row is added), which the count-phrase checks above cannot catch — the
199: # total badge can be bumped without the table gaining a row.
200: #
201: # check_readme_table asserts the set of backticked skill names in that table
202: # equals the on-disk skill set: same cardinality, and (where parseable) the
203: # exact names. It is keyed on the table HEADER, so any README without that
204: # header (e.g. the bats fixture) is a clean no-op — never a false failure.
205: #
206: # Records drift in MISMATCHES (so the existing --check exit-1 path fires) AND
207: # prints an explicit [!!] line per missing/extra skill for an actionable diff.
208: check_readme_table() {
209:   local file="$1"
210:   [[ -f "$file" ]] || return 0
211: 
212:   # Locate the catalog-table header. No header -> no table to check (no-op).
213:   # `|| true` keeps a no-match grep from tripping `set -o pipefail`.
214:   local hdr
215:   hdr="$( { grep -nE '^\| *# *\| *Skill *\| *Category' "$file" 2>/dev/null || true; } \
216:            | head -n1 | cut -d: -f1)"
217:   [[ -z "$hdr" ]] && return 0
218: 
219:   # Collect backticked skill names from contiguous numbered rows after the
220:   # header (skip the `|---|` separator). Stop at the first non-row line.
221:   local tmp_t tmp_d
222:   tmp_t="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-table.XXXXXX")"
223:   awk -v start="$hdr" '
224:     NR <= start { next }
225:     /^\|[[:space:]]*-+/ { next }                 # separator row
226:     /^\|[[:space:]]*[0-9]+[[:space:]]*\|/ {       # | N | `name` | ... |
227:       if (match($0, /`[^`]+`/)) {
228:         s = substr($0, RSTART + 1, RLENGTH - 2)
229:         print s
230:       }
231:       next
232:     }
233:     { exit }                                      # first non-row line ends table
234:   ' "$file" | LC_ALL=C sort > "$tmp_t"
235: 
236:   tmp_d="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-disk.XXXXXX")"
237:   list_skills "$SKILLS_ROOT" > "$tmp_d"
238: 
239:   local disk_n table_n
240:   disk_n="$(wc -l < "$tmp_d" | tr -d ' ')"
241:   table_n="$(wc -l < "$tmp_t" | tr -d ' ')"
242: 
243:   # Cardinality assertion (always meaningful even if name parsing is imperfect).
244:   if [[ "$table_n" != "$disk_n" ]]; then
245:     MISMATCHES+=("README catalog table rows: found $table_n, disk says $disk_n  ($(basename "$file"))")
246:   fi
247: 
248:   # Name-level diff: skills on disk but missing a table row, and vice versa.
249:   local missing extra line
250:   missing="$(LC_ALL=C comm -23 "$tmp_d" "$tmp_t")"
251:   extra="$(LC_ALL=C comm -13 "$tmp_d" "$tmp_t")"
252: 
253:   if [[ -n "$missing" ]]; then
254:     while IFS= read -r line; do
255:       [[ -z "$line" ]] && continue
256:       MISMATCHES+=("README catalog table: missing row for on-disk skill '$line'")
257:     done <<EOF
258: $missing
259: EOF
260:   fi
261:   if [[ -n "$extra" ]]; then
262:     while IFS= read -r line; do
263:       [[ -z "$line" ]] && continue
264:       MISMATCHES+=("README catalog table: stale row '$line' (no such skill on disk)")
265:     done <<EOF
266: $extra
267: EOF
268:   fi
269: 
270:   rm -f "$tmp_t" "$tmp_d"
271: }
272: 
273: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
274: # README architecture-diagram BOX check.
275: #
276: # The category subgraph labels ("⚙️ workflows/ — 32 skills") are kept fresh by
277: # the MERMAID_LABELS assertions below, but the box CONTENTS are hand-drawn:
278: # named nodes plus an optional overflow node (`more["+ N more"]`). Both have
279: # drifted silently — 13 named + "+ 18 more" = 31 under a "32 skills" label,
280: # and a node id reused across two boxes made one box render a node short
281: # (mermaid node ids are global per graph). Per category box this asserts:
282: # named nodes + overflow == disk count, and no node id is defined twice inside
283: # one mermaid block. Boxes with no nodes at all are skipped (fixture READMEs
284: # carry bare subgraph headers), so a README without drawn boxes is a no-op.
285: #
286: # --sync repairs only the overflow number; named nodes are hand-maintained,
287: # like the catalog table — add/remove those by hand.
288: 
289: # _mermaid_boxes <file> — emit "BOX|<subgraph-id>|<category>|<named>|<overflow-or-->"
290: # per category box and "DUP|<node-id>" per duplicated node id.
291: _mermaid_boxes() {
292:   awk '
293:     function flushbox() {
294:       if (box != "") print "BOX|" box "|" cat "|" named "|" over
295:       box = ""
296:     }
297:     /^```mermaid[[:space:]]*$/ { inm = 1; box = ""; delete seen; next }
298:     inm && /^```/ {
299:       flushbox()
300:       for (id in seen) if (seen[id] > 1) print "DUP|" id
301:       inm = 0
302:       next
303:     }
304:     !inm { next }
305:     /^[[:space:]]*subgraph[[:space:]]/ {
306:       flushbox()
307:       cat = ""
308:       line = $0
309:       sub(/^[[:space:]]*subgraph[[:space:]]+/, "", line)
310:       id = line; sub(/\[.*$/, "", id)
311:       # Category boxes carry a "<dir>/ — N skills|agents" label.
312:       if (match(line, /[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\/ — [0-9]+ (skills|agents)/)) {
313:         cat = substr(line, RSTART, RLENGTH)
314:         sub(/\/.*$/, "", cat)
315:         box = id; named = 0; over = "-"
316:       }
317:       next
318:     }
319:     /^[[:space:]]*end[[:space:]]*$/ { flushbox(); next }
320:     match($0, /^[[:space:]]*[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*\[/) {
321:       id = $0
322:       sub(/^[[:space:]]*/, "", id); sub(/\[.*$/, "", id)
323:       seen[id]++
324:       if (box != "") {
325:         if ($0 ~ /\[" *\+ *[0-9]+ more *"\]/) {
326:           over = $0
327:           sub(/^.*\[" *\+ */, "", over); sub(/ more.*$/, "", over)
328:         } else {
329:           named++
330:         }
331:       }
332:       next
333:     }
334:   ' "$1"
335: }
336: 
337: check_readme_mermaid_boxes() {
338:   local file="$1"
339:   [[ -f "$file" ]] || return 0
340:   local kind id cat named over disk
341:   while IFS='|' read -r kind id cat named over; do
342:     case "$kind" in
343:       DUP)
344:         MISMATCHES+=("README mermaid: node id '$id' defined more than once (one box renders short — rename one id)")
345:         ;;
346:       BOX)
347:         [[ "$named" == "0" && "$over" == "-" ]] && continue
348:         disk="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" "$cat")"
349:         if [[ "$over" == "-" ]]; then
350:           if (( named != disk )); then
351:             MISMATCHES+=("README mermaid ${cat}/ box: $named named nodes, disk says $disk (add/remove nodes by hand)")
352:           fi
353:         else
354:           if (( named + over != disk )); then
355:             MISMATCHES+=("README mermaid ${cat}/ box: $named named + $over more = $(( named + over )), disk says $disk")
356:           fi
357:         fi
358:         ;;
359:     esac
360:   done < <(_mermaid_boxes "$file")
361: }
362: 
363: # sync_readme_mermaid_overflow <file> — rewrite each box's "+ N more" node so
364: # named + N == disk. Boxes without an overflow node are left for --check.
365: sync_readme_mermaid_overflow() {
366:   local file="$1"
367:   [[ -f "$file" ]] || return 0
368:   local kind id cat named over disk expect tmp
369:   while IFS='|' read -r kind id cat named over; do
370:     [[ "$kind" == "BOX" ]] || continue
371:     [[ "$over" == "-" ]] && continue
372:     disk="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" "$cat")"
373:     expect=$(( disk - named ))
374:     (( expect < 1 )) && continue
375:     if (( named + over != disk )); then
376:       tmp="$(mktemp "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/ats-mermaid.XXXXXX")"
377:       awk -v sg="$id" -v n="$expect" '
378:         /^[[:space:]]*subgraph[[:space:]]/ {
379:           line = $0
380:           sub(/^[[:space:]]*subgraph[[:space:]]+/, "", line)
381:           cur = line; sub(/\[.*$/, "", cur)
382:           inbox = (cur == sg)
383:         }
384:         /^[[:space:]]*end[[:space:]]*$/ { inbox = 0 }
385:         inbox && /\[" *\+ *[0-9]+ more *"\]/ {
386:           sub(/\[" *\+ *[0-9]+ more *"\]/, "[\"+ " n " more\"]")
387:         }
388:         { print }
389:       ' "$file" > "$tmp"
390:       cat "$tmp" > "$file"
391:       rm -f "$tmp"
392:     fi
393:   done < <(_mermaid_boxes "$file")
394: }
395: 
396: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
397: # Per-category README mermaid label config.
398: # Maps the README label token -> category -> noun (skills|agents).
399: # Format: "<label-prefix>|<category>|<noun>"
400: # e.g. "contracts/|contracts|skills"  matches "contracts/ — 2 skills".
401: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
402: MERMAID_LABELS=(
403:   "contracts/|contracts|skills"
404:   "roles/|roles|agents"
405:   "meta/|meta|skills"
406:   "git/|git|skills"
407:   "workflows/|workflows|skills"
408:   "loops/|loops|skills"
409: )
410: 
411: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
412: # Build the list of (file, label, grep-ERE, expected, capture-sed) tuples that
413: # describe every asserted count. Emitted via a callback so --check and --sync
414: # share exactly one definition of "where the counts live".
415: #
416: # for_each_assertion <callback>
417: #   callback <file> <label> <grep-ERE> <expected> <capture-sed> <sync-sed>
418: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
419: for_each_assertion() {
420:   local cb="$1"
421:   local total spec cat noun n
422: 
423:   total="$(count_total "$SKILLS_ROOT")"
424: 
425:   # --- README total-count assertions ---------------------------------------
426:   # shields badge:  skills-47-success.svg  and alt="47 skills"
427:   "$cb" "$README" "README badge (skills-N)" \
428:     'skills-[0-9]+-' "$total" \
429:     's/.*skills-([0-9]+)-.*/\1/' \
430:     "s/(skills-)[0-9]+(-)/\1${total}\2/g"
431: 
432:   "$cb" "$README" "README badge alt (N skills)" \
433:     'alt="[0-9]+ skills"' "$total" \
434:     's/.*alt="([0-9]+) skills".*/\1/' \
435:     "s/(alt=\")[0-9]+( skills\")/\1${total}\2/g"
436: 
437:   # Each prose location is targeted by its distinctive surrounding text so we
438:   # never touch the skill-table row index ("| 47 |") or the per-category
439:   # mermaid labels ("contracts/ — 2 skills"), which carry different numbers.
440: 
441:   # "N skills, seven categories" — feature bullet (~45) and roadmap line (~544).
442:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (N skills, seven categories)" \
443:     '[0-9]+ skills, seven categories' "$total" \
444:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) skills, seven categories.*/\1/' \
445:     "s/[0-9]+( skills, seven categories)/${total}\1/g"
446: 
447:   # "all N skills" — install instruction (~80).
448:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (installs all N skills)" \
449:     'all [0-9]+ skills' "$total" \
450:     's/.*all ([0-9]+) skills.*/\1/' \
451:     "s/(all )[0-9]+( skills)/\1${total}\2/g"
452: 
453:   # "N skills organized into six categories" — catalog intro (~252).
454:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (N skills organized)" \
455:     '[0-9]+ skills organized' "$total" \
456:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) skills organized.*/\1/' \
457:     "s/[0-9]+( skills organized)/${total}\1/g"
458: 
459:   # "N-skill library" — progressive-disclosure bullet (~43) and status (~49).
460:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (N-skill library)" \
461:     '[0-9]+-skill library' "$total" \
462:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+)-skill library.*/\1/' \
463:     "s/[0-9]+(-skill library)/${total}\1/g"
464: 
465:   # "N portable skills" — the hero tagline (~9). Drifted 67-vs-68 unguarded.
466:   "$cb" "$README" "README hero (N portable skills)" \
467:     '[0-9]+ portable skills' "$total" \
468:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) portable skills.*/\1/' \
469:     "s/[0-9]+( portable skills)/${total}\1/g"
470: 
471:   # "out of all N" — the madness-router blurb (~63) and front-door bullet (~80).
472:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (out of all N)" \
473:     'out of all [0-9]+' "$total" \
474:     's/.*out of all ([0-9]+).*/\1/' \
475:     "s/(out of all )[0-9]+/\1${total}/g"
476: 
477:   # "skill library (N)" — the project-structure tree comment (~453).
478:   "$cb" "$README" "README tree (skill library (N))" \
479:     'skill library \([0-9]+\)' "$total" \
480:     's/.*skill library \(([0-9]+)\).*/\1/' \
481:     "s/(skill library \()[0-9]+(\))/\1${total}\2/g"
482: 
483:   # --- Convertible-subset assertions (PF3) ----------------------------------
484:   # "N of the M skills" — the other-hosts section (~501) and troubleshooting
485:   # (~675). N = skills without requires_claude_code:true (what convert.sh
486:   # ships); M = the disk total. Two assertions per phrase so a flag flip or a
487:   # new skill can't silently strand either number.
488:   local convertible
489:   convertible="$(count_convertible "$SKILLS_ROOT")"
490:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (N of the M skills — convertible)" \
491:     '[0-9]+ of the [0-9]+ skills' "$convertible" \
492:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) of the [0-9]+ skills.*/\1/' \
493:     "s/[0-9]+( of the [0-9]+ skills)/${convertible}\1/g"
494:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (N of the M skills — total)" \
495:     '[0-9]+ of the [0-9]+ skills' "$total" \
496:     's/.*[0-9]+ of the ([0-9]+) skills.*/\1/' \
497:     "s/( of the )[0-9]+( skills)/\1${total}\2/g"
498: 
499:   # --- Per-category prose/badge phrases (README + plugin/marketplace) --------
500:   # These carry per-category numbers that the total-only sync used to strand
501:   # (the marketplace breakdown shipped 41-era counts at a 68-skill HEAD).
502:   local n_roles n_contracts n_git n_meta n_workflows n_loops f
503:   n_roles="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" roles)"
504:   n_contracts="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" contracts)"
505:   n_git="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" git)"
506:   n_meta="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" meta)"
507:   n_workflows="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" workflows)"
508:   n_loops="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" loops)"
509: 
510:   for f in "$README" "$PLUGIN_JSON" "$MARKETPLACE_JSON"; do
511:     "$cb" "$f" "per-category (N role agents)" \
512:       '[0-9]+ role agents' "$n_roles" \
513:       's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) role agents.*/\1/' \
514:       "s/[0-9]+( role agents)/${n_roles}\1/g"
515:     "$cb" "$f" "per-category (N contract skills)" \
516:       '[0-9]+ contract skills' "$n_contracts" \
517:       's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) contract skills.*/\1/' \
518:       "s/[0-9]+( contract skills)/${n_contracts}\1/g"
519:     "$cb" "$f" "per-category (N git workflow skills)" \
520:       '[0-9]+ git[- ]workflow skills' "$n_git" \
521:       's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) git[- ]workflow skills.*/\1/' \
522:       "s/[0-9]+( git[- ]workflow skills)/${n_git}\1/g"
523:     "$cb" "$f" "per-category (N meta skills)" \
524:       '[0-9]+ meta[-/]s' "$n_meta" \
525:       's@.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) meta[-/]s.*@\1@' \
526:       "s@[0-9]+( meta[-/]s)@${n_meta}\1@g"
527:     "$cb" "$f" "per-category (N cross-cutting workflow)" \
528:       '[0-9]+ cross-cutting workflow' "$n_workflows" \
529:       's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) cross-cutting workflow.*/\1/' \
530:       "s/[0-9]+( cross-cutting workflow)/${n_workflows}\1/g"
531:     "$cb" "$f" "per-category (N autonomous-loop skills)" \
532:       '[0-9]+ autonomous-loop skills' "$n_loops" \
533:       's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) autonomous-loop skills.*/\1/' \
534:       "s/[0-9]+( autonomous-loop skills)/${n_loops}\1/g"
535:   done
536: 
537:   # README-only per-category phrasings: badges + loop prose.
538:   "$cb" "$README" "README badge (role%20agents-N)" \
539:     'role%20agents-[0-9]+-' "$n_roles" \
540:     's/.*role%20agents-([0-9]+)-.*/\1/' \
541:     "s/(role%20agents-)[0-9]+(-)/\1${n_roles}\2/g"
542:   "$cb" "$README" "README badge (autonomous%20loops-N)" \
543:     'autonomous%20loops-[0-9]+-' "$n_loops" \
544:     's/.*autonomous%20loops-([0-9]+)-.*/\1/' \
545:     "s/(autonomous%20loops-)[0-9]+(-)/\1${n_loops}\2/g"
546:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (N autonomous loops)" \
547:     '[0-9]+ autonomous loops' "$n_loops" \
548:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) autonomous loops.*/\1/' \
549:     "s/[0-9]+( autonomous loops)/${n_loops}\1/g"
550:   "$cb" "$README" "README prose (N loop skills)" \
551:     '[0-9]+ loop skills' "$n_loops" \
552:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) loop skills.*/\1/' \
553:     "s/[0-9]+( loop skills)/${n_loops}\1/g"
554: 
555:   # PLAN.md "a **13-skill autonomous-loop library**".
556:   "$cb" "$PLAN_MD" "PLAN.md (N-skill autonomous-loop library)" \
557:     '[0-9]+-skill autonomous-loop library' "$n_loops" \
558:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+)-skill autonomous-loop library.*/\1/' \
559:     "s/[0-9]+(-skill autonomous-loop library)/${n_loops}\1/g"
560: 
561:   # --- CLAUDE.md per-category "(N)" breakdown --------------------------------
562:   # "- **`skills/<cat>/`** (N) — ..." — the always-loaded instruction file's
563:   # category counts drifted silently after #33 (workflows said 31 at a 32 disk).
564:   for cat in $CATEGORIES; do
565:     n="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" "$cat")"
566:     "$cb" "$CLAUDE_MD" "CLAUDE.md category skills/${cat}/ (N)" \
567:       "\\*\\*\`skills/${cat}/\`\\*\\* \\([0-9]+\\)" "$n" \
568:       "s@.*\`skills/${cat}/\`\\*\\* \\(([0-9]+)\\).*@\\1@" \
569:       "s@(\`skills/${cat}/\`\\*\\* \\()[0-9]+(\\))@\\1${n}\\2@"
570:   done
571: 
572:   # --- README per-category mermaid labels ("contracts/ — 2 skills") ---------
573:   for spec in "${MERMAID_LABELS[@]}"; do
574:     local prefix
575:     prefix="${spec%%|*}"
576:     cat="${spec#*|}"; cat="${cat%%|*}"
577:     noun="${spec##*|}"
578:     n="$(count_category "$SKILLS_ROOT" "$cat")"
579:     # Escape '/' in prefix for use inside the regex/sed (e.g. "contracts/").
580:     local pesc
581:     pesc="$(printf '%s' "$prefix" | sed 's/[\/&]/\\&/g')"
582:     "$cb" "$README" "README mermaid ${prefix} — N ${noun}" \
583:       "${pesc} — [0-9]+ ${noun}" "$n" \
584:       "s/.*${pesc} — ([0-9]+) ${noun}.*/\1/" \
585:       "s/(${pesc} — )[0-9]+( ${noun})/\1${n}\2/g"
586:   done
587: 
588:   # --- plugin.json description "N skills" -----------------------------------
589:   "$cb" "$PLUGIN_JSON" "plugin.json description (N skills)" \
590:     'with [0-9]+ skills' "$total" \
591:     's/.*with ([0-9]+) skills.*/\1/' \
592:     "s/(with )[0-9]+( skills)/\1${total}\2/g"
593: 
594:   # --- marketplace.json count phrases ---------------------------------------
595:   # metadata.description: "the 41-skill library"
596:   "$cb" "$MARKETPLACE_JSON" "marketplace metadata (N-skill)" \
597:     'the [0-9]+-skill library' "$total" \
598:     's/.*the ([0-9]+)-skill library.*/\1/' \
599:     "s/(the )[0-9]+(-skill library)/\1${total}\2/g"
600: 
601:   # plugins[].description: "Install all 41 Skill-Madness skills"
602:   "$cb" "$MARKETPLACE_JSON" "marketplace plugin desc (all N)" \
603:     'Install all [0-9]+ Skill-Madness skills' "$total" \
604:     's/.*Install all ([0-9]+) Skill-Madness skills.*/\1/' \
605:     "s/(Install all )[0-9]+( Skill-Madness skills)/\1${total}\2/g"
606: 
607:   # --- CLAUDE.md / PLAN.md / START-HERE.md live total counts -----------------
608:   # Only the LIVE current-count phrasings are targeted. Historical mentions
609:   # ("47 skills across 6 categories", "validating all 47 skills clean",
610:   # "across 49 skills") use different wording and are deliberately left alone.
611: 
612:   # CLAUDE.md "What This Is": "— N OSS-publishable skills in `skills/`".
613:   "$cb" "$CLAUDE_MD" "CLAUDE.md (N OSS-publishable skills)" \
614:     '[0-9]+ OSS-publishable skills' "$total" \
615:     's/.*[^0-9]([0-9]+) OSS-publishable skills.*/\1/' \
616:     "s/[0-9]+( OSS-publishable skills)/${total}\1/g"
617: 
618:   # PLAN.md "Where we are": "a mature **N-skill** library".
619:   "$cb" "$PLAN_MD" "PLAN.md (N-skill library)" \
620:     '\*\*[0-9]+-skill\*\* library' "$total" \
621:     's/.*\*\*([0-9]+)-skill\*\* library.*/\1/' \
622:     "s/(\*\*)[0-9]+(-skill\*\* library)/\1${total}\2/g"
623: 
624:   # START-HERE.md "Status at a glance": "A mature library of **N skills**".
625:   "$cb" "$START_HERE" "START-HERE.md (library of N skills)" \
626:     'library of \*\*[0-9]+ skills\*\*' "$total" \
627:     's/.*library of \*\*([0-9]+) skills\*\*.*/\1/' \
628:     "s/(library of \*\*)[0-9]+( skills\*\*)/\1${total}\2/g"
629: }
630: 
631: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
632: # --check
633: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
634: _check_cb() {
635:   # args: file label ere expected capture-sed sync-sed
636:   check_pattern "$1" "$2" "$3" "$4" "$5"
637: }
638: 
639: cmd_check() {
640:   MISMATCHES=()
641:   for_each_assertion _check_cb
642:   # Additionally assert the README's one-row-per-skill catalog table matches
643:   # disk (row count + skill names). No-op on READMEs without the table header.
644:   check_readme_table "$README"
645:   # And the architecture-diagram boxes: named nodes + "+ N more" == disk per
646:   # category, no duplicate node ids. No-op on READMEs without drawn boxes.
647:   check_readme_mermaid_boxes "$README"
648: 
649:   local total rc=0
650:   total="$(count_total "$SKILLS_ROOT")"
651: 
652:   if (( ${#MISMATCHES[@]} > 0 )); then
653:     ats_err "catalog drift: asserted counts disagree with disk (disk total = $total)"
654:     local m
655:     for m in "${MISMATCHES[@]}"; do
656:       ats_warn "$m"
657:     done
658:     rc=1
659:   fi
660: 
661:   # plugin.json `skills` array registration — delegated to the reconciler, which
662:   # prints its own drift lines. Missing python3 degrades to count-only checking.
663:   if [[ -f "$SKILLS_SYNC" ]] && command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then
664:     python3 "$SKILLS_SYNC" --check || rc=1
665:   fi
666: 
667:   if (( rc != 0 )); then
668:     ats_info "run 'scripts/catalog.sh --sync' to reconcile"
669:     return 1
670:   fi
671:   ats_ok "catalog clean: counts + plugin.json listing all match disk (total = $total)"
672:   return 0
673: }
674: 
675: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
676: # --sync
677: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
678: _sync_cb() {
679:   # args: file label ere expected capture-sed sync-sed
680:   local file="$1" sync_sed="$6"
681:   sed_replace "$file" -E -e "$sync_sed"
682: }
683: 
684: cmd_sync() {
685:   for_each_assertion _sync_cb
686:   # Repair the architecture-diagram overflow nodes ("+ N more") from disk.
687:   sync_readme_mermaid_overflow "$README"
688:   # Reconcile the plugin.json `skills` array (register new, drop stale).
689:   if [[ -f "$SKILLS_SYNC" ]] && command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then
690:     python3 "$SKILLS_SYNC" --sync
691:   fi
692:   local total
693:   total="$(count_total "$SKILLS_ROOT")"
694:   ats_ok "synced counts + plugin.json listing to disk (total = $total)"
695:   return 0
696: }
697: 
698: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
699: # Main
700: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
701: usage() {
702:   sed -n '3,28p' "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'
703:   exit 0
704: }
705: 
706: main() {
707:   local mode="check"
708:   while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
709:     case "$1" in
710:       --check) mode="check"; shift ;;
711:       --sync)  mode="sync";  shift ;;
712:       --text)  mode="text";  shift ;;
713:       --help|-h) usage ;;
714:       -*) ats_err "Unknown option: $1"; exit 2 ;;
715:       *)  ats_err "Unexpected argument: $1"; exit 2 ;;
716:     esac
717:   done
718: 
719:   case "$mode" in
720:     text)  cmd_text ;;
721:     check) cmd_check ;;
722:     sync)  cmd_sync ;;
723:   esac
724: }
725: 
726: main "$@"

=== .github/workflows/lint-skills.yml ===
1: name: Skill Lint
2: 
3: # Trigger on every push to any branch and on PRs targeting main.
4: # This ensures lint failures are caught both during feature development
5: # and at the merge gate.
6: on:
7:   push:
8:     branches:
9:       - "**"
10:   pull_request:
11:     branches:
12:       - main
13: 
14: # Cancel in-progress runs for the same ref on rapid pushes.
15: # Saves CI minutes and reduces queue congestion without losing coverage.
16: concurrency:
17:   group: lint-skills-${{ github.ref }}
18:   cancel-in-progress: true
19: 
20: # NOTE: the five Ubuntu job display names below are pinned as required status
21: # checks in the main-branch protection rules. Renaming any of them strands the
22: # PR gate on a check that never reports. Change steps, not names.
23: jobs:
24:   # Primary blocking job. A non-zero exit from lint-skills.sh fails the PR.
25:   lint-ubuntu:
26:     name: Lint Skills (Ubuntu)
27:     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
28:     steps:
29:       # Full history (DV-2): the version-drift guard below diffs against
30:       # origin/main, which a shallow fetch-depth: 1 checkout would not have.
31:       - name: Checkout repository
32:         uses: actions/checkout@v5
33:         with:
34:           fetch-depth: 0
35: 
36:       # python3 is preinstalled on ubuntu-latest; this step confirms
37:       # availability and surfaces the version in the job log.
38:       - name: Verify python3
39:         run: python3 --version
40: 
41:       # bats-core is used by lint-skills.sh for test execution.
42:       # The apt package is named 'bats' on Ubuntu.
43:       # Refresh the package index first — older runner images can 404
44:       # against stale package URLs.
45:       - name: Install bats-core
46:         run: |
47:           sudo apt-get update -y
48:           sudo apt-get install -y bats
49: 
50:       # First pass: capture JUnit output regardless of exit code.
51:       # The '|| true' prevents early failure so the artifact is always
52:       # produced before the authoritative gate run below.
53:       - name: Run lint (capture JUnit report)
54:         run: scripts/lint-skills.sh --format junit > lint-results.xml || true
55: 
56:       # Second pass: real gate. Exits non-zero on any lint violation,
57:       # which marks the job failed. No '|| true' here — intentional.
58:       # (The class-extraction-guard bats suite that used to run here is
59:       # covered by the full-suite run in 'Installer + Catalog (Ubuntu)'.)
60:       - name: Run lint (enforce pass/fail)
61:         run: scripts/lint-skills.sh skills/
62: 
63:       # Version-drift guard (SR13; wired into CI by DV-2): a SKILL.md whose
64:       # body changed but whose frontmatter version: did not fails the PR.
65:       # Diffed against origin/main (available via the fetch-depth: 0 checkout
66:       # above). Exits non-zero on any drifted skill — no '|| true' here.
67:       - name: Check version-drift guard
68:         run: scripts/lint-skills.sh --changed origin/main
69: 
70:       # Upload the JUnit XML even when the job fails so reviewers can
71:       # inspect which rules fired without re-running locally.
72:       - name: Upload lint results
73:         if: always()
74:         uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
75:         with:
76:           name: lint-results-ubuntu
77:           path: lint-results.xml
78: 
79:   # Hooks layer: lint the hooks/ tree and run its bats suite.
80:   # Additive job — independent of the skill lint gate above. Uses only static
81:   # run commands (no GitHub event input), so no injection surface.
82:   hooks-ubuntu:
83:     name: Hooks Layer (Ubuntu)
84:     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
85:     steps:
86:       - name: Checkout repository
87:         uses: actions/checkout@v5
88: 
89:       - name: Verify python3
90:         run: python3 --version
91: 
92:       - name: Install bats-core
93:         run: |
94:           sudo apt-get update -y
95:           sudo apt-get install -y bats
96: 
97:       # Static lint of the hooks tree (manifest, executability, script headers,
98:       # profile validity). Non-zero exit fails the job.
99:       # (The hooks bats suite that used to run here is covered by the
100:       # full-suite run in 'Installer + Catalog (Ubuntu)'.)
101:       - name: Lint hooks layer
102:         run: scripts/lint-hooks.sh
103: 
104:   # Installer discipline (P1) + the single authoritative bats gate: assert the
105:   # advertised skill counts match disk (catalog-as-CI-invariant), then run the
106:   # ENTIRE bats suite via tests/run-all.sh (`bats -r tests/`). This is what
107:   # makes tests/installer/ (convert.sh output for all 11 hosts) and
108:   # tests/skill-health/ gate a merge — a plain `bats tests/` is non-recursive
109:   # and silently runs zero tests. Static run commands only.
110:   installer-ubuntu:
111:     name: Installer + Catalog (Ubuntu)
112:     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
113:     steps:
114:       - name: Checkout repository
115:         uses: actions/checkout@v5
116: 
117:       - name: Verify python3
118:         run: python3 --version
119: 
120:       # jsonschema lets the tests/standard/ real-tree schema gate run
121:       # authoritatively instead of SKIPping; pyyaml is the frontmatter parser.
122:       # --break-system-packages is required on PEP 668 Pythons.
123:       - name: Install jsonschema + pyyaml
124:         run: python3 -m pip install --user --break-system-packages jsonschema pyyaml
125: 
126:       - name: Install bats-core
127:         run: |
128:           sudo apt-get update -y
129:           sudo apt-get install -y bats
130: 
131:       # Catalog-as-CI-invariant: fails the build if README / plugin.json /
132:       # marketplace.json skill counts have drifted from the filesystem.
133:       - name: Catalog count check
134:         run: scripts/catalog.sh --check
135: 
136:       # Every .bats file under tests/ — installer, catalog, install-plan,
137:       # skill-health, hooks, standard, scan-skills, class-extraction-guard.
138:       - name: Run full bats suite
139:         run: bash tests/run-all.sh
140: 
141:   # Skill supply-chain / injection scan (P2): deterministic, LLM-free gate over
142:   # the skills tree (secrets, prompt-injection, zero-width unicode, untrusted
143:   # composes_with). Blocks the build on any HIGH finding. Static commands only.
144:   security-ubuntu:
145:     name: Skill Scan (Ubuntu)
146:     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
147:     steps:
148:       - name: Checkout repository
149:         uses: actions/checkout@v5
150: 
151:       - name: Verify python3
152:         run: python3 --version
153: 
154:       - name: Install bats-core
155:         run: |
156:           sudo apt-get update -y
157:           sudo apt-get install -y bats
158: 
159:       # Blocking: non-zero exit on any HIGH-severity finding (secrets, private
160:       # keys, zero-width unicode). MEDIUM/LOW are reported but do not fail.
161:       # (The scan-skills bats suite that used to run here is covered by the
162:       # full-suite run in 'Installer + Catalog (Ubuntu)'.)
163:       - name: Scan skills (block on HIGH)
164:         run: scripts/scan-skills.sh --check skills/
165: 
166:   # Frontmatter standard (P3): enforce the Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec
167:   # (PSFS) as a real gate. The main lint job's schema cross-check degrades to
168:   # an advisory when jsonschema is absent — this job installs it so the
169:   # portable schema validates the whole tree authoritatively. Static commands
170:   # only; no GitHub event input.
171:   standard-ubuntu:
172:     name: Frontmatter Standard / PSFS (Ubuntu)
173:     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
174:     steps:
175:       - name: Checkout repository
176:         uses: actions/checkout@v5
177: 
178:       - name: Verify python3
179:         run: python3 --version
180: 
181:       # The portable reference validator needs jsonschema; pyyaml parses the
182:       # frontmatter. --break-system-packages is required on PEP 668 Pythons.
183:       - name: Install jsonschema + pyyaml
184:         run: python3 -m pip install --user --break-system-packages jsonschema pyyaml
185: 
186:       - name: Install bats-core
187:         run: |
188:           sudo apt-get update -y
189:           sudo apt-get install -y bats
190: 
191:       # Bind: the bash reference implementation reports the standard it serves.
192:       - name: Report standard binding
193:         run: scripts/lint-skills.sh --standard
194: 
195:       # Authoritative gate: every real SKILL.md must validate against the
196:       # portable schema. With jsonschema installed, lint-skills.sh runs the
197:       # schema cross-check inline and fails on any structural violation.
198:       # (The tests/standard/ bats suite runs — also with jsonschema installed —
199:       # in the full-suite step of 'Installer + Catalog (Ubuntu)'.)
200:       - name: Lint with schema enforcement
201:         run: scripts/lint-skills.sh skills/
202: 
203:   # Smoke-check on macOS to catch bash 3.2 compatibility quirks.
204:   # Not a merge gate — continue-on-error prevents macOS-only failures
205:   # from blocking PRs authored on Linux/Windows.
206:   lint-macos:
207:     name: Lint Skills (macOS)
208:     runs-on: macos-latest
209:     continue-on-error: true
210:     steps:
211:       - name: Checkout repository
212:         uses: actions/checkout@v5
213: 
214:       # python3 is preinstalled on macos-latest runners.
215:       - name: Verify python3
216:         run: python3 --version
217: 
218:       # PyYAML is not preinstalled on macos-latest (it is on ubuntu-latest).
219:       # Without it, the linter falls back to a degraded check that flags
220:       # every skill — install it so the smoke job mirrors Ubuntu coverage.
221:       # jsonschema keeps the tests/standard/ real-tree gate from SKIPping.
222:       # --break-system-packages is required on PEP 668-managed Pythons.
223:       - name: Install pyyaml + jsonschema
224:         run: python3 -m pip install --user --break-system-packages pyyaml jsonschema
225: 
226:       # brew is preinstalled on macos-latest. The package is 'bats-core'
227:       # (not 'bats') on Homebrew — distinct from the Ubuntu apt package name.
228:       - name: Install bats-core
229:         run: brew install bats-core
230: 
231:       # Same two-pass pattern as Ubuntu: capture report first, gate second.
232:       - name: Run lint (capture JUnit report)
233:         run: scripts/lint-skills.sh --format junit > lint-results.xml || true
234: 
235:       - name: Run lint (enforce pass/fail)
236:         run: scripts/lint-skills.sh skills/
237: 
238:       # Full bats suite on macOS/BSD userland (sed/awk/find quirks, stock
239:       # bash 3.2 at /bin/bash). Non-gating like the rest of this job.
240:       - name: Run full bats suite
241:         run: bash tests/run-all.sh
242: 
243:       - name: Upload lint results
244:         if: always()
245:         uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
246:         with:
247:           name: lint-results-macos
248:           path: lint-results.xml

=== hooks/scripts/qa-gate.sh ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env bash
2: #
3: # qa-gate.sh — Stop / TaskCompleted hook [MARQUEE].
4: #
5: # Locates a QA report, validates it with qa-gate-validate.py, applies the
6: # orchestrator gate rules, and BLOCKS the Stop on a gate failure using Claude
7: # Code's documented Stop-hook contract: print {"decision":"block","reason":...}
8: # to stdout and exit 0 (the human reason also goes to stderr). Allowing prints
9: # no decision and exits 0.
10: #
11: # Report location (contract §3 step 1):
12: #   $ATS_QA_REPORT if set, else first existing of
13: #   ./qa-report.json, coordination/qa-report.json, .claude/qa-report.json
14: #
15: # Missing report (step 2): standard/minimal → warn + allow; strict → block.
16: #
17: # Exit: always 0 (block is signalled via the decision JSON, not the exit code).
18: 
19: set -euo pipefail
20: 
21: HOOK_SCRIPTS_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
22: # shellcheck source=/dev/null
23: for cand in \
24:   "$HOOK_SCRIPTS_DIR/../../scripts/lib/term.sh" \
25:   "$HOOK_SCRIPTS_DIR/../lib/term.sh"; do
26:   [[ -f "$cand" ]] && { . "$cand"; break; }
27: done
28: type ats_warn >/dev/null 2>&1 || ats_warn() { printf '[!!]  %s\n' "$*"; }
29: type ats_ok   >/dev/null 2>&1 || ats_ok()   { printf '[OK]  %s\n' "$*"; }
30: type ats_err  >/dev/null 2>&1 || ats_err()  { printf '[ERR] %s\n' "$*" >&2; }
31: 
32: VALIDATOR="$HOOK_SCRIPTS_DIR/qa-gate-validate.py"
33: 
34: # Drain stdin (Stop hook payload not needed for the gate decision).
35: # Never block on a TTY: a real hook receives its payload via a pipe, so the
36: # drain only applies there; a terminal run (or a bats test with inherited
37: # stdin) must not hang on an open stdin (DV-1).
38: if [[ ! -t 0 ]]; then
39:   cat >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
40: fi
41: 
42: active_profile="${ATS_HOOK_PROFILE:-standard}"
43: case "$active_profile" in
44:   minimal|standard|strict) : ;;
45:   *) active_profile="standard" ;;
46: esac
47: 
48: # emit_block <reason> — print the Stop-hook block decision and a stderr note.
49: emit_block() {
50:   local reason="$1"
51:   # JSON-encode the reason safely via python3 (stdlib).
52:   python3 -c '
53: import json, sys
54: print(json.dumps({"decision": "block", "reason": sys.argv[1]}))
55: ' "$reason"
56:   ats_err "qa-gate: BLOCK — $reason"
57:   exit 0
58: }
59: 
60: # --- Locate the report ---
61: report=""
62: if [[ -n "${ATS_QA_REPORT:-}" ]]; then
63:   report="$ATS_QA_REPORT"
64: else
65:   for cand in "./qa-report.json" "coordination/qa-report.json" ".claude/qa-report.json"; do
66:     if [[ -f "$cand" ]]; then
67:       report="$cand"
68:       break
69:     fi
70:   done
71: fi
72: 
73: # --- Missing report handling ---
74: if [[ -z "$report" || ! -f "$report" ]]; then
75:   if [[ "$active_profile" == "strict" ]]; then
76:     emit_block "no qa-report.json found (strict profile requires one)"
77:   fi
78:   ats_warn "qa-gate: no qa-report.json found — allowing (profile=$active_profile)"
79:   exit 0
80: fi
81: 
82: if [[ ! -f "$VALIDATOR" ]]; then
83:   # Cannot validate — non-critical: allow rather than wedge the harness.
84:   ats_warn "qa-gate: validator not found at $VALIDATOR — allowing"
85:   exit 0
86: fi
87: 
88: # --- Validate + evaluate gate rules ---
89: set +e
90: gate_reason="$(python3 "$VALIDATOR" "$report" 2>/dev/null)"
91: rc=$?
92: set -e
93: 
94: case "$rc" in
95:   0)
96:     ats_ok "qa-gate: report passed the gate — allowing"
97:     exit 0
98:     ;;
99:   1)
100:     emit_block "${gate_reason:-gate rule failed}"
101:     ;;
102:   2)
103:     emit_block "${gate_reason:-report malformed: not conformant}"
104:     ;;
105:   *)
106:     # Unexpected validator failure — allow (non-critical) but note it.
107:     ats_warn "qa-gate: validator exited $rc unexpectedly — allowing"
108:     exit 0
109:     ;;
110: esac

=== hooks/scripts/qa-gate-validate.py ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """qa-gate-validate.py — Structural validator + gate-rule evaluator for QA reports.
3: 
4: Pure, unit-testable. Stdlib only (json) — no third-party jsonschema dependency.
5: Validates the required keys/types of skills/roles/qe-agent/references/qa-report-schema.json
6: structurally, then applies the orchestrator's gate rules.
7: 
8: Usage:
9:     qa-gate-validate.py <report.json>
10: 
11: Exit codes (per contracts/hooks/hooks-layer.md §3):
12:     0  allow   — report is conformant AND no gate rule fires
13:     1  block   — report is conformant but a gate rule fires (reason -> stdout)
14:     2  malformed / not-found — file missing or structurally non-conformant
15:                   (reason -> stdout)
16: """
17: import json
18: import sys
19: 
20: ALLOW = 0
21: BLOCK = 1
22: MALFORMED = 2
23: 
24: 
25: def fail_malformed(reason):
26:     print(reason)
27:     sys.exit(MALFORMED)
28: 
29: 
30: def is_int(v):
31:     # bool is a subclass of int in Python; exclude it for integer fields.
32:     return isinstance(v, int) and not isinstance(v, bool)
33: 
34: 
35: def require_keys(obj, keys, where):
36:     if not isinstance(obj, dict):
37:         fail_malformed("report malformed: %s is not an object" % where)
38:     for k in keys:
39:         if k not in obj:
40:             fail_malformed("report malformed: missing key '%s' in %s" % (k, where))
41: 
42: 
43: def check_score_entry(obj, where):
44:     require_keys(obj, ["score", "notes"], where)
45:     if not is_int(obj["score"]):
46:         fail_malformed("report malformed: %s.score must be an integer" % where)
47:     if not (1 <= obj["score"] <= 5):
48:         fail_malformed("report malformed: %s.score out of range 1..5" % where)
49:     if not isinstance(obj["notes"], str):
50:         fail_malformed("report malformed: %s.notes must be a string" % where)
51: 
52: 
53: def check_test_counts(obj, where):
54:     require_keys(obj, ["pass", "fail", "skip"], where)
55:     for k in ("pass", "fail", "skip"):
56:         if not is_int(obj[k]) or obj[k] < 0:
57:             fail_malformed("report malformed: %s.%s must be a non-negative integer" % (where, k))
58: 
59: 
60: def main():
61:     if len(sys.argv) != 2:
62:         fail_malformed("report malformed: usage qa-gate-validate.py <report.json>")
63: 
64:     path = sys.argv[1]
65:     try:
66:         with open(path) as f:
67:             text = f.read()
68:     except OSError:
69:         fail_malformed("report not found: %s" % path)
70: 
71:     try:
72:         data = json.loads(text)
73:     except ValueError as e:
74:         fail_malformed("report malformed: invalid JSON (%s)" % e)
75: 
76:     if not isinstance(data, dict):
77:         fail_malformed("report malformed: top-level value is not an object")
78: 
79:     # --- Top-level required keys (subset enforced per contract §3) ---
80:     require_keys(
81:         data,
82:         [
83:             "schema_version", "status", "scores", "test_results",
84:             "blockers", "issues", "recommendations", "gate_decision",
85:         ],
86:         "report",
87:     )
88: 
89:     if data["schema_version"] != "1.0.0":
90:         fail_malformed(
91:             "report malformed: schema_version must be '1.0.0' (got %r)"
92:             % data["schema_version"]
93:         )
94: 
95:     if data["status"] not in ("PASS", "FAIL", "PARTIAL", "BLOCKED"):
96:         fail_malformed("report malformed: status %r not in enum" % data["status"])
97: 
98:     # --- scores ---
99:     scores = data["scores"]
100:     require_keys(
101:         scores,
102:         ["correctness", "completeness", "code_quality", "security", "contract_conformance"],
103:         "scores",
104:     )
105:     for k in ("correctness", "completeness", "code_quality", "security", "contract_conformance"):
106:         check_score_entry(scores[k], "scores.%s" % k)
107: 
108:     # --- test_results ---
109:     tr = data["test_results"]
110:     require_keys(tr, ["unit", "integration", "e2e", "contract", "security_scan"], "test_results")
111:     for k in ("unit", "integration", "e2e", "contract", "security_scan"):
112:         check_test_counts(tr[k], "test_results.%s" % k)
113: 
114:     # --- blockers / issues / recommendations must be arrays ---
115:     for k in ("blockers", "issues", "recommendations"):
116:         if not isinstance(data[k], list):
117:             fail_malformed("report malformed: %s must be an array" % k)
118: 
119:     # --- gate_decision ---
120:     gd = data["gate_decision"]
121:     require_keys(gd, ["proceed", "reason"], "gate_decision")
122:     if not isinstance(gd["proceed"], bool):
123:         fail_malformed("report malformed: gate_decision.proceed must be a boolean")
124:     if not isinstance(gd["reason"], str):
125:         fail_malformed("report malformed: gate_decision.reason must be a string")
126: 
127:     # Blockers: each must at least carry a 'severity' string to evaluate the rule.
128:     for i, b in enumerate(data["blockers"]):
129:         if not isinstance(b, dict):
130:             fail_malformed("report malformed: blockers[%d] is not an object" % i)
131:         if "severity" not in b or not isinstance(b["severity"], str):
132:             fail_malformed("report malformed: blockers[%d] missing string 'severity'" % i)
133: 
134:     # --- Gate rules (contract §3 step 4) ---
135:     reasons = []
136:     if gd["proceed"] is False:
137:         reasons.append("gate_decision.proceed is false")
138: 
139:     crit = [b for b in data["blockers"] if b.get("severity") == "CRITICAL"]
140:     if crit:
141:         ids = ", ".join(str(b.get("id", "?")) for b in crit)
142:         reasons.append("CRITICAL blocker(s) present: %s" % ids)
143: 
144:     cc = scores["contract_conformance"]["score"]
145:     if cc < 3:
146:         reasons.append("contract_conformance score %d < 3" % cc)
147: 
148:     sec = scores["security"]["score"]
149:     if sec < 3:
150:         reasons.append("security score %d < 3" % sec)
151: 
152:     if reasons:
153:         print("; ".join(reasons))
154:         sys.exit(BLOCK)
155: 
156:     sys.exit(ALLOW)
157: 
158: 
159: if __name__ == "__main__":
160:     main()

=== .scan-skills-ignore ===
1: # scan-skills false-positive suppressions — "path:rule", path relative to repo root.
2: # Only for KNOWN-safe documentation examples, never to hide a real secret.
3: #
4: # use-freellmapi: its curl smoke tests and per-framework recipes show where a key
5: # goes using the placeholder unified key `freellmapi-xxxx` and idiomatic env-var
6: # reads like `apiKey: process.env.OPENAI_API_KEY`. The secret-generic heuristic
7: # can't distinguish a placeholder / env-var reference from a real literal, so the
8: # suppression is scoped to these two docs-only files. The skill ships no real
9: # credentials (verified: every "key" is a placeholder or an env lookup).
10: skills/workflows/use-freellmapi/SKILL.md:secret-generic
11: skills/workflows/use-freellmapi/references/recipes.md:secret-generic
12: 
13: # payload-cms: config and deploy references show idiomatic env-var reads such as
14: # `secret: process.env.PAYLOAD_SECRET || ''` and `token: process.env.BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN || ''`.
15: # The secret-generic heuristic flags the `process.env.X` expression as a long literal.
16: # Scoped to these two docs-only files; the skill ships no credentials (every value is an env lookup).
17: skills/workflows/payload-cms/references/config-and-collections.md:secret-generic
18: skills/workflows/payload-cms/references/scaffolding-and-deploy.md:secret-generic

=== skills/workflows/sync-skills/scripts/sync-skills.sh ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env bash
2: set -euo pipefail
3: 
4: # Sync skills between this repo and global locations for Claude Code and Cursor.
5: # Default mode is symlink; use --copy for file copies.
6: 
7: REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/../../../.." && pwd)"
8: REPO_SKILLS="$REPO_ROOT/skills"
9: 
10: CLAUDE_SKILLS="$HOME/.claude/skills"
11: CURSOR_SKILLS="$HOME/.cursor/skills-cursor"
12: 
13: # Directories under skills/ that are NOT published — drafts and retired skills.
14: # They are excluded from discovery so they never get symlinked into ~/.claude/skills/.
15: SKIP_CATEGORIES=("archive" "in-progress")
16: 
17: # Returns 0 if $1 is a skipped category, 1 otherwise.
18: _is_skipped_category() {
19:   local name="$1"
20:   for skip in "${SKIP_CATEGORIES[@]}"; do
21:     [[ "$name" == "$skip" ]] && return 0
22:   done
23:   return 1
24: }
25: 
26: # Skill categories in the repo (auto-discovered)
27: discover_categories() {
28:   local cats=()
29:   for dir in "$REPO_SKILLS"/*/; do
30:     [[ -d "$dir" ]] || continue
31:     local name
32:     name="$(basename "$dir")"
33:     _is_skipped_category "$name" && continue
34:     cats+=("$name")
35:   done
36:   echo "${cats[@]+"${cats[@]}"}"
37: }
38: 
39: # Discover individual skills within all categories
40: # Returns lines of "category/skill-name" where skill-name is a subdirectory with SKILL.md
41: # For categories that ARE skills themselves (SKILL.md at category root), returns "category/."
42: discover_skills() {
43:   for cat_dir in "$REPO_SKILLS"/*/; do
44:     [[ -d "$cat_dir" ]] || continue
45:     local cat
46:     cat="$(basename "$cat_dir")"
47:     _is_skipped_category "$cat" && continue
48: 
49:     # Check for skill subdirectories (e.g., meta/skill-review/SKILL.md)
50:     local has_subdirs=""
51:     for skill_dir in "$cat_dir"/*/; do
52:       [[ -d "$skill_dir" && -f "$skill_dir/SKILL.md" ]] || continue
53:       local skill
54:       skill="$(basename "$skill_dir")"
55:       echo "$cat/$skill"
56:       has_subdirs="yes"
57:     done
58: 
59:     # If no skill subdirs but category itself has SKILL.md, treat category as the skill
60:     if [[ -z "$has_subdirs" && -f "$cat_dir/SKILL.md" ]]; then
61:       echo "$cat/."
62:     fi
63:   done
64: }
65: 
66: usage() {
67:   cat <<EOF
68: Usage: $(basename "$0") [OPTIONS] [category-or-skill-name ...]
69: 
70: Sync skills between this repo and global locations using symlinks (default) or copies.
71: 
72: Modes:
73:   --link            Symlink repo categories to global locations (default for --to-*)
74:   --copy            Copy files instead of symlinking
75:   --unlink          Remove symlinks pointing to this repo
76: 
77: Directions (at least one required, unless --status):
78:   --to-cursor       Target ~/.cursor/skills-cursor/
79:   --to-claude       Target ~/.claude/skills/
80:   --to-all          Target both
81:   --from-cursor     Pull from Cursor into repo
82:   --from-claude     Pull from Claude Code into repo
83:   --from-all        Pull from both
84: 
85: Options:
86:   --status          Show link/copy/missing status for all locations
87:   --clean           Remove broken symlinks from global locations
88:   --dry-run         Preview without making changes
89:   -h, --help        Show this help
90: 
91: Examples:
92:   $(basename "$0") --link --to-all                    # Symlink all categories everywhere
93:   $(basename "$0") --status                           # What's linked, copied, missing?
94:   $(basename "$0") --clean                            # Remove broken symlinks
95:   $(basename "$0") --copy --to-claude meta            # Copy just meta/ to Claude Code
96:   $(basename "$0") --unlink --to-all                  # Remove all symlinks
97:   $(basename "$0") --from-cursor shell                # Pull a Cursor skill into repo
98:   $(basename "$0") --dry-run --link --to-all          # Preview linking
99: EOF
100:   exit 0
101: }
102: 
103: # --- Parse args ---
104: 
105: MODE=""  # link, copy, unlink
106: TO_CURSOR="" TO_CLAUDE=""
107: FROM_CURSOR="" FROM_CLAUDE=""
108: DRY_RUN="" STATUS_MODE="" CLEAN_MODE=""
109: TARGETS=()
110: 
111: while [[ $# -gt 0 ]]; do
112:   case "$1" in
113:     --link)        MODE="link"; shift ;;
114:     --copy)        MODE="copy"; shift ;;
115:     --unlink)      MODE="unlink"; shift ;;
116:     --to-cursor)   TO_CURSOR="yes"; shift ;;
117:     --to-claude)   TO_CLAUDE="yes"; shift ;;
118:     --to-all)      TO_CURSOR="yes"; TO_CLAUDE="yes"; shift ;;
119:     --from-cursor) FROM_CURSOR="yes"; shift ;;
120:     --from-claude) FROM_CLAUDE="yes"; shift ;;
121:     --from-all)    FROM_CURSOR="yes"; FROM_CLAUDE="yes"; shift ;;
122:     --status)      STATUS_MODE="yes"; shift ;;
123:     --clean)       CLEAN_MODE="yes"; shift ;;
124:     --dry-run)     DRY_RUN="yes"; shift ;;
125:     -h|--help)     usage ;;
126:     -*)            echo "Unknown option: $1" >&2; usage ;;
127:     *)             TARGETS+=("$1"); shift ;;
128:   esac
129: done
130: 
131: # Default mode for --to-* is link
132: if [[ -z "$MODE" && ( -n "$TO_CURSOR" || -n "$TO_CLAUDE" ) ]]; then
133:   MODE="link"
134: fi
135: 
136: # --- Status mode ---
137: 
138: # Status for Claude Code (flattened — individual skills)
139: check_status_claude() {
140:   local global_dir="$1"
141:   echo ""
142:   echo "=== Claude Code ($global_dir) — flattened ==="
143: 
144:   if [[ ! -d "$global_dir" ]]; then
145:     echo "  (directory does not exist)"
146:     return
147:   fi
148: 
149:   # Build list of expected skill names
150:   local skills
151:   skills=($(discover_skills))
152:   local skill_names=()
153:   local broken=0
154: 
155:   for entry in "${skills[@]}"; do
156:     local cat="${entry%%/*}"
157:     local skill="${entry##*/}"
158:     local name target expected_src display_cat
159:     if [[ "$skill" == "." ]]; then
160:       name="$cat"; target="$global_dir/$cat"; expected_src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat"; display_cat="$cat"
161:     else
162:       name="$skill"; target="$global_dir/$skill"; expected_src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat/$skill"; display_cat="$cat/$skill"
163:     fi
164:     skill_names+=("$name")
165: 
166:     if [[ -L "$target" ]]; then
167:       local link_target
168:       link_target="$(readlink "$target")"
169:       if [[ ! -e "$target" ]]; then
170:         printf "  %-30s ✗ BROKEN → %s\n" "$name" "$link_target"
171:         broken=$((broken + 1))
172:       elif [[ "$link_target" == "$expected_src" || "$link_target" == "${expected_src}/" ]]; then
173:         printf "  %-30s ✓ linked → %s\n" "$name" "$display_cat"
174:       else
175:         printf "  %-30s ⚠ linked → %s (unexpected)\n" "$name" "$link_target"
176:       fi
177:     elif [[ -d "$target" ]]; then
178:       printf "  %-30s ● copied (independent)\n" "$name"
179:     else
180:       printf "  %-30s ○ not present\n" "$name"
181:     fi
182:   done
183: 
184:   # Check for old category-level symlinks that should be cleaned up
185:   local categories
186:   categories=($(discover_categories))
187:   for cat in "${categories[@]}"; do
188:     # Skip if this category is also a skill name (e.g., orchestrator)
189:     local is_skill=""
190:     for s in "${skill_names[@]}"; do
191:       [[ "$cat" == "$s" ]] && is_skill="yes" && break
192:     done
193:     [[ "$is_skill" == "yes" ]] && continue
194: 
195:     local target="$global_dir/$cat"
196:     if [[ -L "$target" || -d "$target" ]]; then
197:       printf "  %-30s ⚠ old category-level entry (should remove)\n" "$cat/"
198:     fi
199:   done
200: 
201:   # Check non-repo items
202:   for item in "$global_dir"/*/; do
203:     [[ -d "$item" || -L "${item%/}" ]] || continue
