204:     local name
205:     name="$(basename "$item")"
206:     local is_repo=""
207:     for s in "${skill_names[@]}"; do
208:       [[ "$name" == "$s" ]] && is_repo="yes" && break
209:     done
210:     for cat in "${categories[@]}"; do
211:       [[ "$name" == "$cat" ]] && is_repo="yes" && break
212:     done
213:     if [[ -z "$is_repo" ]]; then
214:       printf "  %-30s · non-repo skill\n" "$name/"
215:     fi
216:   done
217: 
218:   [[ $broken -gt 0 ]] && echo "  ($broken broken symlink(s) — run --clean to remove)"
219:   return 0
220: }
221: 
222: # Status for Cursor (category-level)
223: check_status_cursor() {
224:   local global_dir="$1"
225:   echo ""
226:   echo "=== Cursor ($global_dir) — category-level ==="
227: 
228:   if [[ ! -d "$global_dir" ]]; then
229:     echo "  (directory does not exist)"
230:     return
231:   fi
232: 
233:   local categories
234:   categories=($(discover_categories))
235:   local broken=0
236: 
237:   for cat in "${categories[@]}"; do
238:     local target="$global_dir/$cat"
239:     if [[ -L "$target" ]]; then
240:       local link_target
241:       link_target="$(readlink "$target")"
242:       if [[ ! -e "$target" ]]; then
243:         printf "  %-20s ✗ BROKEN → %s\n" "$cat/" "$link_target"
244:         broken=$((broken + 1))
245:       elif [[ "$link_target" == "$REPO_SKILLS/$cat" || "$link_target" == "${REPO_SKILLS}/${cat}/" ]]; then
246:         printf "  %-20s ✓ linked → %s\n" "$cat/" "$link_target"
247:       else
248:         printf "  %-20s ⚠ linked → %s (different repo!)\n" "$cat/" "$link_target"
249:       fi
250:     elif [[ -d "$target" ]]; then
251:       printf "  %-20s ● copied (independent)\n" "$cat/"
252:     else
253:       printf "  %-20s ○ not present\n" "$cat/"
254:     fi
255:   done
256: 
257:   # Check non-repo items
258:   for item in "$global_dir"/*/; do
259:     [[ -d "$item" ]] || continue
260:     local name
261:     name="$(basename "$item")"
262:     local is_repo_cat=""
263:     for cat in "${categories[@]}"; do
264:       [[ "$name" == "$cat" ]] && is_repo_cat="yes" && break
265:     done
266:     if [[ -z "$is_repo_cat" ]]; then
267:       printf "  %-20s · non-repo skill\n" "$name/"
268:     fi
269:   done
270: 
271:   [[ $broken -gt 0 ]] && echo "  ($broken broken symlink(s) — run --clean to remove)"
272:   return 0
273: }
274: 
275: if [[ "$STATUS_MODE" == "yes" ]]; then
276:   echo "Repo skills: $REPO_SKILLS"
277:   echo "Categories: $(discover_categories)"
278:   check_status_claude "$CLAUDE_SKILLS"
279:   check_status_cursor "$CURSOR_SKILLS"
280:   exit 0
281: fi
282: 
283: # --- Clean mode ---
284: 
285: clean_broken() {
286:   local global_dir="$1" label="$2"
287:   echo ""
288:   echo "--- Cleaning broken symlinks in $label ($global_dir) ---"
289: 
290:   if [[ ! -d "$global_dir" ]]; then
291:     echo "  (directory does not exist)"
292:     return
293:   fi
294: 
295:   local cleaned=0
296:   for target in "$global_dir"/*; do
297:     [[ -L "$target" ]] || continue
298:     if [[ ! -e "$target" ]]; then
299:       local link_target
300:       link_target="$(readlink "$target")"
301:       if [[ "$DRY_RUN" == "yes" ]]; then
302:         echo "  [dry-run] Would remove broken link: $(basename "$target") → $link_target"
303:       else
304:         rm "$target"
305:         echo "  Removed broken link: $(basename "$target") → $link_target"
306:       fi
307:       cleaned=$((cleaned + 1))
308:     fi
309:   done
310: 
311:   [[ $cleaned -eq 0 ]] && echo "  No broken symlinks found"
312: }
313: 
314: if [[ "$CLEAN_MODE" == "yes" ]]; then
315:   clean_broken "$CLAUDE_SKILLS" "Claude Code"
316:   clean_broken "$CURSOR_SKILLS" "Cursor"
317:   echo ""
318:   echo "Done."
319:   exit 0
320: fi
321: 
322: # --- Validate args ---
323: 
324: if [[ -z "$TO_CURSOR" && -z "$TO_CLAUDE" && -z "$FROM_CURSOR" && -z "$FROM_CLAUDE" ]]; then
325:   echo "Error: specify a direction (--to-claude, --to-cursor, --to-all, --from-claude, --from-cursor, --from-all) or --status / --clean." >&2
326:   echo "Run with -h for help." >&2
327:   exit 1
328: fi
329: 
330: if [[ -n "$FROM_CURSOR" || -n "$FROM_CLAUDE" ]] && [[ "$MODE" == "link" || "$MODE" == "unlink" ]]; then
331:   echo "Error: --from-* only supports --copy mode (pulling into repo)." >&2
332:   exit 1
333: fi
334: 
335: # --- Resolve targets ---
336: # Targets can be category names (meta, roles) or individual skill names within categories.
337: # If no targets given, operate on all categories.
338: 
339: resolve_repo_categories() {
340:   if [[ ${#TARGETS[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
341:     discover_categories
342:   else
343:     # Check if targets are categories or individual skills
344:     for t in "${TARGETS[@]}"; do
345:       if [[ -d "$REPO_SKILLS/$t" ]]; then
346:         echo "$t"
347:       else
348:         # Search for skill name within categories
349:         for cat_dir in "$REPO_SKILLS"/*/; do
350:           [[ -d "$cat_dir" ]] || continue
351:           if [[ -d "$cat_dir/$t" ]]; then
352:             echo "$(basename "$cat_dir")"
353:             break
354:           fi
355:         done
356:       fi
357:     done | sort -u
358:   fi
359: }
360: 
361: # --- Link functions ---
362: 
363: # Link a single symlink: src → dst
364: _do_link() {
365:   local name="$1" src="$2" dst="$3" label="$4"
366: 
367:   if [[ ! -d "$src" ]]; then
368:     echo "  [$name] Not found in repo — skipping"
369:     return
370:   fi
371: 
372:   # Already correctly linked
373:   if [[ -L "$dst" ]]; then
374:     local existing
375:     existing="$(readlink "$dst")"
376:     if [[ "$existing" == "$src" || "$existing" == "${src}/" ]]; then
377:       echo "  [$name] Already linked ✓"
378:       return
379:     else
380:       if [[ "$DRY_RUN" == "yes" ]]; then
381:         echo "  [dry-run] Would relink: $dst (currently → $existing)"
382:         return
383:       fi
384:       echo "  [$name] Relinking (was → $existing)"
385:       rm "$dst"
386:     fi
387:   elif [[ -d "$dst" ]]; then
388:     if [[ "$DRY_RUN" == "yes" ]]; then
389:       echo "  [dry-run] Would replace copy with link: $dst → $src"
390:       return
391:     fi
392:     echo "  [$name] Replacing existing copy with symlink"
393:     rm -rf "$dst"
394:   else
395:     if [[ "$DRY_RUN" == "yes" ]]; then
396:       echo "  [dry-run] Would link: $dst → $src"
397:       return
398:     fi
399:   fi
400: 
401:   mkdir -p "$(dirname "$dst")"
402:   ln -s "$src" "$dst"
403:   echo "  [$name] Linked → $label"
404: }
405: 
406: link_category() {
407:   local cat="$1" global_dir="$2" label="$3"
408:   _do_link "$cat" "$REPO_SKILLS/$cat" "$global_dir/$cat" "$label"
409: }
410: 
411: # Link individual skills directly (flattened — no category subdirs)
412: link_skills_flat() {
413:   local global_dir="$1" label="$2"
414:   local skills
415:   skills=($(discover_skills))
416: 
417:   for entry in "${skills[@]}"; do
418:     local cat="${entry%%/*}"
419:     local skill="${entry##*/}"
420:     local src dst name
421:     if [[ "$skill" == "." ]]; then
422:       # Category IS the skill (e.g., orchestrator/SKILL.md)
423:       src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat"
424:       dst="$global_dir/$cat"
425:       name="$cat"
426:     else
427:       src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat/$skill"
428:       dst="$global_dir/$skill"
429:       name="$skill"
430:     fi
431:     _do_link "$name" "$src" "$dst" "$label"
432:   done
433: }
434: 
435: # --- Copy functions ---
436: 
437: _do_copy() {
438:   local name="$1" src="$2" dst="$3" label="$4"
439: 
440:   if [[ ! -d "$src" ]]; then
441:     echo "  [$name] Not found in repo — skipping"
442:     return
443:   fi
444: 
445:   if [[ "$DRY_RUN" == "yes" ]]; then
446:     echo "  [dry-run] Would copy: $src → $dst"
447:     return
448:   fi
449: 
450:   # Remove existing symlink if present (replacing link with copy)
451:   if [[ -L "$dst" ]]; then
452:     echo "  [$name] Replacing symlink with copy"
453:     rm "$dst"
454:   fi
455: 
456:   mkdir -p "$dst"
457:   if command -v rsync &>/dev/null; then
458:     rsync -a --delete "$src/" "$dst/"
459:   else
460:     rm -rf "$dst"
461:     cp -R "$src" "$dst"
462:   fi
463:   echo "  [$name] Copied → $label"
464: }
465: 
466: copy_category() {
467:   local cat="$1" global_dir="$2" label="$3"
468:   _do_copy "$cat" "$REPO_SKILLS/$cat" "$global_dir/$cat" "$label"
469: }
470: 
471: copy_skills_flat() {
472:   local global_dir="$1" label="$2"
473:   local skills
474:   skills=($(discover_skills))
475: 
476:   for entry in "${skills[@]}"; do
477:     local cat="${entry%%/*}"
478:     local skill="${entry##*/}"
479:     local src dst name
480:     if [[ "$skill" == "." ]]; then
481:       src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat"; dst="$global_dir/$cat"; name="$cat"
482:     else
483:       src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat/$skill"; dst="$global_dir/$skill"; name="$skill"
484:     fi
485:     _do_copy "$name" "$src" "$dst" "$label"
486:   done
487: }
488: 
489: # --- Unlink functions ---
490: 
491: _do_unlink() {
492:   local name="$1" expected_target="$2" dst="$3" label="$4"
493: 
494:   if [[ -L "$dst" ]]; then
495:     local link_target
496:     link_target="$(readlink "$dst")"
497:     if [[ "$link_target" == "$expected_target" ]]; then
498:       if [[ "$DRY_RUN" == "yes" ]]; then
499:         echo "  [dry-run] Would unlink: $dst"
500:         return
501:       fi
502:       rm "$dst"
503:       echo "  [$name] Unlinked from $label"
504:     else
505:       echo "  [$name] Symlink points elsewhere ($link_target) — skipping"
506:     fi
507:   elif [[ -d "$dst" ]]; then
508:     echo "  [$name] Not a symlink (is a copy) — skipping"
509:   else
510:     echo "  [$name] Not present — nothing to unlink"
511:   fi
512: }
513: 
514: unlink_category() {
515:   local cat="$1" global_dir="$2" label="$3"
516:   _do_unlink "$cat" "$REPO_SKILLS/$cat" "$global_dir/$cat" "$label"
517: }
518: 
519: unlink_skills_flat() {
520:   local global_dir="$1" label="$2"
521:   local skills
522:   skills=($(discover_skills))
523: 
524:   for entry in "${skills[@]}"; do
525:     local cat="${entry%%/*}"
526:     local skill="${entry##*/}"
527:     local src dst name
528:     if [[ "$skill" == "." ]]; then
529:       src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat"; dst="$global_dir/$cat"; name="$cat"
530:     else
531:       src="$REPO_SKILLS/$cat/$skill"; dst="$global_dir/$skill"; name="$skill"
532:     fi
533:     _do_unlink "$name" "$src" "$dst" "$label"
534:   done
535: }
536: 
537: # --- Pull functions ---
538: 
539: pull_skill() {
540:   local src="$1" dst="$2" name="$3"
541: 
542:   if [[ ! -d "$src" ]]; then
543:     echo "  [$name] Not found — skipping"
544:     return
545:   fi
546: 
547:   if [[ ! -f "$src/SKILL.md" ]]; then
548:     echo "  [$name] No SKILL.md — skipping (not a skill)"
549:     return
550:   fi
551: 
552:   if [[ "$DRY_RUN" == "yes" ]]; then
553:     echo "  [dry-run] Would pull: $src → $dst"
554:     return
555:   fi
556: 
557:   mkdir -p "$dst"
558:   if command -v rsync &>/dev/null; then
559:     rsync -a --delete "$src/" "$dst/"
560:   else
561:     rm -rf "$dst"
562:     cp -R "$src" "$dst"
563:   fi
564:   echo "  [$name] Pulled into repo"
565: }
566: 
567: pull_from() {
568:   local global_dir="$1" label="$2"
569: 
570:   echo ""
571:   echo "--- Pulling from $label ---"
572: 
573:   if [[ ${#TARGETS[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
574:     # Pull all skills found (skip symlinks pointing back to repo)
575:     for item in "$global_dir"/*/; do
576:       [[ -d "$item" ]] || continue
577:       local name
578:       name="$(basename "$item")"
579: 
580:       # If it's a symlink to our repo, skip
581:       if [[ -L "$item" ]]; then
582:         local lt
583:         lt="$(readlink "$item")"
584:         if [[ "$lt" == "$REPO_SKILLS/"* ]]; then
585:           echo "  [$name] Symlink to this repo — skipping"
586:           continue
587:         fi
588:       fi
589: 
590:       # If it contains SKILL.md directly, it's a skill
591:       if [[ -f "$item/SKILL.md" ]]; then
592:         pull_skill "$item" "$REPO_SKILLS/$name" "$name"
593:       else
594:         # Category directory — pull individual skills within
595:         for skill_dir in "$item"/*/; do
596:           [[ -d "$skill_dir" ]] || continue
597:           local skill_name
598:           skill_name="$(basename "$skill_dir")"
599:           [[ -f "$skill_dir/SKILL.md" ]] || continue
600:           pull_skill "$skill_dir" "$REPO_SKILLS/$name/$skill_name" "$name/$skill_name"
601:         done
602:       fi
603:     done
604:   else
605:     for name in "${TARGETS[@]}"; do
606:       # Search for the skill in global dir
607:       if [[ -d "$global_dir/$name" ]]; then
608:         pull_skill "$global_dir/$name" "$REPO_SKILLS/$name" "$name"
609:       else
610:         # Search within category dirs
611:         local found=""
612:         for cat_dir in "$global_dir"/*/; do
613:           [[ -d "$cat_dir" ]] || continue
614:           if [[ -d "$cat_dir/$name" && -f "$cat_dir/$name/SKILL.md" ]]; then
615:             local cat
616:             cat="$(basename "$cat_dir")"
617:             pull_skill "$cat_dir/$name" "$REPO_SKILLS/$cat/$name" "$cat/$name"
618:             found="yes"
619:             break
620:           fi
621:         done
622:         [[ -z "$found" ]] && echo "  [$name] Not found in $label — skipping"
623:       fi
624:     done
625:   fi
626: }
627: 
628: # --- Execute ---
629: 
630: # Claude Code: flatten skills (no category subdirs) so each skill is at ~/.claude/skills/<skill>/SKILL.md
631: sync_to_claude() {
632:   local mode_label
633:   mode_label="$(echo "$MODE" | awk '{print toupper(substr($0,1,1)) substr($0,2)}')"
634:   echo ""
635:   echo "--- ${mode_label}ing to Claude Code (flattened) ---"
636: 
637:   case "$MODE" in
638:     link)   link_skills_flat "$CLAUDE_SKILLS" "Claude Code" ;;
639:     copy)   copy_skills_flat "$CLAUDE_SKILLS" "Claude Code" ;;
640:     unlink) unlink_skills_flat "$CLAUDE_SKILLS" "Claude Code" ;;
641:   esac
642: }
643: 
644: # Cursor: keep category-level structure
645: sync_to_cursor() {
646:   local categories
647:   categories=($(resolve_repo_categories))
648: 
649:   local mode_label
650:   mode_label="$(echo "$MODE" | awk '{print toupper(substr($0,1,1)) substr($0,2)}')"
651:   echo ""
652:   echo "--- ${mode_label}ing to Cursor ---"
653: 
654:   for cat in "${categories[@]}"; do
655:     case "$MODE" in
656:       link)   link_category "$cat" "$CURSOR_SKILLS" "Cursor" ;;
657:       copy)   copy_category "$cat" "$CURSOR_SKILLS" "Cursor" ;;
658:       unlink) unlink_category "$cat" "$CURSOR_SKILLS" "Cursor" ;;
659:     esac
660:   done
661: }
662: 
663: [[ "$TO_CLAUDE" == "yes" ]]   && sync_to_claude
664: [[ "$TO_CURSOR" == "yes" ]]   && sync_to_cursor
665: [[ "$FROM_CURSOR" == "yes" ]] && pull_from "$CURSOR_SKILLS" "Cursor"
666: [[ "$FROM_CLAUDE" == "yes" ]] && pull_from "$CLAUDE_SKILLS" "Claude Code"
667: 
668: echo ""
669: echo "Done."

=== skills/orchestrator/references/phase-guide.md ===
1: # 14-Phase Build Playbook
2: 
3: ## Phase 0: External Services Audit (MANDATORY when integrating with existing services)
4: 
5: **Before reading the plan**, if the build integrates with any existing external service (auth server, API gateway, OAuth provider, payment processor, third-party backend), do this first:
6: 
7: 1. **Read the Terraform / deployment config** for that service — not just the code, the *infrastructure*. Look for:
8:    - Allowed origins / redirect URIs / CORS whitelists
9:    - Environment variables set on the running service (Cloud Run, ECS, Heroku, etc.)
10:    - Firewall rules, VPC config, IAM permissions
11:    - Any hardcoded values that differ from `.env.example` or docs
12: 2. **Identify every constraint the running service imposes** on consumers — ports, domains, scopes, rate limits
13: 3. **Record these as hard constraints in the contracts** before any agent is spawned. These are non-negotiable facts about the real world, not design decisions.
14: 4. **If a constraint blocks the plan** (e.g., the auth server doesn't allow localhost callbacks), flag it to the user BEFORE writing contracts and BEFORE spawning any agents. Do not proceed around it silently.
15: 
16: > **Why this phase exists**: The most common integration failure is building against documentation or code while the actual *running* service has different configuration. A Terraform file telling you `ALLOWED_REDIRECT_ORIGINS=https://*.example.com` with no localhost entries will break every local dev OAuth flow — and no amount of correct application code will fix it. Read the infra first.
17: 
18: ## Phase 1: Read and Analyze the Plan
19: 
20: Read the plan document. Extract:
21: 
22: - **What** are we building? (product, features, acceptance criteria)
23: - **Components**: Major layers (frontend, backend, database, infra, workers)
24: - **Technologies**: Languages, frameworks, tools
25: - **Shared data models**: Entities referenced by multiple components
26: - **Dependency graph**: What must exist before something else can be built
27: - **Validation criteria**: How we know it works (tests, curl commands, UI flows)
28: - **Architecture visualization** — use the mermaid-charts skill to generate an architecture overview diagram showing major components, their layers, and dependency flow. This visual anchor guides the rest of the build and is included in final documentation.
29: 
30: ## Phase 2: Size the Team
31: 
32: See `references/team-sizing.md` for the full decision framework.
33: 
34: **Size the team based on the work.** Consider:
35: 
36: - How many components can be built in parallel?
37: - Do they have clear ownership boundaries (no shared files)?
38: - Can the orchestrator manage context for this many agents? (Use handoffs and phased spawning for larger teams)
39: 
40: ## Phase 3: Define Agents
41: 
42: Before defining agent boundaries, apply the **assumption-audit** move (`skills/workflows/deployment-checklist/references/assumption-audit.md`) to surface any unverified beliefs about the plan's environment, data, and constraints. Catching a bad assumption here is far cheaper than after agents have built against it.
43: 
44: For each agent:
45: 
46: 1. Name (short, descriptive)
47: 2. Exact file/directory ownership
48: 3. Off-limits boundaries
49: 4. Concrete responsibilities
50: 5. Validation commands
51: 
52: Assign every shared infrastructure file to exactly one agent.
53: 
54: ## Phase 4: Author Integration Contracts
55: 
56: **This is the most critical phase.** Contracts prevent the ~42% of multi-agent failures caused by specification problems.
57: 
58: Order:
59: 
60: 1. **Shared types first** — single source of truth for all entities
61: 2. **API contract** — URLs, methods, request/response shapes, status codes, SSE events
62: 3. **Data layer contract** — function signatures, storage semantics, indexes
63: 4. **Cross-cutting concerns** — assign each to exactly one agent
64: 
65: Use the contract-author skill and templates in `skills/contracts/contract-author/references/`.
66: 
67: For monorepos where ≥2 implementation agents will each write a package manifest (`package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `Cargo.toml`), also invoke the `dependency-coordinator` skill in this same phase. It authors the cross-package dependency contract so parallel agents don't produce transitive version drift that breaks `install` after the wave completes.
68: 
69: Quality checklist (all must pass):
70: 
71: - URLs are exact (method + path)
72: - Response shapes are explicit JSON
73: - All status codes specified (success AND error)
74: - Trailing slash convention stated
75: - Error envelope defined
76: - CORS origin specified — and verified against the **actual running Terraform/deployment config**, not just docs
77: - For OAuth/auth integrations: allowed redirect origins verified in the **running** service config (not just `.env.example`)
78: - Every external service constraint from Phase 0 is explicitly reflected in the contract
79: - Every contract versioned as v1
80: 
81: ## Phase 5: Distill Agent Prompts
82: 
83: **Do NOT paste the full plan into every agent's prompt.** Each agent receives only:
84: 
85: 1. Their ownership scope and boundaries
86: 2. Shared types file (or path)
87: 3. Contracts they produce (implement exactly)
88: 4. Contracts they consume (build against exactly)
89: 5. Cross-cutting concerns they own
90: 6. Relevant plan excerpt only
91: 7. Validation checklist
92: 8. Coordination rules
93: 
94: ## Phase 6: Pre-Create Scaffolding
95: 
96: Before spawning implementation agents:
97: 
98: - **Create a feature branch** — `git checkout -b build/<project-name>`. All work happens here, never on main. Do not merge to main unless the user explicitly requests it.
99: - Follow git-commit conventions for branch naming and commit messages throughout the build
100: - Create `.gitignore` (orchestrator-owned)
101: - Invoke `contract-author` skill to create `contracts/` with shared types and integration contracts — contract-author owns this directory
102: - Create any skeleton files needed for agent orientation (assign ownership per the canonical table in `skills/orchestrator/SKILL.md` § File Ownership Map)
103: 
104: ## Phase 7: Detect Runtime and Spawn
105: 
106: Use the runtime detection tree in `skills/orchestrator/SKILL.md` § Runtime Detection (the canonical version — kept there because it's always loaded). Once the runtime is chosen, spawn all implementation agents simultaneously with their distilled prompts.
107: 
108: ## Phase 8: Active Coordination
109: 
110: While agents work:
111: 
112: - Relay inter-agent messages (agents can't talk directly)
113: - Manage contract change requests (pause → update → version → notify → confirm)
114: - Handle shared file change requests
115: - Track progress
116: - Apply circuit breaker if needed (see `references/circuit-breaker.md`)
117: 
118: ### Contract Change Protocol
119: 
120: 1. Tell requesting agent to STOP work on affected interface
121: 2. Evaluate the change and which agents it affects
122: 3. Write updated contract with incremented version (v1 → v2)
123: 4. Send full updated contract to ALL affected agents
124: 5. Wait for acknowledgment from each
125: 6. Log the change in the contract changelog
126: 
127: ## Phase 9: Contract Diff
128: 
129: Before any agent reports "done":
130: 
131: 1. Get backend's exact curl commands for each endpoint
132: 2. Get frontend's exact API call URLs/methods/bodies
133: 3. Compare line by line — URLs, request bodies, response shapes, error handling
134: 4. Flag and resolve ALL mismatches
135: 
136: ## Phase 10: Agent Validation
137: 
138: Each agent runs their domain-specific validation checklist:
139: 
140: - Backend: server starts, endpoints respond correctly, CORS headers present
141: - Frontend: TypeScript compiles, build succeeds, dev server loads, zero CORS errors
142: - Infrastructure: Docker builds, services healthy
143: - Data layer: schema correct, CRUD works, cascades work
144: - **External service integration**: For every external service integrated, verify the running service config actually accepts requests from the app (test the actual OAuth redirect, API call, webhook, etc.) — do not assume it works because the code is correct
145: 
146: ## Phase 11: Smoke Testing (Orchestrator)
147: 
148: You (the orchestrator) run quick smoke tests to verify integration before spawning QE. This is a fast sanity check, NOT a thorough test suite:
149: 
150: 1. **Startup**: All services start, connect, no errors
151: 2. **Happy path**: One primary flow works end-to-end
152: 3. **Data flow**: Verify one write is visible via read (Frontend → Backend → Database → Backend → Frontend)
153: 
154: For browser-based flows (auth, form submit, navigation), invoke the `playwright` skill to drive a real browser through the happy path — this catches CORS, redirect, and rendering issues that curl-only smoke tests miss. The QE agent will rerun and extend these in Phase 13.
155: 
156: If smoke tests fail, fix integration issues before wasting QE agent context on a broken build. If they pass, spawn QE for thorough verification.
157: 
158: ## Phase 12: Fix Failures
159: 
160: - **Single-agent bug** (contract correct, implementation wrong): Re-spawn that agent with specific error + expected behavior
161: - **Contract bug** (contract itself was wrong): Follow Contract Change Protocol, increment version, re-spawn affected agents
162: - **Cascading failure**: Stop all agents, rewrite affected contracts, rebuild in dependency order (data → backend → frontend)
163: 
164: ## Phase 13: QA Gate
165: 
166: Spawn the QE agent for thorough verification. QE handles contract conformance, integration testing, adversarial probing, and produces `qa-report.json` per the schema in `skills/roles/qe-agent/references/qa-report-schema.json`.
167: 
168: Before parsing scores, validate the report conforms to the schema (scores are `{score, notes}` objects, all required fields present). Send non-conformant reports back to QE for correction.
169: 
170: Gate rules:
171: 
172: - `gate_decision.proceed = false` blocks the build
173: - Blocked when: any CRITICAL blocker, `contract_conformance.score < 3`, `security.score < 3`
174: - The orchestrator does NOT override the QE gate
175: 
176: ### Optional build loops — dispatch when the mission calls for them
177: 
178: All four are `loop-controller` configs and `disable-model-invocation: true`: they run only when you dispatch them, and the **mission text** is the trigger — never a failing check on its own.
179: 
180: | Mission signal | Dispatch | When |
181: |---|---|---|
182: | A component must *converge on its authored contract* (build-until-spec) rather than one-shot it | `contract-conformance-loop` | Wraps that component's build (Phases 8–13); its fresh-context contract-auditor verdict feeds this QA gate |
183: | A test-coverage target is set ("≥80% on the backend") | `coverage-loop` | After the QA gate passes — grow the suite to the target without gaming the metric |
184: | A performance budget is set (p95 latency, bundle size, load time) | `perf-loop` | After the QA gate passes — profile → optimize → re-benchmark under repeatable conditions, no functional regression |
185: | The plan contains an enumerated wide-refactor / transform set ("migrate all N call sites / files") | `migration-loop` | Plan it as its own work stream at Phases 2–3 — one file per iteration until the set is empty and the suite is green |
186: 
187: `fix-until-green` (the wave/QA fix cycle) and `orchestrator-task-loop` (the Agent Teams outer loop) are already wired into the phases above; these four are the mission-conditional ones.
188: 
189: **Decomposing a wide-blast-radius refactor (expand–contract).** When one mechanical change would break thousands of call sites at once (rename a column, retype a shared symbol), don't plan it as a single vertical slice — decompose it into `migration-loop`'s **expand → migrate → contract** mode: land the new form beside the old (nothing breaks), migrate call sites in blast-radius-sized batches (each green because the old form still exists), then delete the old form once no caller remains. Plan the three stages as ordered tasks with the expand as a blocking dependency of every migrate batch; when batches can't stay green alone, fall back to a shared integration branch. See `migration-loop` § *Expand–contract mode*.
190: 
191: ## Phase 14: Post-Build
192: 
193: 1. Spawn docs-agent to write README.md — provide full-system context (architecture summary, how to run, directory map). Docs-agent owns README.md.
194: 2. Generate final architecture diagram(s) using mermaid-charts — system overview, data flow, and deployment topology as appropriate. Include in README or `docs/`.
195: 3. Clean up any temporary files
196: 4. Verify the plan's acceptance criteria are met
197: 5. If the build will go through PR review, hand off to the `git-pr` and `git-pr-feedback` skills for the review cycle — they own commit/PR conventions and the response-to-review loop.
198: 6. Produce final status report
199: 
200: ## Definition of Done
201: 
202: The canonical DoD lives in `skills/orchestrator/SKILL.md` § Definition of Done. Use that — it's the always-loaded version and includes the UI-renders gate and one-command-dev requirement that this file would otherwise drift on.

=== skills/orchestrator/references/file-ownership.md ===
1: # File Ownership Map
2: 
3: This table is the **canonical** ownership map for the whole Skill-Madness toolkit — it enumerates **every** skill that declares exclusive ownership of a directory or pattern, not just the build-time role agents. When in doubt, this overrides any individual skill's frontmatter.
4: 
5: **Precedence rules:**
6: 
7: 1. **Directory ownership takes precedence over pattern ownership.** If a pattern (e.g. `docs/adr/**`) would land inside a directory owned by another skill, the more specific directory owner wins.
8: 2. **Subdirectory carve-outs are explicit.** A deeper-nested directory can be carved out of a broader owner (e.g. `performance-agent` owns `tests/performance/` carved out of qe-agent's `tests/`; `setup-project-skills` owns `docs/agents/` and `maintain-context` owns `docs/adr/`, both carved out of docs-agent's `docs/`).
9: 3. **No path has two owners.** Every owned directory/pattern below resolves to exactly one skill. Skills not listed here own nothing — their write capability (if any) comes from `allowed-tools`, not ownership.
10: 4. **Test/spec files beat UI-extension patterns (glob-intersection tiebreak).** One filename can match two *pattern* owners at once — e.g. `Button.test.tsx` matches both frontend-agent's `*.tsx` and qe-agent's `*.test.*`. The more specific test/spec pattern wins: any file matching `*.test.*` or `*.spec.*` belongs to **qe-agent**, even when it also carries a UI extension (`.tsx`, `.jsx`, `.vue`, `.svelte`, `.css`). `lint-skills.sh` surfaces cross-skill `owns.patterns` intersections as a WARN; this frontend/qe pair is the one documented tiebreak on its allowlist (add a pair to that allowlist only after stating its tiebreak here).
11: 
12: ## Build-time role agents
13: 
14: These are spawned by the orchestrator during a multi-agent build. File ownership between them is exclusive and resolved before any agent is spawned.
15: 
16: | Agent Role | Owns (Exclusive) | Shared Read | Never Touches |
17: |------------|-----------------|-------------|---------------|
18: | orchestrator | `.gitignore` | `contracts/`, `.claude/handoffs/`, `*` | `src/` |
19: | backend-agent | `src/api/`, `src/services/`, `src/models/`, `src/middleware/`, `src/utils/` | `contracts/`, `shared/`, `src/types/` | `src/components/`, `src/pages/`, `.env.example` |
20: | frontend-agent | `src/components/`, `src/pages/`, `src/hooks/`, `src/styles/`, `public/`, `*.tsx`, `*.jsx`, `*.vue`, `*.svelte`, `*.css` | `contracts/`, `shared/`, `src/types/`, `assets/` | `src/api/`, `src/services/` |
21: | infrastructure-agent | `.github/workflows/`, `nginx/`, `k8s/`, `terraform/`, `scripts/deploy/`, `Dockerfile*`, `docker-compose*`, `Makefile`, `justfile`, **`.env.example`** | All (read-only) | `src/` |
22: | qe-agent | `tests/` *(excl. `tests/performance/`)*, `e2e/`, `__tests__/`, `*.test.*`, `*.spec.*`, `qa-report.md`, `qa-report.json` | All (read-only) | `src/` (test files in `src/` owned by the directory's agent) |
23: | performance-agent | `tests/performance/`, `load-tests/` | All (read-only) | `src/` |
24: | security-agent | `.github/security/`, `SECURITY.md` | All (read-only) | `src/` |
25: | db-migration-agent | `migrations/`, `seeds/`, `prisma/`, `alembic/`, `knex/migrations/` | `src/models/` | `src/` (non-migration) |
26: | observability-agent | `src/telemetry/`, `src/logging/`, `monitoring/`, `alerts/` | `src/` | other agents' `src/` |
27: | docs-agent | `docs/` *(excl. `docs/agents/` and `docs/adr/`)*, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` | All (read-only) | `src/`, config files, test files, `docs/agents/`, `docs/adr/` |
28: | code-review-agent | *(nothing — review only)* | All (read-only) | everything (read-only role) |
29: | contract-author | `contracts/`, `schemas/`, `openapi.yaml`, `asyncapi.yaml` | `*` | `src/` |
30: | contract-auditor | *(nothing — audit only)* | `contracts/`, `src/`, `backend/`, `frontend/`, `docs/` | everything (read-only role) |
31: 
32: ## Workflow & meta skills that own paths
33: 
34: These skills run outside (or alongside) a build but still claim exclusive ownership of specific paths. They are part of the canonical map so their paths never collide with a role agent's.
35: 
36: | Skill | Owns (Exclusive) | Notes |
37: |-------|-----------------|-------|
38: | setup-project-skills | `docs/agents/` | **Carve-out** from docs-agent's `docs/`. Writes the per-repo config (`domain-docs.md`, `contract-format.md`, `work-item-tracker.md`). |
39: | maintain-context | `docs/adr/`, `CONTEXT.md` | **Carve-out** from docs-agent's `docs/`. `docs/adr/` is a directory (precedence over patterns); `CONTEXT.md` is the domain glossary. |
40: | context-manager | `.claude/handoffs/` | Session-handoff files. Orchestrator shares-read this directory. |
41: | project-profiler | `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/profile.yaml` | Generated project profile + machine-readable profile. |
42: | llm-wiki | `index.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/overview.md` | Root knowledge-base index + `wiki/` log/overview pages (canonical layout: root `index.md` entry, `wiki/` articles). |
43: | settings-consolidator | `settings.local.json` | Claude Code permission files (merged into the global one). |
44: | work-item-brief | `briefs/**/*.md` | Generated work-item brief documents. |
45: | railway-deploy | `railway.toml` | Railway deployment config. (No directory ownership; pattern-only.) |
46: | sync-skills | `skills/workflows/sync-skills/` | Owns only its own subtree — the toolkit's `skills/` tree is otherwise un-owned. |
47: 
48: **Skills that own nothing:** `skill-update`, `skill-writer`, `skill-review`, `skill-explorer`, `deployment-checklist`, `claude-design-brief`, `architecture-rescue`, `ui-brief`, `diagnose-loop`, `wiki-research`, `plan-builder`, `playwright`, `render-sanity`, `living-plan`, `zoom-out`, `repo-deep-dive`, `dependency-coordinator`, `mermaid-charts`, `plan-intake`, `caveman`, `grill-me`, `nano-banana`, and the git skills (`git-commit`, `git-pr`, `git-pr-feedback`, `git-post-merge-cleanup`). Their `owns` blocks are empty by design; any write capability comes from `allowed-tools`. In particular `skill-update` owns nothing — it edits skills via `allowed-tools`, exactly like its sibling meta skills, so it does not swallow the `skills/` tree (which would otherwise collide with `sync-skills`).
49: 
50: ## The three explicit carve-outs under `docs/`
51: 
52: `docs-agent` owns `docs/` broadly, but two subtrees are owned by other skills and **must not** be written by docs-agent:
53: 
54: | Path | Exclusive owner | Carved out of |
55: |------|-----------------|---------------|
56: | `docs/agents/` | `setup-project-skills` | `docs/` (docs-agent) |
57: | `docs/adr/` | `maintain-context` | `docs/` (docs-agent) |
58: | `docs/` (everything else) | `docs-agent` | — |
59: 
60: Without these carve-outs, docs-agent's `docs/` directory ownership would silently swallow both subtrees (directory ownership beats pattern ownership), so each carve-out is declared as a directory in the owning skill's frontmatter and called out here.
61: 
62: ## The `.env.example` resolution
63: 
64: `.env.example` is owned by **infrastructure-agent only**. backend-agent does **not** own or create it: backend defines the variable names and safe local-dev defaults its services need and hands them to infrastructure-agent (via the lead), who writes the committed `.env.example`. The gitignored `.env` (real local values) remains backend-agent's concern. See `team-sizing.md` for the shared-infrastructure file assignment table.
65: 
66: ## Conflict resolution
67: 
68: **Rule**: If two roles would touch the same file, resolve the conflict by assigning that file to exactly one role before spawning. Unresolvable conflicts → human decision.
69: 
70: ## Contract-First Architecture
71: 
72: Contracts prevent the ~42% of multi-agent failures caused by specification problems. Before any agent is spawned:
73: 
74: 1. **Shared types first** — single source of truth for all entities
75: 2. **API contract** — exact URLs, methods, request/response JSON shapes, status codes
76: 3. **Data layer contract** — function signatures, storage semantics, cascade behavior
77: 4. **Cross-cutting concerns** — each assigned to exactly one agent
78: 
79: Use the `contract-author` skill and templates in `contracts/contract-author/references/`.

=== skills/orchestrator/references/agent-spawning.md ===
1: # Agent Spawning
2: 
3: The template, the permissions, and a worked example for spawning an implementation agent.
4: 
5: ## Role label ≠ subagent type (read this first)
6: 
7: `backend-agent`, `frontend-agent`, `docs-agent`, `qe-agent`, etc. are **role labels** — they name a *kind* of work and a body of instructions (the matching skill under `skills/roles/`). They are **NOT** valid `subagent_type` values for the Agent/Task tool.
8: 
9: When you dispatch, the tool's `subagent_type` must be a type the host actually registers. Passing a role label like `docs-agent` fails with `Agent type 'docs-agent' not found`. So:
10: 
11: 1. **Default to `general-purpose`.** It is always available and is the correct target for every role. The role is established by the *prompt*, not the type.
12: 2. **Carry the role instructions in the prompt.** The subagent type does NOT auto-load the role skill. Invoke the role skill inside the agent (or paste its checklist) as part of the prompt body. "Be the docs agent" must mean "apply the docs-agent skill," delivered as text.
13: 3. **Specialist types are optional and host-dependent.** If you've confirmed the host registers a closely-matching type, you MAY use it — but `general-purpose` + the role skill is always correct and never errors.
14: 
15: | Role label | Safe `subagent_type` (always works) | Specialist *if the host registers it* |
16: |---|---|---|
17: | backend-agent | `general-purpose` | `backend-architect`, `fullstack-developer` |
18: | frontend-agent | `general-purpose` | `frontend-developer` |
19: | infrastructure-agent | `general-purpose` | `deployment-engineer` |
20: | db-migration-agent | `general-purpose` | `backend-architect` |
21: | qe-agent | `general-purpose` | `test-engineer` |
22: | security-agent | `general-purpose` | `api-security-audit` |
23: | observability-agent | `general-purpose` | `error-detective` |
24: | performance-agent | `general-purpose` | `performance-profiler` |
25: | docs-agent | `general-purpose` | `api-documenter` |
26: | code-review-agent | `general-purpose` | `code-reviewer` |
27: 
28: When in doubt, use `general-purpose`. Never pass a `*-agent` label as `subagent_type`.
29: 
30: ## Agent Prompt Template
31: 
32: Each agent receives ONLY what they need:
33: 
34: ```text
35: You are the [ROLE] agent for this build.
36: 
37: ## Your Ownership
38: - You own: [exact directories/files]
39: - Do NOT touch: [other agents' territories]
40: - Read-only: contracts/
41: 
42: ## What You're Building
43: [Relevant plan excerpt — NOT the full plan]
44: 
45: ## Contracts
46: ### Shared Types (v1)
47: [Paste or reference]
48: ### Contract You Produce (v1)
49: [The contract this agent implements]
50: ### Contract You Consume (v1)
51: [The contract this agent depends on]
52: 
53: ## Domain Rules
54: [Relevant business rules from contracts/README.md that this agent must enforce]
55: 
56: ## Implementation Notes
57: [Per-agent guidance from contracts/README.md — libraries, patterns, framework specifics]
58: 
59: ## Before Reporting Done
60: [Specific validation commands]
61: ```
62: 
63: **Agent spawn permissions:** Spawn agents in a permission mode that allows file writes without per-tool prompts (in Claude Code, this is `mode: "auto"` on the Agent tool). Agents that cannot write files burn their entire context asking for permission instead of building.
64: 
65: ## The split gate: ~60 lines
66: 
67: The distilled brief has a numeric ceiling: **if it exceeds ~60 lines, split the task — don't compress the brief.** A brief that long means the task itself carries more than one concern (two contracts to implement, two subsystems, a build plus its migration), and the fix is two agents with whole briefs, not one agent with a trimmed one. Cutting to fit is how agents ship plausible-but-wrong work: the squeezed-out line — usually a Domain Rule — is exactly the one the agent needed, and the rework surfaces a wave later at the QA gate. The worked example below is ~40 lines against a 12-page plan; that ratio is healthy. A brief near 60 lines built from a *short* plan excerpt signals the ownership split is wrong, not that the template needs headroom.
68: 
69: ## Pre-dispatch checklist (run before every spawn)
70: 
71: Thirty seconds here is cheaper than the half-done dispatch any unchecked box produces. Before each Agent call, confirm:
72: 
73: - [ ] `subagent_type` is a registered type (`general-purpose` unless the host provably registers a specialist) — never a `*-agent` role label
74: - [ ] The role skill's instructions travel **in the prompt** (invoked or pasted) — the type does not load them
75: - [ ] Ownership block present: owns / do-NOT-touch / read-only, and the owns set overlaps no live agent's
76: - [ ] Contracts pasted or referenced **with versions** — both the contract this agent produces and the one it consumes
77: - [ ] Domain rules relevant to this agent included (the section most often squeezed out — see the split gate)
78: - [ ] "Before Reporting Done" lists concrete, runnable validation commands
79: - [ ] Ports and env values come from the project's convention (`.env.example`, dev scripts, `profile.yaml`), never a template default
80: - [ ] Brief is under the ~60-line split gate; if not, split the task
81: - [ ] AFK or HITL declared (next section), with checkpoints listed if HITL
82: - [ ] Permission mode allows file writes without per-tool prompts
83: 
84: ## AFK / HITL Classification (required)
85: 
86: Every agent dispatch MUST declare whether it can finish unattended:
87: 
88: - **AFK (away-from-keyboard)** — the agent has everything it needs to complete its work without further input. No mid-flight clarifying questions, no permission stalls, no external secrets it can't read.
89: - **HITL (human-in-the-loop)** — the agent will pause at known checkpoints for a human. Document the checkpoints up front so the user knows when to expect a return.
90: 
91: Spawning without an explicit classification is forbidden (see Anti-Patterns in the main skill).
92: 
93: ## Example: a filled-in backend-agent prompt
94: 
95: Here's what the template looks like in practice for a habit-tracker build. Notice how short the plan excerpt is — only the API/data sections, not the marketing copy or design system:
96: 
97: ```text
98: You are the backend agent for the habit-tracker build.
99: 
100: ## Your Ownership
101: - You own: src/api/, src/services/, src/models/, src/middleware/, .env.example
102: - Do NOT touch: src/components/, src/pages/, tests/ (qe agent owns)
103: - Read-only: contracts/
104: 
105: ## What You're Building
106: Habit CRUD + JWT auth + streak calculation. Plan §3.2 (Habits API) and §3.4 (Auth).
107: Soft-delete only. Streaks reset at 04:00 in the user's timezone.
108: 
109: ## Contracts
110: ### Shared Types (v1)
111: See contracts/types.ts — Habit, User, AuthToken, ErrorEnvelope.
112: ### Contract You Produce (v1)
113: contracts/api.openapi.yaml — implement exactly. URLs include trailing slashes.
114: ### Contract You Consume (v1)
115: contracts/data-layer.yaml — Postgres via the Drizzle client in src/db/.
116: 
117: ## Domain Rules
118: - Streak = consecutive days with ≥1 completion, computed in user TZ, reset at 04:00 local
119: - Soft-delete sets `deleted_at`; queries filter it out by default
120: - All timestamps stored UTC, returned ISO8601
121: 
122: ## Implementation Notes
123: Express + Zod for validation. JWT in Authorization header. CORS origin from
124: ALLOWED_ORIGIN env var (verified against the Cloud Run config in Phase 0).
125: 
126: ## Before Reporting Done
127: - `pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` clean
128: - `curl -i localhost:8000/api/habits/` returns 401 without auth, 200 with
129: - CORS preflight returns ALLOWED_ORIGIN, not `*`
130: ```
131: 
132: The prompt is ~40 lines. The full plan was 12 pages. That ratio is the point — every line not relevant to the backend agent is noise that crowds out the work.
133: 
134: > **Ports come from the project's convention, never a hardcoded default.** This example uses `8000` (the house backend port; frontend `5173`, FreeLLMAPI proxy `3001`) — do **not** paste a literal `localhost:3000` into agent prompts. `3000` is a frontend framework default, and worked examples that carry it leak into project code as the API port. Read the project's `.env.example` / dev scripts / `profile.yaml` for the real ports and pass *those* to the agent — ideally the env-var form (`curl -i localhost:$API_PORT/...`) so a port that auto-stepped at startup still validates. See `references/port-conventions.md` for the full map, the per-service bands, and the preflight rule that lets two projects run at once without colliding.

=== skills/loops/loop-controller/references/safety.md ===
1: # Loop safety — the guardrail stack, in full
2: 
3: A loop without enforcement is a way to spend money in your sleep. The dominant
4: non-convergence modes are **thrashing** (~36% of incomplete agent runs in one
5: benchmark) and **budget exhaustion** (~29%) — together the majority. The stack
6: below targets both. Alerting is not enough; every guard must *terminate*.
7: 
8: ## Contents
9: - [Exit condition (default-FAIL)](#exit-condition)
10: - [Iteration cap / circuit breaker](#iteration-cap)
11: - [Token / cost budget — enforced](#budget)
12: - [No-progress and oscillation detection](#oscillation)
13: - [HITL checkpoints before irreversible actions](#hitl)
14: - [Checkpoints and rollback](#rollback)
15: - [Guardrails against making things worse](#dont-make-it-worse)
16: - [Staged adoption and rollback ladder](#staged-adoption)
17: 
18: ---
19: 
20: ## Exit condition
21: 
22: Phrase "done" as a **mechanical, observable proof**: a test exit code, a file
23: count, an empty queue, a coverage %, a fresh evaluator's verdict. Use a
24: **default-FAIL contract** — criteria start `false`, evidence flips them — so the
25: loop can't mark itself passing. Avoid subjective conditions ("looks good") on the
26: model-evaluator path; route those through a fresh-context evaluator instead (see
27: `authoring.md`).
28: 
29: ## Iteration cap
30: 
31: Always set a hard cap. `--max-iterations` for hook/bash loops; "stop after N
32: turns" inside the `/goal` condition (it has no native cap). Reference defaults
33: from production loop engines (verify before hard-coding — these are
34: ralph-orchestrator's, partly from its legacy v1):
35: 
36: - `max_iterations: 100`
37: - `max_runtime_seconds: 14400` (4h)
38: - consecutive-failure limit: **5**
39: 
40: Stop-hook loops **must check `stop_hook_active`** in the hook payload before
41: blocking — an unconditional block is an infinite loop.
42: 
43: ## Budget
44: 
45: A token/cost ceiling that **terminates** the loop. Per-loop, enforced:
46: 
47: - `/goal` has **no** native budget — embed a turn cap and watch `/cost`.
48: - Dynamic workflows take an explicit token budget — pass one.
49: - Bash loops need an external iteration + token counter that exits at the cap.
50: 
51: Sizing reality: "Autonomous loops consume significant tokens. A 50-iteration
52: cycle on large codebases can cost $50–100+ in API credits." Multi-agent loops
53: use 3–5×+ the tokens of a single session. The lesson from the documented **11-day
54: / tens-of-thousands-of-dollars** runaway: it had observability but no
55: *enforcement*. Set per-agent budget caps with hard termination — non-negotiable
56: for anything unattended.
57: 
58: ## Oscillation
59: 
60: Stop when the loop stops making progress:
61: 
62: - **Output repetition** — ≥~90% similarity to a recent iteration's output trips
63:   the breaker (one documented system repeated the same answer **58 times**
64:   without this guard).
65: - **No state change** — no files changed, no proof movement across an iteration.
66: - **Token growth** that is quadratic rather than linear across iterations — a
67:   sign of context bloat / re-reading without progress.
68: 
69: ## HITL
70: 
71: Mandatory human review **before any irreversible action**: DB writes, deploys,
72: external API calls, force-push, anything that touches production. Implement as a
73: checkpoint that *pauses* the loop (ralph-orchestrator uses a `human.interact`
74: event with a default 300s timeout). Unattended loops run **only** what is
75: reversible and has a hard verifier — coverage, lint/type fixes, dependency-
76: update-then-test, docs. Never unattended: production data changes, deploys,
77: anything without a test gate.
78: 
79: ## Rollback
80: 
81: - **Checkpoint commit every iteration** with a descriptive message (the
82:   coding-agent recipe). The git trail is your undo.
83: - On a wedged codebase, `git reset --hard` to the last green checkpoint and
84:   re-loop is usually cheaper than rescuing the mess.
85: - **Worktree isolation** lets parallel agents fail without contaminating each
86:   other — use it for any fan-out loop.
87: 
88: ## Don't make it worse
89: 
90: - Validate **only the changed unit** each loop for *speed*, but re-run the
91:   **whole** proof before declaring done (catch fixes that broke something else).
92: - **Forbid editing or deleting tests to make them pass.** Anthropic's harness
93:   says, verbatim, *"It is unacceptable to remove or edit tests"* — and stores the
94:   feature list as JSON because the model is less likely to overwrite JSON than
95:   Markdown.
96: - Use a **fresh-context evaluator with no Write/Edit tools** so the grader
97:   cannot "fix" a failure by lowering the bar.
98: - **A suspiciously easy green is a finding.** When a gate flips red→green, read
99:   the diff that did it: did it *resolve* the finding or *relocate* it into the
100:   checker's blind spot (a banned `rounded-full` reborn as inline
101:   `borderRadius:"50%"`), silence it with an ignore directive, or delete the
102:   assertion? Adversarial verification checks the fix, not the count.
103: - **A self-improving loop must gate its own edits on measured, held-out
104:   results — and there is now hard evidence for why.** microsoft/SkillOpt's
105:   Sleep study ran twin overnight self-improvement loops on SearchQA, one with
106:   an accept/reject validation gate and one without. The ungated twin learned a
107:   plausible-but-wrong rule ("answer with the document-title string verbatim")
108:   and collapsed **−52.8 points** night over night; the gated twin rejected
109:   every one of those edits and lost nothing. For any loop that edits its own
110:   prompt, skill, or config, the gate is not polish — it is the difference
111:   between converging and self-lobotomizing.
112: 
113: ## Staged adoption
114: 
115: Don't go from zero to overnight-unattended. Promote on evidence:
116: 
117: - **Stage 1 (attended, one repo):** run the loop with a human watching.
118:   **Promote when** it completes **5 consecutive runs** with no human intervention
119:   and no oscillation.
120: - **Stage 2 (fresh-context evaluator introduced):** add the separate grader.
121:   **Promote when** the evaluator's pass/fail agrees with your judgment **≥90%**
122:   of the time — tune its prompt against the divergences (Anthropic found Claude
123:   is "out of the box a poor QA agent" and needed several rounds of evaluator
124:   tuning; budget for it).
125: - **Stage 3 (unattended / overnight):** allow **only when** every loop has a
126:   hard external verifier, an *enforced* token budget, no-progress detection,
127:   checkpoint commits, and a sandbox.
128: 
129: **Roll a loop back to attended (or kill it) when** any of: token spend grows
130: non-linearly, output similarity >90% across iterations, the evaluator and you
131: disagree, or it touches an irreversible resource without a checkpoint.

=== skills/loops/loop-controller/references/primitives.md ===
1: # Loop primitives — mechanics, constraints, and how to choose
2: 
3: The five native ways to run an autonomous loop in Claude Code, plus the bash
4: "Ralph" loop. Pick by the decision rule in SKILL.md Step 1, then use the exact
5: mechanics here. **Version- and plan-dependent — verify against your build;** the
6: version floors below were current at research time (Opus 4.8 / Fable 5 era).
7: 
8: ## Contents
9: - [`/goal` — the "loop until done" wrapper](#goal)
10: - [`/loop` — the scheduler](#loop)
11: - [Stop / SubagentStop hooks — the gate that ships with a skill](#stop-hooks)
12: - [`/batch` and dynamic workflows — fan-out](#batch-and-dynamic-workflows)
13: - [The bash "Ralph" loop — fresh context every iteration](#ralph)
14: - [Implementation-approach tradeoff table](#tradeoffs)
15: 
16: ---
17: 
18: ## /goal
19: 
20: The real "loop until done." Requires Claude Code **v2.1.139+**.
21: 
22: You set a completion condition. After each turn a **small fast model (defaults
23: to Haiku)** judges from the transcript whether the condition holds, returning
24: yes/no + a reason. "No" feeds the reason back as guidance for the next turn;
25: "yes" clears the goal. It is a wrapper around a session-scoped, prompt-based Stop
26: hook.
27: 
28: **The critical constraint:** the evaluator **does not run tools or read files** —
29: it judges *only what Claude has surfaced in the conversation*. So the condition
30: must be phrased as something Claude's own output can demonstrate:
31: 
32: - ✅ `"npm test exits 0 and git status is clean"`
33: - ✅ `"every acceptance criterion in DESIGN.md is shown satisfied, or stop after 20 turns"`
34: - ❌ `"the code is correct"` (nothing in the transcript proves it)
35: 
36: Other mechanics:
37: - Condition up to **4,000 characters**; **one goal per session** (a new `/goal`
38:   replaces the old); `/goal clear` cancels.
39: - **No built-in token budget.** It runs until the condition is met or you
40:   Ctrl+C. Embed a turn cap *in the condition* (`"… or stop after N turns"`) —
41:   this is the official recommendation, and your iteration-cap guardrail.
42: - Works interactive, headless (`claude -p "/goal …"`), desktop, and Remote
43:   Control. The recommended unattended combo is **auto mode + `/goal`**: auto mode
44:   removes per-tool prompts, `/goal` removes per-turn prompts.
45: - Requires accepting the workspace-trust dialog; unavailable if
46:   `disableAllHooks` or `allowManagedHooksOnly` is set.
47: 
48: Anthropic's canonical use cases: migrate a module until every call site compiles
49: and tests pass; implement a design doc until all acceptance criteria hold; split
50: a large file until each module is under a size budget; work a labeled-issue
51: backlog until empty.
52: 
53: ## /loop
54: 
55: A scheduler, **not** a "loop until done." A bundled skill that re-runs a prompt
56: or slash command on a cron interval. Requires **v2.1.71/72+**.
57: 
58: ```
59: /loop 5m check if the deploy finished
60: /loop 20m /review-pr 1234          # schedule ANY slash command or skill
61: /loop 30m /slack-feedback
62: ```
63: 
64: This is the key composition point — **skills + loops**. Boris Cherny's
65: documented daily setup: `/loop 5m /babysit` (auto-address review comments,
66: auto-rebase PRs) and `/loop 30m /slack-feedback`.
67: 
68: Mechanics:
69: - Interval = number + unit (`s` rounds up to 1 min, `m`, `h`, `d`); **default 10
70:   minutes**. Omit the interval and Claude picks a **dynamic self-paced delay
71:   (1 min–1 hour)** based on what it observed, or runs `loop.md` if present.
72: - **Session-scoped** — dies when you close the terminal.
73: - **No catch-up** — fires once when idle, not once per missed interval.
74: - **Auto-expires after 3 days** (some docs say 7 — treat **3 days** as the safe
75:   assumption and verify on your version).
76: - Max **50 scheduled tasks** per session; **Esc** clears the pending wakeup.
77: - It does **not** automate Claude's internal agentic loop — it's a thin
78:   scheduler that periodically restarts that loop from the outside.
79: - Disable entirely with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CRON=1`.
80: 
81: ## Stop hooks
82: 
83: The gate that ships *with* a skill. A **Stop / SubagentStop** hook intercepts the
84: agent trying to exit; returning `decision: "block"` (or exit code 2) forces it to
85: keep working until a real condition clears — "don't stop until tests pass."
86: 
87: - A Stop hook declared in a skill/agent's frontmatter is **automatically
88:   converted to SubagentStop** when that agent runs as a subagent — so a loop gate
89:   travels with the skill into orchestrated builds.
90: - The hook can be a **script** (run the real check, block on non-zero) or a
91:   **prompt/agent** evaluation (`{"type":"prompt","prompt":"Evaluate if Claude
92:   should stop: $ARGUMENTS"}`) — the latter is how `/goal` is implemented under
93:   the hood.
94: - **MUST guard `stop_hook_active`** in the hook payload. A hook that always
95:   blocks is an infinite loop; check the flag and let the stop through once the
96:   condition is genuinely met.
97: - Related lifecycle hooks for orchestrated loops: **TaskCompleted** (a
98:   validation gate — exit 2 rolls a task back), **TeammateIdle** ("assign new
99:   work" — keeps an outer loop fed).
100: 
101: This repo already ships a hooks layer (`hooks/`, e.g. the `qa-gate` Stop hook).
102: A loop skill's gate plugs in the same way.
103: 
104: ## /batch and dynamic workflows
105: 
106: Fan-out, not convergence-in-place.
107: 
108: - **`/batch`** spreads one large change across **5–30 parallel worktree-isolated
109:   subagents**, each opening a PR.
110: - **Dynamic workflows** (debuted as a research preview at CLI **v2.1.154**; the
111:   opt-in keyword was renamed `workflow` → **`ultracode`** at v2.1.160, and the
112:   feature ships default-on for Max/Team/API at v2.1.170 / Fable 5): Claude writes a
113:   **JavaScript orchestration script** that fans out across up to **1,000
114:   subagents (16 concurrent)**, keeping intermediate results in script variables
115:   (not context). Primitives: `agent()` (one subagent, optional JSON-schema-
116:   validated output), `parallel()` (barrier — awaits all), `pipeline()` (stream
117:   items through stages, no barrier), with `isolation: 'worktree'` and a token
118:   budget. Named patterns: **fan-out → reduce → synthesize**, **judge panel**,
119:   **loop-until-dry** (spawn finders until K rounds find nothing new).
120: 
121: In this repo, **don't hand-roll this** — it's the `orchestrator`'s Workflow mode
122: (ultracode). A migration/fan-out loop composes the orchestrator rather than
123: writing its own JS. Caveat: workflows are "meaningfully more usage" — one
124: developer spawned 90 review agents and hit monthly token limits. Budget hard.
125: 
126: ## Ralph
127: 
128: The bash loop — `while :; do cat PROMPT.md | claude -p ; done` — Geoffrey
129: Huntley's pattern, the philosophical root the native commands absorbed.
130: 
131: Use it when you want a **genuinely fresh context window every iteration** (state
132: lives entirely on disk, so context never overflows) — primarily **greenfield**
133: bootstrapping. Hard-won principles worth carrying into any loop:
134: 
135: - **"Deterministically bad in an undeterministic world"** — failures are
136:   predictable; tune the prompt ("erect a sign") each time it falls off the slide.
137: - **One thing per loop.** Trust the loop to pick the most important thing.
138: - **Primary context as scheduler** — spawn subagents for expensive work; cap
139:   build/test parallelism at 1 to preserve backpressure.
140: - **File-system-as-memory** — `fix_plan.md`, `@AGENT.md`, `specs/*`; state and
141:   git history survive between fresh-context passes.
142: - **Backpressure is the engineering** — wire in type checkers, tests, scanners;
143:   "the wheel has got to turn fast."
144: - **Anti-cheating** — Claude is biased toward placeholder/minimal
145:   implementations; prompt emphatically against stubs and run a separate pass to
146:   hunt them down.
147: 
148: Requires `--dangerously-skip-permissions` → **must run sandboxed** (Docker / a
149: throwaway box). No native budget — wrap an external iteration + token counter.
150: Huntley's own caveat: Ralph is greenfield-only and gets "~90% done"; senior
151: judgment is still required. The official `ralph-loop` plugin implements the same
152: idea via a Stop hook with `--completion-promise` (exact-string match) and
153: `--max-iterations` (the real safety mechanism).
154: 
155: ## Tradeoffs
156: 
157: | Approach | Mechanism | Best for | Tradeoff |
158: |---|---|---|---|
159: | **`/goal`** | Haiku evaluator loops turns until a transcript-provable condition holds | "work until correct" where done is provable from output | evaluator can't read files/run tools; no built-in budget |
160: | **Stop/SubagentStop hook** | hook blocks exit until a script/prompt condition passes | deterministic gates ("tests must pass"); ships with the skill | must guard `stop_hook_active`; always-block = infinite loop |
161: | **`/loop` + skill** | scheduler re-runs a slash command on an interval | recurring/poll workflows (babysit PRs, nightly sweeps) | session-scoped, ~3-day expiry, no catch-up |
162: | **`/batch` / dynamic workflow** | fan-out across worktree subagents | large parallel migration/research/review | token-hungry; research preview; use via orchestrator |
163: | **bash Ralph** | `while :; do claude -p < PROMPT.md ; done` | greenfield, fresh context per iteration | needs sandbox; no native budget; ~90% done |
164: 
165: **Fresh-context vs same-session:** Stop-hook loops continue the *same* session
166: (risking context overflow + lossy compaction); the Ralph school **restarts each
167: iteration with clean context**, reading state from disk. For long migrations,
168: prefer fresh-context-per-task; for short refine loops, same-session is fine.

=== skills/workflows/design-token-guard/scripts/check_design_tokens.py ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """
3: design-token-guard — a source-level gate that prevents inline styles and
4: hardcoded CSS values from bypassing a project's design-token system.
5: 
6: Why this exists: render-level gates (visual review, e2e, screenshots) never
7: catch a hardcoded color, because `style={{ background: "#07090c" }}` renders
8: identically to `var(--tl-tooltip)`. The violation is only visible in source.
9: This is that missing source-level check.
10: 
11: It is framework-agnostic (React/JSX, Vue, Svelte, Angular, Astro, plain HTML)
12: and token-source-agnostic (CSS custom properties, SCSS/Less vars, JS/TS theme
13: objects, design-token JSON). It auto-discovers the project's tokens and derives
14: a value->token map, so the fix it suggests is exact ("#07090c is var(--tl-tooltip)")
15: no matter the stack. No third-party dependencies — Python 3.8+ stdlib only.
16: 
17: Usage:
18:   python3 check_design_tokens.py [--root DIR] [--config PATH]
19:                                  [--staged] [--json] [--quiet] [PATHS...]
20: 
21: Exit codes: 0 = clean, 1 = error-severity findings, 2 = usage/config error.
22: """
23: from __future__ import annotations
24: 
25: import argparse
26: import json
27: import os
28: import re
29: import subprocess
30: import sys
31: from dataclasses import dataclass, field, asdict
32: from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
33: 
34: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
35: # Defaults. Everything here is overridable via .design-guard.json at the root.
36: # The two universal rules are "error"; the project-specific library is "off".
37: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
38: DEFAULT_CONFIG = {
39:     # Token sources. Empty => auto-discover (see discover_token_sources).
40:     "tokenSources": [],
41:     # File extensions to scan for violations.
42:     "scanExtensions": [
43:         ".tsx", ".ts", ".jsx", ".js", ".mjs", ".cjs",
44:         ".vue", ".svelte", ".astro", ".html",
45:     ],
46:     # Directories never walked.
47:     "ignoreDirs": [
48:         "node_modules", ".git", ".next", "dist", "build", "out",
49:         "coverage", ".turbo", ".vercel", "__pycache__", ".svelte-kit",
50:     ],
51:     # Glob-ish substrings; a file whose path contains any of these is skipped
52:     # for the violation scan (token-definition files belong here).
53:     "ignorePathContains": [".config.", ".d.ts"],
54:     "rules": {
55:         # --- universal, on by default ---
56:         "no-hardcoded-color": "error",
57:         "no-inline-style": "warn",
58:         # --- project-specific library, opt-in ---
59:         "no-class-in-svg": "off",       # utility classes inside <svg> primitives
60:         "restricted-radius": "off",     # border-radius above maxRadiusPx
61:         "forbidden-colors": "off",      # see forbiddenColors list below
62:         "no-arbitrary-tailwind": "off", # arbitrary VALUES: text-[10px], max-w-[1100px]
63:     },
64:     # no-inline-style mode: "literal" flags only inline styles carrying a
65:     # hardcoded literal (dynamic/token-referencing inline styles are allowed);
66:     # "strict" flags every inline style attribute regardless.
67:     "inlineStyleMode": "literal",
68:     # restricted-radius: any radius value over this many px (or a rounded-*
69:     # utility class larger than ~2px) is flagged.
70:     "maxRadiusPx": 2,
71:     # forbidden-colors: list of regexes matched against each source line. Use
72:     # for partisan colors, a banned second accent, etc.
73:     "forbiddenColors": [],
74:     # Scan CSS/SCSS files themselves for hardcoded colors? Usually off — that
75:     # is where colors legitimately live (the token definitions).
76:     "scanStyleSheets": False,
77: }
78: 
79: COLOR_KEYS = ("color", "background", "fill", "stroke", "border", "shadow",
80:               "outline", "gradient")
81: 
82: # A color literal: #hex (3/4/6/8), rgb()/rgba(), hsl()/hsla().
83: COLOR_RE = re.compile(
84:     r"#(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{8}|[0-9a-fA-F]{6}|[0-9a-fA-F]{4}|[0-9a-fA-F]{3})\b"
85:     r"|rgba?\([^)]*\)"
86:     r"|hsla?\([^)]*\)"
87: )
88: 
89: # Dimension literal inside a quoted string: "12px", '2rem', "1.5em" ...
90: DIM_RE = re.compile(r"""['"]\s*-?\d*\.?\d+(px|rem|em|vh|vw|vmin|vmax|pt|ch)\s*['"]""")
91: 
92: SVG_PRIMITIVES = (
93:     "rect", "circle", "path", "line", "polyline", "polygon", "ellipse",
94:     "g", "text", "tspan", "use", "defs", "linearGradient", "radialGradient",
95:     "stop", "clipPath", "mask",
96: )
97: 
98: RADIUS_CLASS_RE = re.compile(r"\brounded-(?:md|lg|xl|2xl|3xl|full)\b")
99: RADIUS_VALUE_RE = re.compile(r"border-radius\s*:\s*(\d*\.?\d+)px")
100: 
101: # Tailwind arbitrary-value utility: prefix-[value] (text-[10px], max-w-[1100px]).
102: ARBITRARY_TW_RE = re.compile(r"(?<![\w-])([a-z][a-z0-9]*(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*)-\[([^\]]+)\]")
103: 
104: 
105: @dataclass
106: class Finding:
107:     rule: str
108:     severity: str
109:     file: str
110:     line: int
111:     col: int
112:     snippet: str
113:     message: str
114:     suggestion: Optional[str] = None
115: 
116: 
117: @dataclass
118: class TokenIndex:
119:     sources: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
120:     by_value: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)  # norm value -> token ref
121:     count: int = 0
122: 
123: 
124: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
125: # Color normalization — canonical key so source literals match token values.
126: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
127: def normalize_color(raw: str) -> Optional[str]:
128:     s = raw.strip().lower()
129:     if s.startswith("#"):
130:         h = s[1:]
131:         if len(h) == 3:
132:             h = "".join(c * 2 for c in h)
133:         elif len(h) == 4:
134:             h = "".join(c * 2 for c in h)
135:         if len(h) == 6:
136:             return "#" + h
137:         if len(h) == 8:
138:             r, g, b = int(h[0:2], 16), int(h[2:4], 16), int(h[4:6], 16)
139:             a = round(int(h[6:8], 16) / 255, 3)
140:             return _rgba_key(r, g, b, a)
141:         return None
142:     m = re.match(r"rgba?\(([^)]*)\)", s)
143:     if m:
144:         parts = [p.strip() for p in re.split(r"[,/ ]+", m.group(1)) if p.strip()]
145:         try:
146:             chans = []
147:             for i in range(3):
148:                 p = parts[i]
149:                 if p.endswith("%"):            # rgb(100%, 50%, 0%) is on a 0-100 scale
150:                     chans.append(int(round(float(p[:-1]) / 100 * 255)))
151:                 else:
152:                     chans.append(int(round(float(p))))
153:             r, g, b = (min(255, max(0, v)) for v in chans)
154:         except (ValueError, IndexError):
155:             return None
156:         a = 1.0
157:         if len(parts) >= 4:
158:             try:
159:                 a = float(parts[3].rstrip("%"))
160:                 if parts[3].endswith("%"):
161:                     a /= 100
162:             except ValueError:
163:                 a = 1.0
164:         if a >= 1.0:
165:             return "#%02x%02x%02x" % (r, g, b)
166:         return _rgba_key(r, g, b, round(a, 3))
167:     m = re.match(r"hsla?\(([^)]*)\)", s)
168:     if m:
169:         return "hsl:" + re.sub(r"\s+", "", m.group(1))
170:     return None
171: 
172: 
173: def _rgba_key(r: int, g: int, b: int, a: float) -> str:
174:     astr = ("%.3f" % a).rstrip("0").rstrip(".")
175:     return "rgba(%d,%d,%d,%s)" % (r, g, b, astr)
176: 
177: 
178: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
179: # Token discovery + parsing.
180: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
181: def discover_token_sources(root: str, cfg: dict) -> List[str]:
182:     if cfg["tokenSources"]:
183:         return [os.path.join(root, p) for p in cfg["tokenSources"]]
184:     found: List[str] = []
185:     name_hints = ("token", "theme", "variable", "var", "design", "palette", "color")
186:     for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
187:         dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in cfg["ignoreDirs"]]
188:         for fn in filenames:
189:             low = fn.lower()
190:             if low.endswith((".css", ".scss", ".less")) and any(h in low for h in name_hints):
191:                 found.append(os.path.join(dirpath, fn))
192:             elif low.endswith((".json",)) and "token" in low:
193:                 found.append(os.path.join(dirpath, fn))
194:     # Fallback: any css that declares many custom properties.
195:     if not found:
196:         for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
197:             dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in cfg["ignoreDirs"]]
198:             for fn in filenames:
199:                 if fn.lower().endswith((".css", ".scss")):
200:                     p = os.path.join(dirpath, fn)
201:                     try:
202:                         txt = _read(p)
203:                     except OSError:
204:                         continue
205:                     if len(re.findall(r"--[\w-]+\s*:", txt)) >= 5:
206:                         found.append(p)
207:     return found
208: 
209: 
210: def build_token_index(sources: List[str]) -> TokenIndex:
211:     idx = TokenIndex()
212:     for src in sources:
213:         try:
214:             text = _read(src)
215:         except OSError:
216:             continue
217:         idx.sources.append(src)
218:         pairs: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
219:         if src.lower().endswith(".json"):
220:             pairs = _parse_json_tokens(text)
221:         else:
222:             pairs += re.findall(r"(--[\w-]+)\s*:\s*([^;]+);", text)          # CSS vars
223:             pairs += re.findall(r"(\$[\w-]+)\s*:\s*([^;]+);", text)           # SCSS
224:             pairs += re.findall(r"(@[\w-]+)\s*:\s*([^;]+);", text)            # Less
225:             pairs += re.findall(r"['\"]?([\w-]+)['\"]?\s*:\s*['\"](#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8})['\"]", text)  # JS theme obj
226:         for name, value in pairs:
227:             idx.count += 1
228:             for m in COLOR_RE.finditer(value):
229:                 key = normalize_color(m.group(0))
230:                 if key and key not in idx.by_value:
231:                     idx.by_value[key] = _token_ref(name)
232:     return idx
233: 
234: 
235: def _parse_json_tokens(text: str) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
236:     try:
237:         data = json.loads(text)
238:     except json.JSONDecodeError:
239:         return []
240:     out: List[Tuple[str, str]] = []
241: 
242:     def walk(node, path):
243:         if isinstance(node, dict):
244:             if "value" in node and isinstance(node["value"], (str, int, float)):
245:                 out.append(("-".join(path), str(node["value"])))
246:             for k, v in node.items():
247:                 if k == "value":
248:                     continue
249:                 walk(v, path + [str(k)])
250:         elif isinstance(node, str):
251:             out.append(("-".join(path), node))
252: 
253:     walk(data, [])
254:     return out
255: 
256: 
257: def _token_ref(name: str) -> str:
258:     if name.startswith("--"):
259:         return "var(%s)" % name
260:     return name  # $scss / @less / js-key referenced as-is in suggestion text
261: 
262: 
263: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
264: # File collection.
265: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
266: def collect_files(root: str, cfg: dict, explicit: List[str], staged: bool) -> List[str]:
267:     exts = tuple(cfg["scanExtensions"])
268:     if cfg["scanStyleSheets"]:
269:         exts = exts + (".css", ".scss", ".less")
270: 
271:     def keep(path: str) -> bool:
272:         if not path.endswith(exts):
273:             return False
274:         if any(s in path for s in cfg["ignorePathContains"]):
275:             return False
276:         norm = path.replace(os.sep, "/")
277:         if any(("/%s/" % d) in norm for d in cfg["ignoreDirs"]):
278:             return False
279:         return True
280: 
281:     if staged:
282:         try:
283:             out = subprocess.run(
284:                 ["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only", "--diff-filter=ACM"],
285:                 cwd=root, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,
286:             ).stdout
287:         except (subprocess.CalledProcessError, FileNotFoundError) as e:
288:             # A gate that silently passes when git can't be queried is a false
289:             # green — surface it instead of reporting "nothing to check".
290:             print("design-token-guard: --staged could not list staged files "
291:                   "(%s); treating as no files." % e, file=sys.stderr)
292:             return []
293:         staged_files: List[str] = []
294:         for rel in out.splitlines():
295:             ap = os.path.join(root, rel)
296:             if keep(ap):  # keep() needs the joined path so the ignoreDirs "/d/" test matches
297:                 staged_files.append(ap)
298:         return staged_files
299: 
300:     if explicit:
301:         files: List[str] = []
302:         for p in explicit:
303:             ap = p if os.path.isabs(p) else os.path.join(root, p)
304:             if os.path.isdir(ap):
305:                 files += _walk(ap, cfg, keep)
306:             elif os.path.isfile(ap) and keep(ap):
307:                 files.append(ap)
308:         return files
309: 
310:     return _walk(root, cfg, keep)
311: 
312: 
313: def _walk(base: str, cfg: dict, keep) -> List[str]:
314:     files: List[str] = []
315:     for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(base):
316:         dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in cfg["ignoreDirs"]]
317:         for fn in filenames:
318:             p = os.path.join(dirpath, fn)
319:             if keep(p):
320:                 files.append(p)
321:     return files
322: 
323: 
324: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
325: # Comment masking — blank comment spans while preserving length/newlines so
326: # line/col stay accurate. Keeps the color rule from flagging documented hexes.
327: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
328: def mask_comments(text: str) -> str:
329:     out = list(text)
330:     i, n = 0, len(text)
331:     state = None  # None | "line" | "block" | "string"
332:     quote = ""
333:     while i < n:
334:         c = text[i]
335:         two = text[i:i + 2]
336:         if state is None:
337:             if two == "//":
338:                 state = "line"; out[i] = out[i + 1] = " "; i += 2; continue
339:             if two == "/*":
340:                 state = "block"; out[i] = out[i + 1] = " "; i += 2; continue
341:             if text[i:i + 4] == "<!--":        # HTML/Vue/Svelte/Astro comment
342:                 state = "html"
343:                 out[i] = out[i + 1] = out[i + 2] = out[i + 3] = " "
344:                 i += 4; continue
345:             if c in "\"'`":
346:                 state = "string"; quote = c; i += 1; continue
347:         elif state == "line":
348:             if c == "\n":
349:                 state = None
350:             else:
351:                 out[i] = " "
352:             i += 1; continue
353:         elif state == "block":
354:             if two == "*/":
355:                 out[i] = out[i + 1] = " "; state = None; i += 2; continue
356:             if c != "\n":
357:                 out[i] = " "
358:             i += 1; continue
359:         elif state == "html":
360:             if text[i:i + 3] == "-->":
361:                 out[i] = out[i + 1] = out[i + 2] = " "; state = None; i += 3; continue
362:             if c != "\n":
363:                 out[i] = " "
364:             i += 1; continue
365:         elif state == "string":
366:             if c == "\\":
367:                 i += 2; continue
368:             if c == quote:
369:                 state = None
370:             i += 1; continue
371:         i += 1
372:     return "".join(out)
373: 
374: 
375: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
376: # Position helpers.
377: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
378: def line_col(text: str, offset: int) -> Tuple[int, int]:
379:     line = text.count("\n", 0, offset) + 1
380:     last_nl = text.rfind("\n", 0, offset)
381:     col = offset - last_nl
382:     return line, col
383: 
384: 
385: def line_at(text: str, offset: int) -> str:
386:     start = text.rfind("\n", 0, offset) + 1
387:     end = text.find("\n", offset)
388:     if end == -1:
389:         end = len(text)
390:     return text[start:end].strip()
391: 
392: 
393: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
394: # Rules. Each returns a list of Finding.
395: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
396: def rule_hardcoded_color(path, rel, raw, masked, idx, cfg, sev) -> List[Finding]:
397:     findings = []
398:     for m in COLOR_RE.finditer(masked):
399:         literal = raw[m.start():m.end()]
400:         key = normalize_color(literal)
401:         token = idx.by_value.get(key) if key else None
402:         if token:
