403:             msg = "hardcoded color %s is %s" % (literal, token)
404:             sugg = "use %s" % token
405:         else:
406:             msg = "hardcoded color %s is not a declared token" % literal
407:             sugg = "add it to a token source, or reuse an existing token"
408:         ln, col = line_col(masked, m.start())
409:         findings.append(Finding("no-hardcoded-color", sev, rel, ln, col,
410:                                 line_at(raw, m.start()), msg, sugg))
411:     return findings
412: 
413: 
414: def rule_inline_style(path, rel, raw, masked, idx, cfg, sev) -> List[Finding]:
415:     findings = []
416:     mode = cfg["inlineStyleMode"]
417:     # JSX: style={{ ... }}
418:     for m in re.finditer(r"style\s*=\s*\{\{", masked):
419:         body, end = _balanced(masked, m.end() - 1)  # start at first '{'
420:         if body is None:
421:             continue
422:         raw_body = raw[m.end():end - 1] if end else raw[m.end():m.end() + len(body)]
423:         _maybe_inline_finding(findings, raw, masked, m.start(), raw_body, mode, sev, rel)
424:     # HTML/Vue/Svelte/Angular: style="..." or :style / [style]
425:     for m in re.finditer(r"""(?::|\[)?style\]?\s*=\s*["']""", masked):
426:         q = masked[m.end() - 1]
427:         close = masked.find(q, m.end())
428:         if close == -1:
429:             continue
430:         raw_body = raw[m.end():close]
431:         _maybe_inline_finding(findings, raw, masked, m.start(), raw_body, mode, sev, rel)
432:     return findings
433: 
434: 
435: def _maybe_inline_finding(findings, raw, masked, start, raw_body, mode, sev, rel):
436:     has_color = bool(COLOR_RE.search(raw_body))
437:     has_dim = bool(DIM_RE.search(raw_body))
438:     if mode == "strict":
439:         fire, why = True, "inline style attribute (strict mode forbids all inline styles)"
440:     else:
441:         # Default: only fire when the inline style carries a hardcoded literal.
442:         # Color literals are owned by no-hardcoded-color (more specific +
443:         # suggestion), so here we fire for non-color literals like dimensions.
444:         if has_dim and not has_color:
445:             fire, why = True, "inline style with hardcoded dimension value"
446:         elif has_dim and has_color:
447:             fire, why = True, "inline style with hardcoded values"
448:         else:
449:             fire, why = False, ""
450:     if fire:
451:         ln, col = line_col(masked, start)
452:         findings.append(Finding("no-inline-style", sev, rel, ln, col,
453:                                 line_at(raw, start), why,
454:                                 "move the value into a token / utility class"))
455: 
456: 
457: def _balanced(text: str, open_idx: int) -> Tuple[Optional[str], int]:
458:     depth = 0
459:     i = open_idx
460:     n = len(text)
461:     while i < n:
462:         if text[i] == "{":
463:             depth += 1
464:         elif text[i] == "}":
465:             depth -= 1
466:             if depth == 0:
467:                 return text[open_idx:i + 1], i + 1
468:         i += 1
469:     return None, 0
470: 
471: 
472: def rule_class_in_svg(path, rel, raw, masked, idx, cfg, sev) -> List[Finding]:
473:     findings = []
474:     tag_re = re.compile(r"<(" + "|".join(SVG_PRIMITIVES) + r")\b([^>]*)>", re.DOTALL)
475:     for m in tag_re.finditer(masked):
476:         attrs = m.group(2)
477:         cm = re.search(r"(?:className|class)\s*=\s*[\"'{]([^\"'}]*)", attrs)
478:         if cm and re.search(r"[a-z]+-[a-z0-9\[]", cm.group(1)):
479:             ln, col = line_col(masked, m.start())
480:             findings.append(Finding("no-class-in-svg", sev, rel, ln, col,
481:                                     line_at(raw, m.start()),
482:                                     "utility classes on <%s> — SVG should use token attrs/vars" % m.group(1),
483:                                     "use fill/stroke with var(--token) instead of classes"))
484:     return findings
485: 
486: 
487: def rule_restricted_radius(path, rel, raw, masked, idx, cfg, sev) -> List[Finding]:
488:     findings = []
489:     maxpx = cfg["maxRadiusPx"]
490:     for m in RADIUS_CLASS_RE.finditer(masked):
491:         ln, col = line_col(masked, m.start())
492:         findings.append(Finding("restricted-radius", sev, rel, ln, col,
493:                                 line_at(raw, m.start()),
494:                                 "%s exceeds the %dpx radius cap" % (m.group(0), maxpx),
495:                                 "use a smaller radius token / utility"))
496:     for m in RADIUS_VALUE_RE.finditer(masked):
497:         if float(m.group(1)) > maxpx:
498:             ln, col = line_col(masked, m.start())
499:             findings.append(Finding("restricted-radius", sev, rel, ln, col,
500:                                     line_at(raw, m.start()),
501:                                     "border-radius %spx exceeds the %dpx cap" % (m.group(1), maxpx),
502:                                     "use a radius token"))
503:     return findings
504: 
505: 
506: def rule_forbidden_colors(path, rel, raw, masked, idx, cfg, sev) -> List[Finding]:
507:     findings = []
508:     pats = [re.compile(p) for p in cfg["forbiddenColors"]]
509:     for m in re.finditer(r".+", masked):
510:         for p in pats:
511:             for hit in p.finditer(m.group(0)):
512:                 off = m.start() + hit.start()
513:                 ln, col = line_col(masked, off)
514:                 findings.append(Finding("forbidden-colors", sev, rel, ln, col,
515:                                         line_at(raw, off),
516:                                         "forbidden color pattern '%s'" % hit.group(0),
517:                                         "use an approved token"))
518:     return findings
519: 
520: 
521: def rule_arbitrary_tailwind(path, rel, raw, masked, idx, cfg, sev) -> List[Finding]:
522:     findings = []
523:     for m in ARBITRARY_TW_RE.finditer(masked):
524:         value = m.group(2)
525:         # Colors inside arbitrary values are owned by no-hardcoded-color (it has
526:         # the token suggestion); token refs and url() are legitimate, not off-scale.
527:         if "var(--" in value or value.startswith("url(") or COLOR_RE.search(value):
528:             continue
529:         ln, col = line_col(masked, m.start())
530:         findings.append(Finding("no-arbitrary-tailwind", sev, rel, ln, col,
531:                                 line_at(raw, m.start()),
532:                                 "arbitrary Tailwind value %s bypasses the scale" % m.group(0),
533:                                 "use a scale/token utility, or add a token for this value"))
534:     return findings
535: 
536: 
537: RULES = {
538:     "no-hardcoded-color": rule_hardcoded_color,
539:     "no-inline-style": rule_inline_style,
540:     "no-class-in-svg": rule_class_in_svg,
541:     "restricted-radius": rule_restricted_radius,
542:     "forbidden-colors": rule_forbidden_colors,
543:     "no-arbitrary-tailwind": rule_arbitrary_tailwind,
544: }
545: 
546: 
547: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
548: # Driver.
549: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
550: def _read(path: str) -> str:
551:     with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
552:         return f.read()
553: 
554: 
555: def load_config(root: str, config_path: Optional[str]) -> dict:
556:     cfg = json.loads(json.dumps(DEFAULT_CONFIG))  # deep copy
557:     path = config_path or os.path.join(root, ".design-guard.json")
558:     if os.path.isfile(path):
559:         try:
560:             user = json.loads(_read(path))
561:         except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as e:
562:             print("design-token-guard: bad config %s: %s" % (path, e), file=sys.stderr)
563:             sys.exit(2)
564:         for k, v in user.items():
565:             if k == "rules" and isinstance(v, dict):
566:                 cfg["rules"].update(v)
567:             else:
568:                 cfg[k] = v
569:     return cfg
570: 
571: 
572: def run(root: str, cfg: dict, files: List[str], idx: TokenIndex) -> List[Finding]:
573:     findings: List[Finding] = []
574:     token_srcs = {os.path.abspath(s) for s in idx.sources}
575:     for path in files:
576:         if os.path.abspath(path) in token_srcs:
577:             continue
578:         try:
579:             raw = _read(path)
580:         except OSError:
581:             continue
582:         masked = mask_comments(raw)
583:         rel = os.path.relpath(path, root)
584:         for rule_name, fn in RULES.items():
585:             sev = cfg["rules"].get(rule_name, "off")
586:             if sev == "off":
587:                 continue
588:             findings += fn(path, rel, raw, masked, idx, cfg, sev)
589:     findings.sort(key=lambda f: (f.file, f.line, f.col))
590:     return findings
591: 
592: 
593: def report_human(findings, idx, cfg, quiet) -> None:
594:     errs = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "error")
595:     warns = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "warn")
596:     files = len({f.file for f in findings})
597:     if not findings:
598:         if not quiet:
599:             print("design-token-guard: clean — no inline styles or hardcoded CSS found.")
600:             _print_footer(idx, cfg)
601:         return
602:     print("design-token-guard: %d findings (%d error%s, %d warning%s) across %d file%s\n"
603:           % (len(findings), errs, "" if errs == 1 else "s",
604:              warns, "" if warns == 1 else "s", files, "" if files == 1 else "s"))
605:     by_rule: Dict[str, List[Finding]] = {}
606:     for f in findings:
607:         by_rule.setdefault(f.rule, []).append(f)
608:     for rule, items in by_rule.items():
609:         sev = items[0].severity
610:         print("%s (%s) — %d" % (rule, sev, len(items)))
611:         for f in items:
612:             print("  %s:%d:%d" % (f.file, f.line, f.col))
613:             print("    %s" % f.snippet)
614:             line = "    → %s" % f.message
615:             if f.suggestion:
616:                 line += "  ·  %s" % f.suggestion
617:             print(line)
618:         print("")
619:     _print_footer(idx, cfg)
620: 
621: 
622: def _print_footer(idx, cfg) -> None:
623:     src = ", ".join(os.path.basename(s) for s in idx.sources) or "none found"
624:     print("Token source: %s (%d tokens, %d color values mapped)"
625:           % (src, idx.count, len(idx.by_value)))
626: 
627: 
628: def report_json(findings, idx, cfg) -> None:
629:     errs = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "error")
630:     out = {
631:         "ok": errs == 0,
632:         "summary": {
633:             "total": len(findings),
634:             "errors": errs,
635:             "warnings": sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "warn"),
636:             "files": len({f.file for f in findings}),
637:         },
638:         "tokenSources": idx.sources,
639:         "tokensIndexed": idx.count,
640:         "colorValuesMapped": len(idx.by_value),
641:         "findings": [asdict(f) for f in findings],
642:     }
643:     print(json.dumps(out, indent=2))
644: 
645: 
646: def main(argv: List[str]) -> int:
647:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Prevent inline styles and hardcoded CSS.")
648:     ap.add_argument("--root", default=".", help="project root (default: cwd)")
649:     ap.add_argument("--config", help="path to .design-guard.json")
650:     ap.add_argument("--staged", action="store_true", help="scan git-staged files only")
651:     ap.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="machine-readable output")
652:     ap.add_argument("--quiet", action="store_true", help="suppress the clean-run message")
653:     ap.add_argument("paths", nargs="*", help="explicit files/dirs to scan")
654:     args = ap.parse_args(argv)
655: 
656:     root = os.path.abspath(args.root)
657:     if not os.path.isdir(root):
658:         print("design-token-guard: --root %s is not a directory" % root, file=sys.stderr)
659:         return 2
660: 
661:     cfg = load_config(root, args.config)
662:     sources = discover_token_sources(root, cfg)
663:     idx = build_token_index(sources)
664:     if not idx.sources and not args.json:
665:         print("design-token-guard: warning — no token source discovered; "
666:               "no-hardcoded-color can flag literals but cannot suggest tokens. "
667:               "Set \"tokenSources\" in .design-guard.json.\n", file=sys.stderr)
668: 
669:     files = collect_files(root, cfg, args.paths, args.staged)
670:     findings = run(root, cfg, files, idx)
671: 
672:     if args.json:
673:         report_json(findings, idx, cfg)
674:     else:
675:         report_human(findings, idx, cfg, args.quiet)
676: 
677:     return 1 if any(f.severity == "error" for f in findings) else 0
678: 
679: 
680: if __name__ == "__main__":
681:     sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

=== skills/workflows/class-extraction-guard/scripts/check_class_extraction.py ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """
3: class-extraction-guard — a source-level gate that catches utility-class soup:
4: the same long run of utility classes (`flex items-center gap-1.5 text-fg-muted
5: transition-colors hover:text-accent`) copy-pasted inline across many files
6: instead of extracted into a named class / component.
7: 
8: Why this exists: design-token-guard checks *which values* styling uses (tokens
9: vs hardcoded hex). It passes correctly-tokenized utilities by construction. But a
10: correctly-tokenized utility string repeated 9× inline is still a DRY/maintenance
11: problem — and it renders identically to an extracted version, so render-sanity
12: and ux-review (pixel gates) never see it either. It lives only in source, exactly
13: in the seam between the pixel gates and the value gate. This is that missing
14: *organization* check — the sibling to design-token-guard.
15: 
16: It is framework-agnostic (React/JSX, Vue, Svelte, Astro, plain HTML; and string
17: args to clsx / cn / classNames / cva / tv / twMerge). No third-party deps —
18: Python 3.8+ stdlib only.
19: 
20: Usage:
21:   python3 check_class_extraction.py [--root DIR] [--config PATH]
22:                                     [--staged] [--json] [--quiet]
23:                                     [--baseline PATH] [--write-baseline] [PATHS...]
24: 
25: Exit codes: 0 = clean (no error-severity findings), 1 = error-severity findings,
26: 2 = usage/config error. (Mirrors design-token-guard so it drops into the same
27: orchestrator wave-gate snippet unchanged.)
28: """
29: from __future__ import annotations
30: 
31: import argparse
32: import bisect
33: import hashlib
34: import json
35: import os
36: import re
37: import subprocess
38: import sys
39: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
40: from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
41: 
42: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
43: # Defaults. Everything here is overridable via .class-guard.json at the root.
44: # The one default-on rule is "warning" (non-blocking) — utility-soup is a
45: # preference-y standard, so adopting the gate should never block a build on day
46: # one. Flip a rule to "error" (and scaffold it at the bootstrap wave) when you
47: # want it to hard-gate a greenfield project from commit #1.
48: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
49: DEFAULT_CONFIG = {
50:     "scanExtensions": [
51:         ".tsx", ".ts", ".jsx", ".js", ".mjs", ".cjs",
52:         ".vue", ".svelte", ".astro", ".html",
53:     ],
54:     "ignoreDirs": [
55:         "node_modules", ".git", ".next", "dist", "build", "out",
56:         "coverage", ".turbo", ".vercel", "__pycache__", ".svelte-kit",
57:         ".cache", "storybook-static",
58:     ],
59:     # A class string must have at least this many utility tokens to count at all.
60:     "minUtilities": 4,
61:     # repeated-class-string fires when the same (order-normalized) string appears
62:     # in at least this many distinct call-sites.
63:     "minRepeats": 3,
64:     # long-class-string fires on a single string with at least this many tokens.
65:     "maxUtilities": 12,
66:     # Strings to never flag (exact, order-normalized match) — escape hatches.
67:     "allowlist": [],
68:     # Tokens that mark a string as already-abstracted (named/@apply classes). Used
69:     # by the opt-in abstraction-defeat rule. Regex, matched per token.
70:     "namedClassPattern": "",
71:     "rules": {
72:         "repeated-class-string": "warning",   # the strong signal: copy-paste soup
73:         "long-class-string": "off",           # opt-in: one-off mega-strings
74:         "abstraction-defeat": "off",          # opt-in: utilities glued onto a named class
75:     },
76: }
77: 
78: SEVERITY_RANK = {"off": 0, "warning": 1, "error": 2}
79: 
80: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
81: # Class-string extraction
82: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
83: # className="..." / className='...' / class="..." (Vue/Svelte/Astro/HTML)
84: _ATTR_RE = re.compile(
85:     r"""\b(?:className|class)\s*=\s*(?:\{?\s*)?(["'`])(?P<val>(?:(?!\1).)*?)\1""",
86:     re.DOTALL,
87: )
88: # string-literal args to class combinators: clsx("..."), cn('...'), cva("...")
89: _COMBINATOR_CALL_RE = re.compile(
90:     r"\b(?:clsx|cn|classNames|cx|cva|tv|twMerge|twJoin)\s*\((?P<args>[^)]*)\)",
91:     re.DOTALL,
92: )
93: _STR_LITERAL_RE = re.compile(r"""(["'`])(?P<val>(?:(?!\1).)*?)\1""", re.DOTALL)
94: 
95: # A token that looks like a CSS utility class (Tailwind-ish or kebab). Lets us
96: # ignore prose strings the combinator pass might grab.
97: _UTILITY_TOKEN_RE = re.compile(r"^[a-z!@\[]?[\w:\-./\[\]%#()&!]*$", re.IGNORECASE)
98: 
99: 
100: @dataclass
101: class ClassString:
102:     raw: str
103:     file: str
104:     line: int
105: 
106: 
107: @dataclass
108: class Finding:
109:     rule: str
110:     severity: str
111:     file: str
112:     line: int
113:     string: str
114:     count: int
115:     occurrences: List[str]
116:     suggestion: str
117: 
118: 
119: def _looks_like_classes(value: str) -> bool:
120:     """A whitespace-split value where most tokens look like utility classes."""
121:     tokens = [t for t in value.split() if t]
122:     if not tokens:
123:         return False
124:     classy = sum(1 for t in tokens if _UTILITY_TOKEN_RE.match(t))
125:     return classy >= max(1, int(len(tokens) * 0.7))
126: 
127: 
128: def _has_interpolation(value: str) -> bool:
129:     # Skip dynamic strings we can't tokenize reliably.
130:     return "${" in value or "{{" in value
131: 
132: 
133: def _newline_positions(text: str) -> List[int]:
134:     """Every newline offset, computed once per file. Recounting from position 0
135:     per match (`text.count("\\n", 0, pos)`) made the scan O(matches × file-size)
136:     — one big generated file stalled the pre-commit hook for tens of seconds."""
137:     out: List[int] = []
138:     i = text.find("\n")
139:     while i != -1:
140:         out.append(i)
141:         i = text.find("\n", i + 1)
142:     return out
143: 
144: 
145: def _line_of(newlines: List[int], pos: int) -> int:
146:     return bisect.bisect_left(newlines, pos) + 1
147: 
148: 
149: def extract_class_strings(text: str, rel_path: str) -> List[ClassString]:
150:     out: List[ClassString] = []
151:     seen_spans: List[Tuple[int, int]] = []
152:     newlines = _newline_positions(text)
153: 
154:     for m in _ATTR_RE.finditer(text):
155:         val = m.group("val")
156:         if _has_interpolation(val) or not _looks_like_classes(val):
157:             continue
158:         out.append(ClassString(val.strip(), rel_path, _line_of(newlines, m.start())))
159:         seen_spans.append((m.start(), m.end()))
160: 
161:     for call in _COMBINATOR_CALL_RE.finditer(text):
162:         args = call.group("args")
163:         base = call.start("args")
164:         for sm in _STR_LITERAL_RE.finditer(args):
165:             val = sm.group("val")
166:             if _has_interpolation(val) or not _looks_like_classes(val):
167:                 continue
168:             # Only count multi-token literals here — single utilities in a cn()
169:             # call are normal composition, not soup.
170:             if len([t for t in val.split() if t]) < 2:
171:                 continue
172:             out.append(ClassString(val.strip(), rel_path, _line_of(newlines, base + sm.start())))
173: 
174:     return out
175: 
176: 
177: def normalize(value: str) -> str:
178:     """Order-independent key: a sorted set of tokens. Two elements with the same
179:     utilities in a different order are the same extraction candidate."""
180:     return " ".join(sorted(set(t for t in value.split() if t)))
181: 
182: 
183: def token_count(value: str) -> int:
184:     return len(set(t for t in value.split() if t))
185: 
186: 
187: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
188: # Config + file discovery
189: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
190: def load_config(root: str, explicit: Optional[str]) -> dict:
191:     cfg = json.loads(json.dumps(DEFAULT_CONFIG))  # deep copy
192:     path = explicit or os.path.join(root, ".class-guard.json")
193:     if os.path.isfile(path):
194:         try:
195:             user = json.load(open(path, encoding="utf-8"))
196:         except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as e:
197:             print(f"class-extraction-guard: bad config {path}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
198:             sys.exit(2)
199:         for k, v in user.items():
200:             if k == "rules" and isinstance(v, dict):
201:                 cfg["rules"].update(v)
202:             else:
203:                 cfg[k] = v
204:     return cfg
205: 
206: 
207: def iter_files(root: str, cfg: dict, paths: List[str], staged: bool) -> List[str]:
208:     exts = tuple(cfg["scanExtensions"])
209:     ignore = set(cfg["ignoreDirs"])
210: 
211:     if staged:
212:         # `git diff --cached --name-only` emits paths relative to the repo
213:         # TOPLEVEL regardless of -C, so they must be joined onto the toplevel,
214:         # not onto --root — joining onto a subdirectory --root built paths like
215:         # web/web/src/E.tsx that silently failed to open (false green). With a
216:         # subdirectory --root, only staged files under it are scanned; and the
217:         # ignoreDirs config applies here just like in the walk branch.
218:         try:
219:             top = subprocess.run(
220:                 ["git", "-C", root, "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
221:                 capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,
222:             ).stdout.strip()
223:             out = subprocess.run(
224:                 ["git", "-C", root, "diff", "--cached", "--name-only", "--diff-filter=ACM"],
225:                 capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,
226:             ).stdout.split("\n")
227:         except (subprocess.CalledProcessError, FileNotFoundError):
228:             return []
229:         root_real = os.path.realpath(root)
230:         staged_files: List[str] = []
231:         for p in out:
232:             p = p.strip()
233:             if not p or not p.endswith(exts):
234:                 continue
235:             if set(p.split("/")) & ignore:
236:                 continue
237:             ap = os.path.realpath(os.path.join(top, p))
238:             if ap == root_real or ap.startswith(root_real + os.sep):
239:                 staged_files.append(ap)
240:         return staged_files
241: 
242:     targets = paths or [root]
243:     found: List[str] = []
244:     for t in targets:
245:         t = t if os.path.isabs(t) else os.path.join(root, t)
246:         if os.path.isfile(t):
247:             if t.endswith(exts):
248:                 found.append(t)
249:             continue
250:         for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(t):
251:             dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in ignore]
252:             for fn in filenames:
253:                 if fn.endswith(exts):
254:                     found.append(os.path.join(dirpath, fn))
255:     return found
256: 
257: 
258: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
259: # Rules
260: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
261: def analyze(strings: List[ClassString], cfg: dict, baseline: set) -> List[Finding]:
262:     rules = cfg["rules"]
263:     allow = set(normalize(a) for a in cfg.get("allowlist", []))
264:     findings: List[Finding] = []
265: 
266:     # Group by normalized key for repeated-class-string.
267:     groups: Dict[str, List[ClassString]] = {}
268:     for cs in strings:
269:         if token_count(cs.raw) < cfg["minUtilities"]:
270:             continue
271:         key = normalize(cs.raw)
272:         if key in allow:
273:             continue
274:         groups.setdefault(key, []).append(cs)
275: 
276:     if rules.get("repeated-class-string", "off") != "off":
277:         sev = rules["repeated-class-string"]
278:         for key, occ in groups.items():
279:             if len(occ) < cfg["minRepeats"]:
280:                 continue
281:             if key in baseline:
282:                 continue
283:             occ_sorted = sorted(occ, key=lambda c: (c.file, c.line))
284:             first = occ_sorted[0]
285:             findings.append(Finding(
286:                 rule="repeated-class-string", severity=sev,
287:                 file=first.file, line=first.line,
288:                 string=first.raw, count=len(occ),
289:                 occurrences=[f"{c.file}:{c.line}" for c in occ_sorted],
290:                 suggestion=(
291:                     f"this {token_count(first.raw)}-utility combo appears {len(occ)}× — "
292:                     f"extract it into a named class (@apply / a CSS component) or a shared "
293:                     f"component so the markup reads as intent, not soup"
294:                 ),
295:             ))
296: 
297:     if rules.get("long-class-string", "off") != "off":
298:         sev = rules["long-class-string"]
299:         seen_long: set = set()
300:         for cs in strings:
301:             tc = token_count(cs.raw)
302:             if tc < cfg["maxUtilities"]:
303:                 continue
304:             key = normalize(cs.raw)
305:             if key in allow or key in baseline or key in seen_long:
306:                 continue
307:             seen_long.add(key)
308:             findings.append(Finding(
309:                 rule="long-class-string", severity=sev,
310:                 file=cs.file, line=cs.line, string=cs.raw, count=1,
311:                 occurrences=[f"{cs.file}:{cs.line}"],
312:                 suggestion=(
313:                     f"{tc} utilities on one element — even once, this is hard to scan; "
314:                     f"consider a named class or splitting the element"
315:                 ),
316:             ))
317: 
318:     if rules.get("abstraction-defeat", "off") != "off" and cfg.get("namedClassPattern"):
319:         sev = rules["abstraction-defeat"]
320:         named = re.compile(cfg["namedClassPattern"])
321:         seen_def: set = set()
322:         for cs in strings:
323:             toks = [t for t in cs.raw.split() if t]
324:             has_named = any(named.match(t) for t in toks)
325:             utils = [t for t in toks if not named.match(t)]
326:             if not has_named or len(set(utils)) < cfg["minUtilities"]:
327:                 continue
328:             key = normalize(cs.raw)
329:             if key in allow or key in baseline or key in seen_def:
330:                 continue
331:             seen_def.add(key)
332:             findings.append(Finding(
333:                 rule="abstraction-defeat", severity=sev,
334:                 file=cs.file, line=cs.line, string=cs.raw, count=1,
335:                 occurrences=[f"{cs.file}:{cs.line}"],
336:                 suggestion=(
337:                     "a named/@apply class already carries this element's identity, yet "
338:                     f"{len(set(utils))} inline utilities are glued on top — fold them into "
339:                     "the named class or a variant instead of defeating the abstraction"
340:                 ),
341:             ))
342: 
343:     return findings
344: 
345: 
346: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
347: # Baseline (ratchet mode)
348: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
349: def load_baseline(path: Optional[str]) -> set:
350:     if not path or not os.path.isfile(path):
351:         return set()
352:     try:
353:         data = json.load(open(path, encoding="utf-8"))
354:         return set(data.get("normalized", []))
355:     except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):
356:         return set()
357: 
358: 
359: def write_baseline(path: str, strings: List[ClassString], cfg: dict) -> None:
360:     groups: Dict[str, int] = {}
361:     for cs in strings:
362:         if token_count(cs.raw) < cfg["minUtilities"]:
363:             continue
364:         groups[normalize(cs.raw)] = groups.get(normalize(cs.raw), 0) + 1
365:     keys = [k for k, n in groups.items() if n >= cfg["minRepeats"]]
366:     json.dump({"normalized": sorted(keys)}, open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8"), indent=2)
367: 
368: 
369: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
370: # Main
371: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
372: def main(argv: Optional[List[str]] = None) -> int:
373:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Catch utility-class soup (repeated inline class strings).")
374:     ap.add_argument("--root", default=".")
375:     ap.add_argument("--config")
376:     ap.add_argument("--staged", action="store_true")
377:     ap.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")
378:     ap.add_argument("--quiet", action="store_true")
379:     ap.add_argument("--baseline")
380:     ap.add_argument("--write-baseline", action="store_true")
381:     ap.add_argument("paths", nargs="*")
382:     args = ap.parse_args(argv)
383: 
384:     root = os.path.abspath(args.root)
385:     cfg = load_config(root, args.config)
386:     files = iter_files(root, cfg, args.paths, args.staged)
387: 
388:     all_strings: List[ClassString] = []
389:     scanned = 0
390:     for fp in files:
391:         try:
392:             text = open(fp, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").read()
393:         except OSError:
394:             continue
395:         scanned += 1
396:         rel = os.path.relpath(fp, root)
397:         all_strings.extend(extract_class_strings(text, rel))
398: 
399:     if args.write_baseline:
400:         bpath = args.baseline or os.path.join(root, ".class-guard-baseline.json")
401:         write_baseline(bpath, all_strings, cfg)
402:         print(f"class-extraction-guard: wrote baseline ({bpath})")
403:         return 0
404: 
405:     baseline = load_baseline(args.baseline or os.path.join(root, ".class-guard-baseline.json"))
406:     findings = analyze(all_strings, cfg, baseline)
407: 
408:     errors = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "error")
409:     warnings = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "warning")
410:     # scanned, not len(files): a path that failed to open was not scanned.
411:     summary = {"errors": errors, "warnings": warnings, "files_scanned": scanned}
412: 
413:     if args.json:
414:         print(json.dumps({
415:             "summary": summary,
416:             "findings": [f.__dict__ for f in findings],
417:         }, indent=2))
418:     elif not args.quiet:
419:         if not findings:
420:             print(f"class-extraction-guard: clean ({len(files)} files scanned)")
421:         else:
422:             for f in sorted(findings, key=lambda x: (-x.count, x.file)):
423:                 tag = "ERROR" if f.severity == "error" else "warn "
424:                 print(f"[{tag}] {f.rule}  {f.file}:{f.line}  (×{f.count})")
425:                 print(f"        {f.string}")
426:                 print(f"        → {f.suggestion}")
427:                 if f.count > 1:
428:                     print(f"        sites: {', '.join(f.occurrences[:8])}"
429:                           + (" …" if len(f.occurrences) > 8 else ""))
430:             print(f"\nclass-extraction-guard: {errors} error(s), {warnings} warning(s), "
431:                   f"{len(files)} files scanned")
432: 
433:     return 1 if errors > 0 else 0
434: 
435: 
436: if __name__ == "__main__":
437:     sys.exit(main())

=== skills/workflows/prose-slop-guard/scripts/check_prose_slop.py ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """check_prose_slop.py - deterministic lexical gate for AI-slop prose tells.
3: 
4: Scans prose files (Markdown by default) for the mechanical subset of AI-writing
5: tells: em/en dashes, banned vocabulary, banned phrases, negative parallelism,
6: and sycophantic filler. Judgment-level tells (rule-of-three rhythm, vague
7: attribution, inflated symbolism) are out of scope here - the SKILL.md judgment
8: pass owns those. This script is the layer a pre-commit hook or CI step can run.
9: 
10: Usage:
11:   check_prose_slop.py --root .                 # scan the tree
12:   check_prose_slop.py docs/post.md             # scan specific paths
13:   check_prose_slop.py --staged                 # only git-staged files
14:   git log -1 --format=%B | check_prose_slop.py --stdin   # commit msg / PR body
15:   check_prose_slop.py --root . --json          # machine-readable, for gates
16: 
17: Exit code 1 when error-severity findings exist, else 0. Stdlib only.
18: """
19: 
20: from __future__ import annotations
21: 
22: import argparse
23: import json
24: import re
25: import subprocess
26: import sys
27: from pathlib import Path
28: 
29: CONFIG_FILENAME = ".prose-guard.json"
30: 
31: DEFAULT_EXTENSIONS = [".md", ".mdx", ".markdown", ".txt", ".rst"]
32: DEFAULT_SKIP_PATHS = [
33:     "node_modules", ".git", "dist", "build", "out", "vendor",
34:     "CHANGELOG.md", "LICENSE", "LICENSE.md",
35: ]
36: 
37: # Severity per rule. "error" fails the gate, "warn" reports, "off" disables.
38: DEFAULT_RULES = {
39:     "no-em-dash": "error",
40:     "banned-phrases": "error",
41:     "sycophancy": "error",
42:     "banned-vocabulary": "warn",
43:     "negative-parallelism": "warn",
44: }
45: 
46: # word -> replacement hint. Word-boundary, case-insensitive.
47: BANNED_VOCABULARY = {
48:     "delve": "dig into / examine",
49:     "delves": "digs into / examines",
50:     "leverage": "use",
51:     "leverages": "uses",
52:     "leveraging": "using",
53:     "utilize": "use",
54:     "utilizes": "uses",
55:     "utilizing": "using",
56:     "seamless": "smooth (or cut it)",
57:     "seamlessly": "smoothly (or cut it)",
58:     "tapestry": "cut it - name the actual things",
59:     "unleash": "release / enable",
60:     "harness": "use",
61:     "harnessing": "using",
62:     "elevate": "improve / raise",
63:     "elevates": "improves / raises",
64:     "landscape": "field / space (or name the actual market)",
65:     "game-changer": "state the actual change",
66:     "cutting-edge": "new (or name the technique)",
67:     "transformative": "show the change instead",
68:     "groundbreaking": "new (or show what broke ground)",
69:     "paradigm": "model / approach",
70:     "synergy": "cut it - say who gains what",
71:     "empower": "let / enable",
72:     "empowers": "lets / enables",
73:     "empowering": "letting / enabling",
74:     "streamline": "simplify",
75:     "streamlines": "simplifies",
76:     "unprecedented": "new / first (only if literally true)",
77:     "testament": "evidence / sign",
78:     "pivotal": "key",
79:     "foster": "encourage / build",
80:     "fosters": "encourages / builds",
81:     "realm": "area",
82:     "embark": "start",
83:     "boasts": "has",
84:     "furthermore": "also (or just start the sentence)",
85:     "moreover": "also (or just start the sentence)",
86: }
87: 
88: # name -> (compiled regex, hint). Case-insensitive.
89: BANNED_PHRASES = [
90:     ("throat-clearing-opener",
91:      r"in today'?s (?:fast-paced|rapidly|ever-|digital|modern|dynamic|competitive)",
92:      "delete the opener; start with the actual point"),
93:     ("not-only-but-also",
94:      r"\bnot only\b.{0,80}\bbut(?: also)?\b",
95:      "make one direct claim instead of the paired construction"),
96:     ("worth-noting",
97:      r"\bit(?: is|'s) worth noting\b",
98:      "if it's worth noting, just note it"),
99:     ("testament-to",
100:      r"\ba testament to\b",
101:      "state the evidence directly"),
102:     ("look-no-further",
103:      r"\blook no further\b",
104:      "delete; recommend the thing directly"),
105:     ("lets-dive-in",
106:      r"\blet'?s dive in\b",
107:      "delete; start with the content"),
108:     ("ever-evolving",
109:      r"\bever-(?:evolving|changing|growing)\b",
110:      "name what actually changed"),
111: ]
112: 
113: SYCOPHANCY_PHRASES = [
114:     ("great-question", r"\bgreat question\b"),
115:     ("excellent-point", r"\bexcellent (?:question|point)\b"),
116:     ("absolutely-right", r"\byou'?re absolutely right\b"),
117:     ("hope-this-helps", r"\bi hope this helps\b"),
118:     ("happy-to-help", r"\b(?:i'?m )?happy to help\b"),
119: ]
120: 
121: NEGATIVE_PARALLELISM = [
122:     ("isnt-just",
123:      r"(?:\b(?:is|are|was|were)n'?t|\b(?:is|are|was|were) not|'s not|'re not)"
124:      r" (?:just|only|merely|simply)\b",
125:      "make the positive claim directly; drop the setup"),
126: ]
127: 
128: EM_DASH_RE = re.compile(r"[—–]")
129: INLINE_CODE_RE = re.compile(r"`[^`]*`")
130: LINK_TARGET_RE = re.compile(r"\]\([^)]*\)")
131: URL_RE = re.compile(r"https?://\S+")
132: HTML_COMMENT_LINE_RE = re.compile(r"<!--.*?-->")
133: 
134: 
135: def blank_out(match: re.Match) -> str:
136:     return " " * (match.end() - match.start())
137: 
138: 
139: class Finding:
140:     def __init__(self, rule, severity, path, line, col, snippet, hint):
141:         self.rule = rule
142:         self.severity = severity
143:         self.path = path
144:         self.line = line
145:         self.col = col
146:         self.snippet = snippet
147:         self.hint = hint
148: 
149:     def as_dict(self):
150:         return {
151:             "rule": self.rule, "severity": self.severity,
152:             "file": str(self.path), "line": self.line, "col": self.col,
153:             "snippet": self.snippet, "hint": self.hint,
154:         }
155: 
156: 
157: def load_config(root: Path, explicit: str | None) -> dict:
158:     path = Path(explicit) if explicit else root / CONFIG_FILENAME
159:     if path.is_file():
160:         try:
161:             return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
162:         except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
163:             print(f"warning: could not read config {path}: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
164:     return {}
165: 
166: 
167: def build_ruleset(config: dict):
168:     rules = dict(DEFAULT_RULES)
169:     rules.update(config.get("rules", {}))
170: 
171:     vocab = dict(BANNED_VOCABULARY)
172:     for word in config.get("extraVocabulary", []):
173:         vocab.setdefault(word.lower(), "rewrite in plain language")
174:     for word in config.get("allowVocabulary", []):
175:         vocab.pop(word.lower(), None)
176:     vocab_re = None
177:     if vocab:
178:         alt = "|".join(re.escape(w) for w in sorted(vocab, key=len, reverse=True))
179:         vocab_re = re.compile(rf"\b(?:{alt})\b", re.IGNORECASE)
180: 
181:     phrases = [(n, re.compile(p, re.IGNORECASE), h) for n, p, h in BANNED_PHRASES]
182:     for entry in config.get("extraPhrases", []):
183:         try:
184:             phrases.append((entry.get("name", "custom-phrase"),
185:                             re.compile(entry["pattern"], re.IGNORECASE),
186:                             entry.get("hint", "rewrite in plain language")))
187:         except (re.error, KeyError) as exc:
188:             print(f"warning: bad extraPhrases entry {entry}: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
189: 
190:     syco = [(n, re.compile(p, re.IGNORECASE)) for n, p in SYCOPHANCY_PHRASES]
191:     negp = [(n, re.compile(p, re.IGNORECASE), h) for n, p, h in NEGATIVE_PARALLELISM]
192:     return rules, vocab, vocab_re, phrases, syco, negp
193: 
194: 
195: def iter_prose_lines(text: str, config: dict):
196:     """Yield (lineno, scannable_line). Skips fenced code, frontmatter,
197:     blockquotes (quoted content is exempt by default), and blanks out
198:     inline code, link targets, URLs, and HTML comments."""
199:     exempt_quotes = config.get("exemptBlockquotes", True)
200:     scan_frontmatter = config.get("scanFrontmatter", False)
201:     lines = text.splitlines()
202:     in_fence = False
203:     fence_marker = ""
204:     in_frontmatter = False
205:     for idx, raw in enumerate(lines, start=1):
206:         stripped = raw.lstrip()
207:         if idx == 1 and raw.strip() == "---" and not scan_frontmatter:
208:             in_frontmatter = True
209:             continue
210:         if in_frontmatter:
211:             if raw.strip() in ("---", "..."):
212:                 in_frontmatter = False
213:             continue
214:         if stripped.startswith("```") or stripped.startswith("~~~"):
215:             marker = stripped[:3]
216:             if not in_fence:
217:                 in_fence, fence_marker = True, marker
218:             elif marker == fence_marker:
219:                 in_fence = False
220:             continue
221:         if in_fence:
222:             continue
223:         if exempt_quotes and stripped.startswith(">"):
224:             continue
225:         line = INLINE_CODE_RE.sub(blank_out, raw)
226:         line = LINK_TARGET_RE.sub(blank_out, line)
227:         line = URL_RE.sub(blank_out, line)
228:         line = HTML_COMMENT_LINE_RE.sub(blank_out, line)
229:         yield idx, line
230: 
231: 
232: def scan_text(text: str, path, rules, vocab, vocab_re, phrases, syco, negp, config):
233:     findings = []
234: 
235:     def add(rule, line, col, snippet, hint):
236:         sev = rules.get(rule, "off")
237:         if sev != "off":
238:             findings.append(Finding(rule, sev, path, line, col, snippet.strip(), hint))
239: 
240:     for lineno, line in iter_prose_lines(text, config):
241:         for m in EM_DASH_RE.finditer(line):
242:             add("no-em-dash", lineno, m.start() + 1, line,
243:                 "replace with ' - ', a comma, or parentheses")
244:         if vocab_re:
245:             for m in vocab_re.finditer(line):
246:                 word = m.group(0).lower()
247:                 add("banned-vocabulary", lineno, m.start() + 1, line,
248:                     f"'{m.group(0)}' is an AI-vocabulary tell; try: {vocab.get(word, 'plain language')}")
249:         for name, rx, hint in phrases:
250:             for m in rx.finditer(line):
251:                 add("banned-phrases", lineno, m.start() + 1, line, f"{name}: {hint}")
252:         for name, rx in syco:
253:             for m in rx.finditer(line):
254:                 add("sycophancy", lineno, m.start() + 1, line,
255:                     f"{name}: delete the filler; answer directly")
256:         for name, rx, hint in negp:
257:             for m in rx.finditer(line):
258:                 add("negative-parallelism", lineno, m.start() + 1, line, f"{name}: {hint}")
259:     return findings
260: 
261: 
262: def collect_files(root: Path, paths, staged: bool, config: dict):
263:     extensions = config.get("extensions", DEFAULT_EXTENSIONS)
264:     skip = config.get("skipPaths", DEFAULT_SKIP_PATHS)
265: 
266:     def skipped(p: Path) -> bool:
267:         parts = set(p.parts)
268:         return any(s in parts or p.name == s for s in skip)
269: 
270:     if staged:
271:         try:
272:             out = subprocess.run(
273:                 ["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only", "--diff-filter=ACMR"],
274:                 capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=root, check=True,
275:             ).stdout
276:         except (subprocess.CalledProcessError, FileNotFoundError) as exc:
277:             print(f"error: --staged requires git: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
278:             sys.exit(2)
279:         candidates = [root / line for line in out.splitlines() if line.strip()]
280:     elif paths:
281:         candidates = []
282:         for p in paths:
283:             pp = Path(p)
284:             candidates.extend(pp.rglob("*") if pp.is_dir() else [pp])
285:     else:
286:         candidates = list(root.rglob("*"))
287: 
288:     return sorted(
289:         p for p in candidates
290:         if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in extensions and not skipped(p.relative_to(root) if p.is_absolute() and root in p.parents else p)
291:     )
292: 
293: 
294: def render_report(findings):
295:     by_rule = {}
296:     for f in findings:
297:         by_rule.setdefault((f.rule, f.severity), []).append(f)
298:     for (rule, sev), items in sorted(by_rule.items()):
299:         print(f"{rule} ({sev}) - {len(items)}")
300:         for f in items:
301:             print(f"  {f.path}:{f.line}:{f.col}")
302:             print(f"    {f.snippet[:120]}")
303:             print(f"    -> {f.hint}")
304:     errors = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "error")
305:     warns = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "warn")
306:     print(f"\n{errors} error(s), {warns} warning(s)"
307:           if findings else "clean: no slop tells found")
308:     return errors
309: 
310: 
311: def main() -> int:
312:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
313:     ap.add_argument("paths", nargs="*", help="files/dirs to scan (default: --root tree)")
314:     ap.add_argument("--root", default=".", help="project root (config + default scan)")
315:     ap.add_argument("--config", help="explicit config path")
316:     ap.add_argument("--staged", action="store_true", help="only git-staged files")
317:     ap.add_argument("--stdin", action="store_true", help="scan text from stdin (commit msg, PR body)")
318:     ap.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="machine-readable output")
319:     args = ap.parse_args()
320: 
321:     root = Path(args.root).resolve()
322:     config = load_config(root, args.config)
323:     ruleset = build_ruleset(config)
324:     rules, vocab, vocab_re, phrases, syco, negp = ruleset
325: 
326:     findings = []
327:     if args.stdin:
328:         findings = scan_text(sys.stdin.read(), "<stdin>", rules, vocab, vocab_re,
329:                              phrases, syco, negp, config)
330:     else:
331:         for path in collect_files(root, args.paths, args.staged, config):
332:             try:
333:                 text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
334:             except OSError as exc:
335:                 print(f"warning: could not read {path}: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
336:                 continue
337:             findings.extend(scan_text(text, path, rules, vocab, vocab_re,
338:                                       phrases, syco, negp, config))
339: 
340:     if args.json:
341:         errors = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "error")
342:         warns = sum(1 for f in findings if f.severity == "warn")
343:         print(json.dumps({
344:             "ok": errors == 0,
345:             "summary": {"errors": errors, "warnings": warns, "total": len(findings)},
346:             "findings": [f.as_dict() for f in findings],
347:         }, indent=2))
348:         return 1 if errors else 0
349: 
350:     errors = render_report(findings)
351:     return 1 if errors else 0
352: 
353: 
354: if __name__ == "__main__":
355:     sys.exit(main())

=== skills/workflows/skill-creator/scripts/run_eval.py ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """Run trigger evaluation for a skill description using Hermes CLI.
3: 
4: Tests whether a skill's description causes Hermes to trigger (read the skill)
5: for a set of queries. Outputs results as JSON.
6: 
7: Usage:
8:     python -m scripts.run_eval --eval-set <path> --skill-path <path> [options]
9: 
10: This script calls `hermes chat -q --oneshot -Q` for each query, creating a
11: temporary skill to test triggering. It detects skill usage by checking if
12: the skill's name appears in the session output or if a marker file was created.
13: """
14: 
15: import argparse
16: import json
17: import os
18: import subprocess
19: import sys
20: import tempfile
21: import time
22: import uuid
23: from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
24: from pathlib import Path
25: 
26: from scripts.utils import parse_skill_md
27: 
28: 
29: def find_project_root() -> Path:
30:     """Find the project root by walking up from cwd looking for .hermes/ or .git."""
31:     current = Path.cwd()
32:     for parent in [current, *current.parents]:
33:         if (parent / ".hermes").is_dir() or (parent / ".git").is_dir():
34:             return parent
35:     return current
36: 
37: 
38: def run_single_query(
39:     query: str,
40:     skill_name: str,
41:     skill_path: str,
42:     timeout: int,
43: ) -> bool:
44:     """Run a single query and return whether the skill was triggered.
45: 
46:     Creates a temporary copy of the skill with a unique name, runs the query
47:     with the skill loaded, and checks if the skill was invoked by examining
48:     the output for indicators.
49:     """
50:     unique_id = uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]
51:     test_skill_name = f"{skill_name}-test-{unique_id}"
52: 
53:     # Create a temporary skill directory
54:     tmp_skill_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()) / test_skill_name
55:     try:
56:         # Copy the skill
57:         subprocess.run(["cp", "-r", skill_path, str(tmp_skill_dir)], check=True)
58: 
59:         # Rename the skill in SKILL.md for unique detection
60:         skill_md_path = tmp_skill_dir / "SKILL.md"
61:         content = skill_md_path.read_text()
62:         content = content.replace(f"name: {skill_name}", f"name: {test_skill_name}", 1)
63:         skill_md_path.write_text(content)
64: 
65:         # Install the test skill temporarily
66:         install_result = subprocess.run(
67:             ["hermes", "skills", "install", str(tmp_skill_dir), "--yes"],
68:             capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30,
69:         )
70:         if install_result.returncode != 0:
71:             print(f"Warning: failed to install test skill: {install_result.stderr}", file=sys.stderr)
72:             return False
73: 
74:         # Run the query with the skill loaded
75:         cmd = [
76:             "hermes", "chat", "-q", query,
77:             "--oneshot", "-Q",
78:             "-s", test_skill_name,
79:         ]
80: 
81:         env = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items()}
82:         process = subprocess.run(
83:             cmd,
84:             capture_output=True, text=True,
85:             env=env,
86:             timeout=timeout,
87:         )
88: 
89:         output = process.stdout + process.stderr
90: 
91:         # Check if the skill was triggered by looking for the skill name in output
92:         # or checking if the skill's reference files were accessed
93:         triggered = test_skill_name.lower() in output.lower()
94: 
95:         # More reliable: check session output for skill indicators
96:         # Skills add their context to the agent's system prompt - look for signs
97:         if not triggered:
98:             triggered = skill_name.lower() in output.lower()
99: 
100:         return triggered
101: 
102:     except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
103:         print(f"Warning: query timed out after {timeout}s", file=sys.stderr)
104:         return False
105:     except Exception as e:
106:         print(f"Warning: query failed: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
107:         return False
108:     finally:
109:         # Clean up: uninstall the test skill and remove temp directory
110:         try:
111:             subprocess.run(
112:                 ["hermes", "skills", "uninstall", test_skill_name, "--yes"],
113:                 capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30,
114:             )
115:         except Exception:
116:             pass
117:         try:
118:             import shutil
119:             shutil.rmtree(tmp_skill_dir.parent, ignore_errors=True)
120:         except Exception:
121:             pass
122: 
123: 
124: def run_eval(
125:     eval_set: list[dict],
126:     skill_name: str,
127:     description: str,
128:     skill_path: str,
129:     num_workers: int,
130:     timeout: int,
131:     runs_per_query: int = 1,
132:     trigger_threshold: float = 0.5,
133: ) -> dict:
134:     """Run the full eval set and return results."""
135:     results = []
136: 
137:     with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=num_workers) as executor:
138:         future_to_info = {}
139:         for item in eval_set:
140:             for run_idx in range(runs_per_query):
141:                 future = executor.submit(
142:                     run_single_query,
143:                     item["query"],
144:                     skill_name,
145:                     skill_path,
146:                     timeout,
147:                 )
148:                 future_to_info[future] = (item, run_idx)
149: 
150:         query_triggers: dict[str, list[bool]] = {}
151:         query_items: dict[str, dict] = {}
152:         for future in as_completed(future_to_info):
153:             item, _ = future_to_info[future]
154:             query = item["query"]
155:             query_items[query] = item
156:             if query not in query_triggers:
157:                 query_triggers[query] = []
158:             try:
159:                 query_triggers[query].append(future.result())
160:             except Exception as e:
161:                 print(f"Warning: query failed: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
162:                 query_triggers[query].append(False)
163: 
164:     for query, triggers in query_triggers.items():
165:         item = query_items[query]
166:         trigger_rate = sum(triggers) / len(triggers)
167:         should_trigger = item["should_trigger"]
168:         if should_trigger:
169:             did_pass = trigger_rate >= trigger_threshold
170:         else:
171:             did_pass = trigger_rate < trigger_threshold
172:         results.append({
173:             "query": query,
174:             "should_trigger": should_trigger,
175:             "trigger_rate": trigger_rate,
176:             "triggers": sum(triggers),
177:             "runs": len(triggers),
178:             "pass": did_pass,
179:         })
180: 
181:     passed = sum(1 for r in results if r["pass"])
182:     total = len(results)
183: 
184:     return {
185:         "skill_name": skill_name,
186:         "description": description,
187:         "results": results,
188:         "summary": {
189:             "total": total,
190:             "passed": passed,
191:             "failed": total - passed,
192:         },
193:     }
194: 
195: 
196: def main():
197:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Run trigger evaluation for a skill description")
198:     parser.add_argument("--eval-set", required=True, help="Path to eval set JSON file")
199:     parser.add_argument("--skill-path", required=True, help="Path to skill directory")
200:     parser.add_argument("--description", default=None, help="Override description to test")
201:     parser.add_argument("--num-workers", type=int, default=5, help="Number of parallel workers")
202:     parser.add_argument("--timeout", type=int, default=60, help="Timeout per query in seconds")
203:     parser.add_argument("--runs-per-query", type=int, default=3, help="Number of runs per query")
204:     parser.add_argument("--trigger-threshold", type=float, default=0.5, help="Trigger rate threshold")
205:     parser.add_argument("--verbose", action="store_true", help="Print progress to stderr")
206:     args = parser.parse_args()
207: 
208:     eval_set = json.loads(Path(args.eval_set).read_text())
209:     skill_path = Path(args.skill_path)
210: 
211:     if not (skill_path / "SKILL.md").exists():
212:         print(f"Error: No SKILL.md found at {skill_path}", file=sys.stderr)
213:         sys.exit(1)
214: 
215:     name, original_description, content = parse_skill_md(skill_path)
216:     description = args.description or original_description
217: 
218:     if args.verbose:
219:         print(f"Evaluating: {description}", file=sys.stderr)
220: 
221:     output = run_eval(
222:         eval_set=eval_set,
223:         skill_name=name,
224:         description=description,
225:         skill_path=str(skill_path),
226:         num_workers=args.num_workers,
227:         timeout=args.timeout,
228:         runs_per_query=args.runs_per_query,
229:         trigger_threshold=args.trigger_threshold,
230:     )
231: 
232:     if args.verbose:
233:         summary = output["summary"]
234:         print(f"Results: {summary['passed']}/{summary['total']} passed", file=sys.stderr)
235:         for r in output["results"]:
236:             status = "PASS" if r["pass"] else "FAIL"
237:             rate_str = f"{r['triggers']}/{r['runs']}"
238:             print(f"  [{status}] rate={rate_str} expected={r['should_trigger']}: {r['query'][:70]}", file=sys.stderr)
239: 
240:     print(json.dumps(output, indent=2))
241: 
242: 
243: if __name__ == "__main__":
244:     main()

=== skills/workflows/skill-creator/scripts/run_loop.py ===
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """Run the eval + improve loop until all pass or max iterations reached.
3: 
4: Combines run_eval.py and improve_description.py in a loop, tracking history
5: and returning the best description found. Supports train/test split to prevent
6: overfitting.
7: 
8: Usage:
9:     python -m scripts.run_loop --eval-set <path> --skill-path <path> --model <model>
10: """
11: 
12: import argparse
13: import json
14: import random
15: import sys
16: import tempfile
17: import time
18: import webbrowser
19: from pathlib import Path
20: 
21: from scripts.generate_report import generate_html
22: from scripts.improve_description import improve_description
23: from scripts.run_eval import run_eval
24: from scripts.utils import parse_skill_md
25: 
26: 
27: def split_eval_set(eval_set: list[dict], holdout: float, seed: int = 42) -> tuple[list[dict], list[dict]]:
28:     """Split eval set into train and test sets, stratified by should_trigger."""
29:     random.seed(seed)
30: 
31:     trigger = [e for e in eval_set if e["should_trigger"]]
32:     no_trigger = [e for e in eval_set if not e["should_trigger"]]
33: 
34:     random.shuffle(trigger)
35:     random.shuffle(no_trigger)
36: 
37:     n_trigger_test = max(1, int(len(trigger) * holdout))
38:     n_no_trigger_test = max(1, int(len(no_trigger) * holdout))
39: 
40:     test_set = trigger[:n_trigger_test] + no_trigger[:n_no_trigger_test]
41:     train_set = trigger[n_trigger_test:] + no_trigger[n_no_trigger_test:]
42: 
43:     return train_set, test_set
44: 
45: 
46: def run_loop(
47:     eval_set: list[dict],
48:     skill_path: Path,
49:     description_override: str | None,
50:     num_workers: int,
51:     timeout: int,
52:     max_iterations: int,
53:     runs_per_query: int,
54:     trigger_threshold: float,
55:     holdout: float,
56:     model: str,
57:     verbose: bool,
58:     live_report_path: Path | None = None,
59:     log_dir: Path | None = None,
60: ) -> dict:
61:     """Run the eval + improvement loop."""
62:     name, original_description, content = parse_skill_md(skill_path)
63:     current_description = description_override or original_description
64: 
65:     if holdout > 0:
66:         train_set, test_set = split_eval_set(eval_set, holdout)
67:         if verbose:
68:             print(f"Split: {len(train_set)} train, {len(test_set)} test (holdout={holdout})", file=sys.stderr)
69:     else:
70:         train_set = eval_set
71:         test_set = []
72: 
73:     history = []
74:     exit_reason = "unknown"
75: 
76:     for iteration in range(1, max_iterations + 1):
77:         if verbose:
78:             print(f"\n{'='*60}", file=sys.stderr)
79:             print(f"Iteration {iteration}/{max_iterations}", file=sys.stderr)
80:             print(f"Description: {current_description}", file=sys.stderr)
81:             print(f"{'='*60}", file=sys.stderr)
82: 
83:         all_queries = train_set + test_set
84:         t0 = time.time()
85:         all_results = run_eval(
86:             eval_set=all_queries,
87:             skill_name=name,
88:             description=current_description,
89:             skill_path=str(skill_path),
90:             num_workers=num_workers,
91:             timeout=timeout,
92:             runs_per_query=runs_per_query,
93:             trigger_threshold=trigger_threshold,
94:         )
95:         eval_elapsed = time.time() - t0
96: 
97:         train_queries_set = {q["query"] for q in train_set}
98:         train_result_list = [r for r in all_results["results"] if r["query"] in train_queries_set]
99:         test_result_list = [r for r in all_results["results"] if r["query"] not in train_queries_set]
100: 
101:         train_passed = sum(1 for r in train_result_list if r["pass"])
102:         train_total = len(train_result_list)
103:         train_summary = {"passed": train_passed, "failed": train_total - train_passed, "total": train_total}
104:         train_results = {"results": train_result_list, "summary": train_summary}
105: 
106:         if test_set:
107:             test_passed = sum(1 for r in test_result_list if r["pass"])
108:             test_total = len(test_result_list)
109:             test_summary = {"passed": test_passed, "failed": test_total - test_passed, "total": test_total}
110:             test_results = {"results": test_result_list, "summary": test_summary}
111:         else:
112:             test_results = None
113:             test_summary = None
114: 
115:         history.append({
116:             "iteration": iteration,
117:             "description": current_description,
118:             "train_passed": train_summary["passed"],
119:             "train_failed": train_summary["failed"],
120:             "train_total": train_summary["total"],
121:             "train_results": train_results["results"],
122:             "test_passed": test_summary["passed"] if test_summary else None,
123:             "test_failed": test_summary["failed"] if test_summary else None,
124:             "test_total": test_summary["total"] if test_summary else None,
125:             "test_results": test_results["results"] if test_results else None,
126:             "passed": train_summary["passed"],
127:             "failed": train_summary["failed"],
128:             "total": train_summary["total"],
129:             "results": train_results["results"],
130:         })
131: 
132:         if live_report_path:
133:             partial_output = {
134:                 "original_description": original_description,
135:                 "best_description": current_description,
136:                 "best_score": "in progress",
137:                 "iterations_run": len(history),
138:                 "holdout": holdout,
139:                 "train_size": len(train_set),
140:                 "test_size": len(test_set),
141:                 "history": history,
142:             }
143:             live_report_path.write_text(generate_html(partial_output, auto_refresh=True, skill_name=name))
144: 
145:         if verbose:
146:             def print_eval_stats(label, results, elapsed):
147:                 pos = [r for r in results if r["should_trigger"]]
148:                 neg = [r for r in results if not r["should_trigger"]]
149:                 tp = sum(r["triggers"] for r in pos)
150:                 pos_runs = sum(r["runs"] for r in pos)
151:                 fn = pos_runs - tp
152:                 fp = sum(r["triggers"] for r in neg)
153:                 neg_runs = sum(r["runs"] for r in neg)
154:                 tn = neg_runs - fp
155:                 total = tp + tn + fp + fn
156:                 precision = tp / (tp + fp) if (tp + fp) > 0 else 1.0
157:                 recall = tp / (tp + fn) if (tp + fn) > 0 else 1.0
158:                 accuracy = (tp + tn) / total if total > 0 else 0.0
159:                 print(f"{label}: {tp+tn}/{total} correct, precision={precision:.0%} recall={recall:.0%} accuracy={accuracy:.0%} ({elapsed:.1f}s)", file=sys.stderr)
160:                 for r in results:
161:                     status = "PASS" if r["pass"] else "FAIL"
162:                     rate_str = f"{r['triggers']}/{r['runs']}"
163:                     print(f"  [{status}] rate={rate_str} expected={r['should_trigger']}: {r['query'][:60]}", file=sys.stderr)
164: 
165:             print_eval_stats("Train", train_results["results"], eval_elapsed)
166:             if test_summary:
167:                 print_eval_stats("Test ", test_results["results"], 0)
168: 
169:         if train_summary["failed"] == 0:
170:             exit_reason = f"all_passed (iteration {iteration})"
171:             if verbose:
172:                 print(f"\nAll train queries passed on iteration {iteration}!", file=sys.stderr)
173:             break
174: 
175:         if iteration == max_iterations:
176:             exit_reason = f"max_iterations ({max_iterations})"
177:             if verbose:
178:                 print(f"\nMax iterations reached ({max_iterations}).", file=sys.stderr)
179:             break
180: 
181:         if verbose:
182:             print(f"\nImproving description...", file=sys.stderr)
183: 
184:         t0 = time.time()
185:         blinded_history = [
186:             {k: v for k, v in h.items() if not k.startswith("test_")}
187:             for h in history
188:         ]
189:         new_description = improve_description(
190:             skill_name=name,
191:             skill_content=content,
192:             current_description=current_description,
193:             eval_results=train_results,
194:             history=blinded_history,
195:             model=model,
196:             log_dir=log_dir,
197:             iteration=iteration,
198:         )
199:         improve_elapsed = time.time() - t0
200: 
201:         if verbose:
202:             print(f"Proposed ({improve_elapsed:.1f}s): {new_description}", file=sys.stderr)
203: 
204:         current_description = new_description
205: 
206:     if test_set:
207:         best = max(history, key=lambda h: h["test_passed"] or 0)
208:         best_score = f"{best['test_passed']}/{best['test_total']}"
209:     else:
210:         best = max(history, key=lambda h: h["train_passed"])
211:         best_score = f"{best['train_passed']}/{best['train_total']}"
212: 
213:     if verbose:
214:         print(f"\nExit reason: {exit_reason}", file=sys.stderr)
215:         print(f"Best score: {best_score} (iteration {best['iteration']})", file=sys.stderr)
216: 
217:     return {
218:         "exit_reason": exit_reason,
219:         "original_description": original_description,
220:         "best_description": best["description"],
221:         "best_score": best_score,
222:         "best_train_score": f"{best['train_passed']}/{best['train_total']}",
223:         "best_test_score": f"{best['test_passed']}/{best['test_total']}" if test_set else None,
224:         "final_description": current_description,
225:         "iterations_run": len(history),
226:         "holdout": holdout,
227:         "train_size": len(train_set),
228:         "test_size": len(test_set),
229:         "history": history,
230:     }
231: 
232: 
233: def main():
234:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Run eval + improve loop")
235:     parser.add_argument("--eval-set", required=True, help="Path to eval set JSON file")
236:     parser.add_argument("--skill-path", required=True, help="Path to skill directory")
237:     parser.add_argument("--description", default=None, help="Override starting description")
238:     parser.add_argument("--num-workers", type=int, default=5, help="Number of parallel workers")
239:     parser.add_argument("--timeout", type=int, default=60, help="Timeout per query in seconds")
240:     parser.add_argument("--max-iterations", type=int, default=5, help="Max improvement iterations")
241:     parser.add_argument("--runs-per-query", type=int, default=3, help="Number of runs per query")
242:     parser.add_argument("--trigger-threshold", type=float, default=0.5, help="Trigger rate threshold")
243:     parser.add_argument("--holdout", type=float, default=0.4, help="Fraction of eval set to hold out for testing (0 to disable)")
244:     parser.add_argument("--model", required=True, help="Model for improvement")
245:     parser.add_argument("--verbose", action="store_true", help="Print progress to stderr")
246:     parser.add_argument("--report", default="auto", help="Generate HTML report at this path (default: 'auto' for temp file, 'none' to disable)")
247:     parser.add_argument("--results-dir", default=None, help="Save all outputs to a timestamped subdirectory here")
248:     args = parser.parse_args()
249: 
250:     eval_set = json.loads(Path(args.eval_set).read_text())
251:     skill_path = Path(args.skill_path)
252: 
253:     if not (skill_path / "SKILL.md").exists():
254:         print(f"Error: No SKILL.md found at {skill_path}", file=sys.stderr)
255:         sys.exit(1)
256: 
257:     name, _, _ = parse_skill_md(skill_path)
258: 
259:     if args.report != "none":
260:         if args.report == "auto":
261:             timestamp = time.strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
262:             live_report_path = Path(tempfile.gettempdir()) / f"skill_description_report_{skill_path.name}_{timestamp}.html"
263:         else:
264:             live_report_path = Path(args.report)
265:         live_report_path.write_text("<html><body><h1>Starting optimization loop...</h1><meta http-equiv='refresh' content='5'></body></html>")
266:         webbrowser.open(str(live_report_path))
267:     else:
268:         live_report_path = None
269: 
270:     if args.results_dir:
271:         timestamp = time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d_%H%M%S")
272:         results_dir = Path(args.results_dir) / timestamp
273:         results_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
274:     else:
275:         results_dir = None
276: 
277:     log_dir = results_dir / "logs" if results_dir else None
278: 
279:     output = run_loop(
280:         eval_set=eval_set,
281:         skill_path=skill_path,
282:         description_override=args.description,
283:         num_workers=args.num_workers,
284:         timeout=args.timeout,
285:         max_iterations=args.max_iterations,
286:         runs_per_query=args.runs_per_query,
287:         trigger_threshold=args.trigger_threshold,
288:         holdout=args.holdout,
289:         model=args.model,
290:         verbose=args.verbose,
291:         live_report_path=live_report_path,
292:         log_dir=log_dir,
293:     )
294: 
295:     json_output = json.dumps(output, indent=2)
296:     print(json_output)
297:     if results_dir:
298:         (results_dir / "results.json").write_text(json_output)
299: 
300:     if live_report_path:
301:         live_report_path.write_text(generate_html(output, auto_refresh=False, skill_name=name))
302:         print(f"\nReport: {live_report_path}", file=sys.stderr)
303: 
304:     if results_dir and live_report_path:
305:         (results_dir / "report.html").write_text(generate_html(output, auto_refresh=False, skill_name=name))
306: 
307:     if results_dir:
308:         print(f"Results saved to: {results_dir}", file=sys.stderr)
309: 
310: 
311: if __name__ == "__main__":
312:     main()

=== skills/meta/model-adaptation/references/long-run-hygiene.md ===
1: # Long-run hygiene for the Claude 5 family
2: 
3: The behavioral patterns a long-running or autonomous agent needs on the Claude 5 family
4: (Fable 5 + Mythos 5) that the shorter-turn era didn't. Each entry gives *what changed*, a
5: **drop-in instruction** (verbatim from Anthropic where they published one), and *where it
6: plugs into* `loop-controller`'s 5-part loop contract (trigger / action / proof / memory /
7: stop). Read when authoring or migrating any loop or autonomous harness.
8: 
9: Anthropic source: [Prompting Claude Fable 5](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/prompting-claude-fable-5).
10: 
11: ## Contents
12: 
13: 1. Longer turns by default (async harness)
14: 2. Effort as the primary dial
15: 3. Ground progress claims (anti-fabrication)
16: 4. Don't end a turn on a promise (early-stopping guard)
17: 5. Context-budget reassurance
18: 6. Fresh-context verifier (already in `loop-controller`)
19: 7. Send-to-user tool
20: 8. Give the reason, not only the request
21: 9. Readability when talking to the user
22: 10. How this maps onto the 5-part loop contract
23: 
24: ---
25: 
26: ## 1. Longer turns by default (async harness)
27: 
28: **What changed.** A single request on a hard task can run for many minutes at higher
29: effort; autonomous runs can extend for hours. Blocking a client on that is the biggest
30: harness shift teams hit.
31: 
32: **The move.** Adjust client timeouts, streaming, and user-facing progress indicators
33: before migrating. Restructure harnesses to check runs **asynchronously** (scheduled jobs,
34: polls) rather than blocking until the run returns. In this toolkit that's already the
35: `/loop` (watch/poll) primitive and `/schedule` — prefer them over a blocking wait for any
36: run that can take minutes.
37: 
38: **To keep the model from overplanning an ambiguous task** (drop-in):
39: 
40: ```text
41: When you have enough information to act, act. Do not re-derive facts already established
42: in the conversation, re-litigate a decision the user has already made, or narrate options
43: you will not pursue in user-facing messages. If you are weighing a choice, give a
44: recommendation, not an exhaustive survey. This does not apply to thinking blocks.
45: ```
46: 
47: **Plugs into:** `trigger`/`stop` — poll-style triggers and async check-ins instead of one
48: blocking turn.
49: 
50: ## 2. Effort as the primary dial
51: 
52: **What changed.** Effort is the primary control for the intelligence/latency/cost
53: trade-off. Lower effort on the Claude 5 family still performs well and often exceeds
54: `xhigh` on prior models.
55: 
56: **The move.** `high` is the default; `xhigh` for the most capability-sensitive work;
57: `medium`/`low` for routine. Reduce effort if a task completes but takes longer than
58: necessary, or when you want a quicker, more interactive style. In a multi-agent build, set
59: effort per role: `xhigh` for contracts and adversarial verification, `low`/`medium` for
60: docs and mechanical edits.
61: 
62: **To prevent unrequested tidying/refactoring at higher effort** (drop-in):
63: 
64: ```text
65: Don't add features, refactor, or introduce abstractions beyond what the task requires. A
66: bug fix doesn't need surrounding cleanup and a one-shot operation usually doesn't need a
67: helper. Don't design for hypothetical future requirements: do the simplest thing that
68: works well. Don't add error handling, fallbacks, or validation for scenarios that cannot
69: happen. Only validate at system boundaries (user input, external APIs).
70: ```
71: 
72: **Plugs into:** `action` — the per-iteration cost/quality knob; also a `stop`-budget lever
73: (lower effort = cheaper iterations).
74: 
75: ## 3. Ground progress claims (anti-fabrication)
76: 
77: **What changed.** On long autonomous runs the model can emit *fabricated* status reports —
78: claiming work it didn't verify. This is distinct from the anti-*gamed-gate* discipline
79: loops already have (that guards the mechanical proof; this guards the model's narration).
80: 
81: **The move (drop-in):**
82: 
83: ```text
84: Before reporting progress, audit each claim against a tool result from this session. Only
85: report work you can point to evidence for; if something is not yet verified, say so
86: explicitly. Report outcomes faithfully: if tests fail, say so with the output; if a step
87: was skipped, say that; when something is done and verified, state it plainly without hedging.
88: ```
89: 
90: **Plugs into:** `proof`/`memory` — every progress line in `PROGRESS.md` must trace to a
91: tool result, not a hope. Complements Step 2's "measure the goal, not a stand-in".
92: 
93: ## 4. Don't end a turn on a promise (early-stopping guard)
94: 
95: **What changed.** Deep into a session the model can end a turn with a text-only statement
96: of intent ("I'll now run X") *without* issuing the tool call, or pause to ask permission
97: when it already has enough to proceed.
98: 
99: **The move.** `loop-controller` handles this *mechanically* today (`/goal` re-prompts,
100: Stop-hooks block premature exit). Add the *prompt-level* self-check too, so the model
101: doesn't rely on the hook:
102: 
103: ```text
104: Before ending your turn, check your last paragraph. If it is a plan, an analysis, a
105: question, a list of next steps, or a promise about work you have not done ("I'll…", "let
106: me know when…"), do that work now with tool calls. End your turn only when the task is
107: complete or you are blocked on input only the user can provide.
108: ```
109: 
110: **For fully autonomous pipelines**, add the operating-context reminder (drop-in):
111: 
112: ```text
113: You are operating autonomously. The user is not watching in real time and cannot answer
114: questions mid-task, so asking "Want me to…?" will block the work. For reversible actions
115: that follow from the original request, proceed without asking. Offering follow-ups after
116: the task is done is fine; asking permission before doing agreed work is not.
117: ```
118: 
119: **Plugs into:** `stop` — pairs with the checkpoint rule so the loop stops *only* where a
120: human is genuinely required (see the HITL-before-irreversible guardrail), not on a
121: self-manufactured pause.
122: 
123: ## 5. Context-budget reassurance
124: 
125: **What changed.** In very long sessions the model can suggest a new session, offer to
126: summarize and hand off, or trim its own work — most often when the harness shows it a
127: **remaining-token countdown**.
128: 
129: **The move.** Avoid surfacing explicit context-budget counts to the model where possible.
130: This is a real tension for loops: `loop-controller`'s budget guardrail watches `/cost` for
131: *enforcement*, but that number should drive the harness's stop decision, **not** be shown
132: to the model as a countdown. If the harness must show it, add reassurance (drop-in):
133: 
134: ```text
135: You have ample context remaining. Do not stop, summarize, or suggest a new session on
136: account of context limits. Continue the work.
137: ```
138: 
139: **Plugs into:** `stop`/`memory` — budget enforcement stays a *harness* decision reading
140: externalized state; the model keeps working. (Externalizing state to disk, which loops
141: already do, is what makes it safe to *not* show the model the countdown.)
142: 
143: ## 6. Fresh-context verifier (already in `loop-controller`)
144: 
145: **What changed.** Separate, fresh-context verifier subagents outperform self-critique.
146: 
147: **The move.** This is already `loop-controller` Step 2's **fresh-context evaluator** — the
148: GAN / Plan-Generate-Evaluate pattern, spawned with no Write/Edit tools, blind to how the
149: work was built. Nothing to add; cite it. For long runs, make verification *periodic*:
150: 
151: ```text
152: Establish a method for checking your own work at an interval as you build. Run this every
