153: [interval], verifying your work with subagents against the specification.
154: ```
155: 
156: **Plugs into:** `proof` — the subjective-bar grader, run at intervals, not just at the end.
157: 
158: ## 7. Send-to-user tool
159: 
160: **What changed.** Long async agents need to surface a message the user must see *exactly as
161: written* — a deliverable, a numeric progress update, a direct answer — **without ending the
162: turn**. Tool inputs are never summarized, so content routed through a tool arrives intact.
163: 
164: **The move.** Give the agent a client-side tool; render its input directly in the UI and
165: return a simple ack:
166: 
167: ```json
168: {
169:   "name": "send_to_user",
170:   "description": "Display a message directly to the user. Use for progress updates, partial results, or content the user must see exactly as written before the task finishes.",
171:   "input_schema": {
172:     "type": "object",
173:     "properties": {
174:       "message": { "type": "string", "description": "The content to display to the user." }
175:     },
176:     "required": ["message"]
177:   }
178: }
179: ```
180: 
181: Defining the tool is not enough — without an instruction the model rarely calls it. Pair it
182: with elicitation language:
183: 
184: ```text
185: Between tool calls, when you have content the user must read verbatim (a partial
186: deliverable, a direct answer to their question), call the send_to_user tool with that
187: content. Use send_to_user only for user-facing content, not for narration or reasoning.
188: ```
189: 
190: **Note the interaction with refusals:** route *content* here, never a transcript of the
191: model's reasoning — that's the `reasoning_extraction` landmine (see `refusal-and-fallback.md`).
192: 
193: **Plugs into:** `action` — mid-turn user surfacing without breaking the loop; the async
194: complement to a loop that otherwise only speaks by ending its turn for HITL.
195: 
196: ## 8. Give the reason, not only the request
197: 
198: **What changed.** The model performs better when it understands *intent* — context lets it
199: connect the task to relevant information instead of guessing.
200: 
201: **The move (drop-in framing):**
202: 
203: ```text
204: I'm working on [the larger task] for [who it's for]. They need [what the output enables].
205: With that in mind: [request].
206: ```
207: 
208: **Plugs into:** the loop's `trigger` framing and every subagent brief — state the *why*,
209: not just the *what*.
210: 
211: ## 9. Readability when talking to the user
212: 
213: **What changed.** In extended agentic conversations the model can produce text that's hard
214: to follow: arrow-chain shorthand, deep implementation detail, references to thinking the
215: user never saw.
216: 
217: **The move (drop-in):**
218: 
219: ```text
220: Terse shorthand is fine between tool calls (that's you thinking out loud). Your final
221: summary is different: it's for a reader who didn't see any of that. Open with the outcome —
222: one sentence on what happened or what you found — then the supporting detail. Drop the
223: working shorthand: complete sentences, no arrow chains or made-up labels, and give each
224: file/commit/flag its own plain-language clause. If you have to choose between short and
225: clear, choose clear.
226: ```
227: 
228: **Plugs into:** `action` — the end-of-run summary a loop emits when it stops for HITL or
229: completes.
230: 
231: ## 10. How this maps onto the 5-part loop contract
232: 
233: | Loop contract part | Long-run hygiene that lands here |
234: |---|---|
235: | **trigger** | Async/poll check-ins (1); give-the-reason framing (8) |
236: | **action** | Effort selection (2); send-to-user (7); readable summaries (9) |
237: | **proof** | Evidence-backed progress (3); periodic fresh-context verifier (6) |
238: | **memory** | Externalized state that makes budget reassurance safe (5); progress traced to tool results (3) |
239: | **stop** | Last-paragraph / autonomous-operation guard (4); budget as a harness decision, not a model-facing countdown (5) |
240: 
241: When you author a loop, walk this table: for each contract line, confirm the relevant
242: hygiene is wired. A loop that fills in the 5 parts but ignores this table will still
243: converge — it just fabricates status, stops on a promise, or quits early on a phantom
244: context worry on the way there.

=== skills/meta/model-adaptation/references/model-effort-tiering.md ===
1: # Model & effort tiering — the priced ladder and consumer wiring
2: 
3: Depth for the **Model & effort tiering** section of the model-adaptation
4: SKILL.md. Everything in this file is model- and pricing-specific and therefore
5: **ages** — like the SKILL.md *Current landscape* table, update it when a new
6: model ships or pricing moves. Facts verified against the `claude-api` reference
7: on 2026-07-03.
8: 
9: ## Why tiering works — the output-token asymmetry
10: 
11: Across the Anthropic family, **output tokens cost 5× input**. That makes two
12: levers dominate:
13: 
14: 1. **Move bulk work down a tier** — a 2×–10× per-output-token cut with no
15:    quality loss on grunt tasks.
16: 2. **Trim output** (the `caveman` skill) — a flat cut on the dominant cost
17:    component, on top of whichever tier is chosen.
18: 
19: The scarce resource depends on how the project bills, but the doctrine is
20: identical for all three:
21: 
22: | Billing surface | What tiering saves |
23: |---|---|
24: | Metered API | Literal dollars (the price table below) |
25: | Subscription (Claude Code Max) | Usage-allowance burn — you stretch the allotment |
26: | FreeLLMAPI / free tiers | Rate-limit / quota headroom — you stay under provider caps |
27: 
28: ## The Anthropic default ladder
29: 
30: Per-1M-token pricing and effort support. Note **Haiku 4.5 does not accept the
31: `effort` parameter** (it returns a 400) — the effort dial applies to
32: Sonnet-and-up.
33: 
34: | Tier | Model ID | $ in / out (1M) | Output vs Fable | Effort | Use for |
35: |---|---|---|---|---|---|
36: | **Cheapest** | `claude-haiku-4-5` | $1 / $5 | 0.1× | none (400s) | High-volume mechanical passes, classification, simple extraction, cheap fan-out where reasoning is minimal |
37: | **Mid** | `claude-sonnet-5` | $3 / $15 ($2/$10 intro through 2026-08-31) | 0.3× | low–max (+xhigh) | Standard implementation, test writing, most research crawl, routine role-agent build work |
38: | **High** | `claude-opus-4-8` | $5 / $25 | 0.5× | low–max (+xhigh) | Hard implementation, tricky debugging, the reasoning-heavy role work |
39: | **Top** | `claude-fable-5` | $10 / $50 | 1.0× | low–max (+xhigh) | Long-horizon autonomous builds, architecture/contract design, the adversarial verify gate, final synthesis |
40: 
41: Effort defaults: the API default is `high`; Claude Code defaults to `xhigh`. On
42: the Claude 5 family, `low` effort often matches or beats the `xhigh`/`max`
43: output of prior-generation models — so `low`/`medium` is the correct setting
44: for routine work, not a compromise. Reserve `max` for correctness-critical,
45: latency-insensitive steps.
46: 
47: ## The optimizer/target split (role-based tiering)
48: 
49: The ladder above tiers by *task difficulty*. There is a second, role-based rule
50: for any setup where one model **authors or optimizes** an artifact (a skill, a
51: prompt, a config) that another model then **executes under**: put the strong
52: tier on the author/optimizer side and the cheap tier on the execution side.
53: 
54: The evidence is microsoft/SkillOpt's cross-model study: the same optimized
55: skill produced roughly **2× the score gain** on a weaker execution model than
56: on a strong one — the weaker model has more headroom, so the optimization
57: dollars land where they buy the most. The economical pattern is therefore
58: "cheap deployed target + one strong optimizer," not "strong everywhere":
59: 
60: - **Author / optimize / review** a skill or prompt on the top tier (it runs
61:   once per revision).
62: - **Execute and validate under** it on the cheap tier (it runs on every task,
63:   and benefits most from the optimization).
64: 
65: This composes with, and does not replace, the task-difficulty ladder — the
66: fresh-context evaluator in a loop is still top-tier (it is a reasoning gate,
67: not an execution pass).
68: 
69: ## Provider-relative instantiation
70: 
71: A project's provider should be discoverable, not guessed:
72: 
73: - Read the declared provider from `.claude/profile.yaml` (the
74:   `project-profiler` / `setup-project-skills` convention). Absent that,
75:   **default to Anthropic**.
76: - **Anthropic** → the ladder above.
77: - **DeepSeek** (a project that declares it — e.g. a Freebuff session) → the
78:   two-model family *is* the ladder: `deepseek-v4-flash` (thinking off = grunt,
79:   on = mid) → `deepseek-v4-pro` (thinking high/max = the reasoning gate). The
80:   capability dial is thinking mode + effort (`none/low/high/max`; `xhigh` and
81:   `medium` both map to `high`). Full facts: `deepseek-adaptation.md`.
82: - **FreeLLMAPI** (the project uses `use-freellmapi`) → the aggregated free
83:   tiers are the ladder; optimize for rate/quota and capability, not dollars;
84:   multi-provider spanning is expected — and this is the *only* setup where it
85:   is.
86: - **Any other single provider** → that provider's own tier ladder; instantiate
87:   the same shape (cheap-for-grunt, top-for-reasoning) and stay inside it. Never
88:   substitute a different vendor's model to save tokens.
89: 
90: ## Wiring into the consumers
91: 
92: The knob already exists everywhere; this policy sets its defaults. Consumers
93: point here — they don't duplicate the tables.
94: 
95: - **Orchestrator role dispatch** — assign `model` + `effort` per role from the
96:   SKILL.md task→tier map (a QE mechanical sweep is not the architecture pass).
97:   See the *"Model and effort are per-role dials"* paragraph in the
98:   orchestrator's Runtime Detection section.
99: - **Workflow-mode fan-out** — finder/mapper/transform stages take the
100:   mechanical tier (cheap model, low effort); verify/judge/synthesis stages take
101:   the top tier at high effort. Set `opts.model` **and** `opts.effort` per
102:   stage. This deliberately overrides the Workflow tool's generic "omit
103:   `opts.model` by default" guidance: an omitted model resolves to the
104:   session-start model, which goes stale after `/model`. See the orchestrator's
105:   `references/workflow-orchestration.md`.
106: - **loop-controller (Step 6)** — workers and routine iteration passes tier
107:   down; the fresh-context evaluator stays on the top tier — it is the
108:   reasoning gate.
109: - **use-freellmapi** — the carve-out described above. Within any build that is
110:   not a FreeLLMAPI project, never mix vendors to save tokens.
111: - **use-pxpipe** — checks the image-proxy allowlist below before enabling the
112:   proxy; it wires the proxy, the SKILL.md's *Image-proxy model allowlist*
113:   section owns the policy.
114: 
115: ## Image-proxy allowlist — current state
116: 
117: The aging half of the SKILL.md's *Image-proxy model allowlist* section: which
118: models may sit behind a pxpipe-style image proxy today. Numbers are the
119: 2026-07-21 pxpipe deep dive's measurements (its ~20-call glyph sweep over dense
120: rendered text — `teamchong/pxpipe`, v0.8.0). Re-run the sweep and update this
121: table on every model release, the same cadence as the ladder above.
122: 
123: | Model | Behind the image proxy? | Measured (pxpipe dive, 2026-07-21) |
124: |---|---|---|
125: | **DeepSeek V4 (flash/pro)** | **Not allowed** (unlisted default) | Not yet glyph-swept — earns a slot only by passing the sweep (see `deepseek-adaptation.md`) |
126: | **Fable 5** | Allowed | 13–15/15 on the dense-hex glyph sweep |
127: | **Mythos 5** | Allowed | Same family and read behavior as Fable 5 (dive groups them in the 13–15/15 band) |
128: | **Opus 4.8** | **Not allowed** | 6/15 on dense hex — misreads surface as confident wrong answers, not errors |
129: | **Any unlisted / new model** | **Not allowed** (default) | Earns a slot only by passing the glyph sweep |
130: 
131: One interaction to keep in view: a refused Claude 5 request falls back to
132: Opus 4.8 (`references/refusal-and-fallback.md`) — so a session on an allowed
133: model can land on a disallowed one mid-run. The `use-pxpipe` wiring is where
134: that wrinkle gets handled; this table only records who passes.

=== README.md ===
1: <div align="center">
2: 
3: # 🧰 Skill Madness
4: 
5: ### *All the skills, all the agents, all the chaos — coordinated.*
6: 
7: **Most AI coding setups give you one agent, one context window, one shot. Skill Madness gives you a coordinated fleet — plus the autonomous loops to keep it working until the job is provably done.**
8: 
9: A multi-agent orchestration toolkit for Claude Code: **76 skills, seven categories** — turn a one-line ask into a contract-first parallel build, run **autonomous loops** until your tests are actually green, and author everything once in a portable `SKILL.md` format whose converters feed **eleven AI coding tools** (Claude Code runs the full library; the ten other hosts get its portable subset).
10: 
11: <p align="center">
12:   <a href="https://github.com/ivy00johns/Skill-Madness/actions/workflows/lint-skills.yml"><img src="https://github.com/ivy00johns/Skill-Madness/actions/workflows/lint-skills.yml/badge.svg" alt="Skill Lint" /></a>

41: **🪄 Orchestrate**
42: 
43: Turn a one-line ask into a coordinated multi-agent build. The `orchestrator` decomposes the work into a 14-phase plan, authors machine-readable contracts **before** any code, dispatches role agents in parallel with exclusive file ownership, and blocks the merge on a structured QA report.

49:

62: 
63: > 🚪 **New here, or not sure which skill to reach for?** Type **`/madness`** in Claude Code and just say what you want. It reads your intent, picks the right starting skill out of all 76, confirms before anything expensive, and launches it — so you never have to memorize the catalog. One front door for the whole toolkit.

65: <sub><b>Maintainers / agents:</b> see <a href="START-HERE.md"><code>START-HERE.md</code></a> for current status and which docs are canonical.</sub>

67: ---
68: 
69: ## ✨ Why this exists
70: 
71: Every AI coding tool ships the same traps. **One agent, one context window, one set of files** — fine for a small project, brittle for anything bigger than a single context can hold. They **stop at the first draft** — when the tests are still red, *you're* the one re-prompting "still failing, try again" until it finally goes green. And **every tool reinvents the same wheel** — Cursor wants `.mdc`, Aider wants `CONVENTIONS.md`, Windsurf wants `.windsurfrules`, Claude Code wants `SKILL.md` — so any prompt library you build gets stranded on whichever host you wrote it for.
72: 
73: **Skill Madness attacks all three.** The `orchestrator` decomposes a complex build into a 14-phase plan, makes integration surfaces machine-readable *before* anyone writes code, dispatches role agents in parallel with strict file ownership, and refuses to ship until a separate QE agent signs off via a structured report. **Autonomous loops** then keep the work going after that first pass — driving tests to green, coverage to a target, or a contract to conformance, with guardrails that make them converge instead of thrash. And the skill library underneath authors once in the canonical `SKILL.md` format — the same skills install into eleven different AI coding tools without copy-paste drift.
74: 
75: - 👑 **The orchestrator is the entry point** — a single 14-phase playbook covering team sizing, runtime detection, contract authoring, parallel dispatch, integration validation, QA gate, and handoff. It's the skill that turns a one-line ask into a coordinated multi-agent build.
76: - 📜 **Contract-first** — `contract-author` writes OpenAPI / AsyncAPI / Pydantic / TypeScript / JSON Schema *before* a line of implementation. `contract-auditor` verifies every shipped module against the spec. Agents can't drift; the contract is the truth.
77: - 🤖 **Ten role agents, exclusive ownership** — backend, frontend, infrastructure, QE, security, docs, observability, db-migration, performance, code-review. Each declares `owns.directories` / `owns.files` in its frontmatter. No two agents touch the same path. Conflicts get resolved before spawn, not after.
78: - 🛡️ **QA gate that blocks** — `qe-agent` emits a `qa-report.json` with critical / high / medium / low findings plus contract-conformance and security scores. The orchestrator gates the merge on the report. Agents can't self-declare "done."
79: - 🔁 **Autonomous loops that converge** — 13 loop skills keep Claude working until something is *provably* true: `fix-until-green` won't stop until tests + lint + typecheck pass (and can't cheat the gate), `coverage-loop` grows the suite to a target without gaming it, `contract-conformance-loop` builds until a fresh-context evaluator agrees the spec is met, and `babysit` / `self-healing-loop` / `nightly-docs-and-changelog` run on a schedule. Every one is a configuration of `loop-controller`'s guardrail stack — iteration cap, token budget, no-progress breaker, stop condition — so they finish instead of thrashing. [See the loops →](#-autonomous-loops)
80: - 🪄 **One front door** — `madness` is the router: type `/madness`, describe the task, and it picks the right starting skill out of all 76 and launches it. The cure for "which skill was that again?" across a 76-skill library.
81: - 🪜 **Progressive disclosure** — frontmatter (~100 tokens) always loaded, body loaded on trigger, references loaded on demand. A 76-skill library stays cheap to host.
82: - 🔁 **Two-runtime degradation** — Agent Teams (parallel tmux) → subagents (Task tool) → sequential. The orchestrator picks the highest mode the host supports; role skills work standalone in any of them.
83: - 🧰 **76 skills, seven categories, all CI-linted** — the `orchestrator`, 10 role agents, 2 contract skills, 7 meta-skills (including `madness`, the front-door router, and `model-adaptation`), 4 git-workflow skills, 39 cross-cutting workflow skills (plan-builder, repo-deep-dive, ui-brief, mermaid-charts, …), and 13 autonomous-loop skills. Frontmatter, body length, and cross-skill ownership are all gated on every push.
84: - 🌐 **Portable format, honest subset** — `SKILL.md` is the canonical source; converters emit Claude Code, Copilot, Cursor, Aider, Windsurf, OpenCode, Qwen, OpenClaw, Gemini CLI, Antigravity, and Kimi formats. The multi-agent core — the orchestrator, the role agents, and the autonomous loops, whose contracts *are* Claude Code's runtime primitives — stays Claude-Code-only by design; the standalone conventions and workflows (git, planning, docs, review, debugging, contract authoring, and more) convert to all ten other hosts. See [Also works on ten other hosts](#-also-works-on-ten-other-hosts).
85: 
86: > **Status — read before you pitch this to anyone:**
87: > - **The orchestrator + 76-skill library is the mature part.** All bodies under 500 lines, zero ownership conflicts, zero broken cross-references, an Ubuntu lint gate plus a non-blocking macOS smoke on every push.
88: > - **The 13 autonomous loops are the newest layer.** All built on one `loop-controller` guardrail harness and CI-linted. The build/verify loops (`fix-until-green`, `coverage-loop`, `contract-conformance-loop`) are the most exercised; the scheduled ones (`self-healing-loop`, `dependency-health-loop`) are powerful but younger — keep a human in the loop on anything irreversible.
89: > - **Claude Code is the end-to-end-verified host.** Multi-agent dispatch with file-ownership exclusivity and the `qa-report.json` gate runs live on Claude Code today. The other ten hosts receive the library's portable subset and don't run the orchestrator's parallel dispatch.
90: > - **Lossy conversion is announced.** When a skill is converted to a non-Claude-Code host, orchestration-only fields (`allowed_tools`, `owns`, `composes_with`, `spawned_by`, `requires_agent_teams`) are stripped with a stderr line per skill. Skills marked `requires_claude_code: true` are skipped entirely for those targets. See `contracts/installer/per-tool-output-spec.md`.
91: 
92: ---
93: 
94: ## 🚀 Quick Start
95: 
96: ### Prerequisites
97: 
98: | You need | Why |
99: |----------|-----|
100: | **bash 3.2+** + standard POSIX tools | The installer is pure shell, written bash-3.2-safe — stock macOS bash works, no upgrade needed (CI runs a macOS job to keep it that way) |
101: | **python3** | Frontmatter parsing in `lint-skills.sh` |
102: | **git** | Cloning the repo and (optionally) symlinking into your global skills dir |
103: | **Claude Code** (recommended) | Where the orchestrator + multi-agent QA gate actually run end-to-end |
104: | **(optional) any of ten other hosts** | Copilot, Cursor, Aider, Windsurf, OpenCode, Qwen, OpenClaw, Gemini CLI, Antigravity, Kimi — see [Also works on ten other hosts](#-also-works-on-ten-other-hosts) |
105: 
106: ### Install for Claude Code
107: 
108: Two install paths. Pick one.
109: 
110: #### Option A — Claude Code plugin manager (recommended for users)
111: 
112: From inside Claude Code:
113: 
114: ```text
115: /plugin marketplace add ivy00johns/Skill-Madness
116: /plugin install skill-madness@skill-madness
117: ```
118: 
119: That installs all 76 skills into Claude Code's plugin storage. No clone, no symlink, no edits-to-the-repo workflow. Use this if you just want the skills.
120: 
121: To update later: `/plugin update skill-madness`.
122: 
123: #### Option B — Clone + `/sync-skills` (recommended for contributors)
124: 
125: Clone, then run `/sync-skills` from inside Claude Code. It creates flattened symlinks at `~/.claude/skills/<skill-name>` so edits in the repo are live in every session — no rebuild step.
126: 
127: ```bash
128: git clone https://github.com/ivy00johns/Skill-Madness.git
129: cd Skill-Madness
130: 
131: # Inside Claude Code:
132: /sync-skills
133: ```
134: 
135: If you'd rather copy than symlink, the underlying script accepts `--copy` instead of `--link`. See `skills/workflows/sync-skills/SKILL.md`.
136: 
137: Use this if you want to author skills, iterate on them, or contribute upstream.
138: 
139: ### Install for any of the other ten hosts
140: 
141: Two scripts. The first translates the canonical `SKILL.md` files into eleven host-native shapes; the second installs the converted artifacts into whichever hosts it detects on your machine.
142: 
143: ```bash
144: ./scripts/convert.sh   # skills/**/SKILL.md  →  integrations/<host>/...
145: ./scripts/install.sh   # integrations/<host> →  ~/.<host>/, .cursor/rules/, etc.
146: ```
147: 
148: `install.sh` is interactive when run in a TTY and auto-detects from environment variables in CI. See [Also works on ten other hosts](#-also-works-on-ten-other-hosts) for the per-host format matrix and what gets stripped on conversion, and `scripts/README.md` for flag-level docs.
149: 
150: ### Use it
151: 
152: Tell Claude Code to build something with multiple agents:
153: 
154: ```text
155: "Build a chat app with React frontend and FastAPI backend — use an agent team."
156: ```
157: 
158: The `orchestrator` skill triggers automatically: it sizes the team, generates contracts, spawns parallel agents in isolated worktrees, gates the build on `qa-report.json`, and returns when QE signs off.
159: 
160: Or set it loose on a goal and let a loop converge on its own:
161: 
162: ```text
163: "Fix the failing tests and don't stop until everything's green."  → fix-until-green
164: "Get test coverage up to 85% without gaming the metric."          → coverage-loop
165: "Keep this PR rebased and green while review comes in."           → /loop 5m /babysit
166: "Map this whole codebase and write me the architecture."         → codebase-exploration-loop
167: ```
168: 
169: Or invoke any skill standalone:
170: 
171: ```text
172: "Review this code for security vulnerabilities."   → security-agent
173: "Set up Docker and CI/CD for this project."        → infrastructure-agent
174: "Write k6 load tests for the /search endpoint."    → performance-agent
175: "Profile this codebase and write me a CLAUDE.md."  → project-profiler
176: "Generate a UI brief for a refresh of /settings."  → ui-brief
177: ```
178: 
179: Not sure which skill fits? Don't guess — let the front door route you:
180: 
181: ```text
182: /madness build a CLI that syncs two folders, with tests
183: ```
184: 
185: `madness` reads the intent, picks the right starting skill, confirms before anything expensive (a swarm, an overnight loop, a full build), and hands off.
186: 
187: ---
188: 
189: ## 🧬 Architecture
190: 
191: The orchestrator sits above seven skill categories — every build pulls from this library, every category lints clean, and the library's portable subset converts to the ten non-Claude-Code hosts.
192: 
193: ```mermaid
194: flowchart TB
195:     orch["👑 <b>orchestrator</b><br/><sub>14-phase build playbook · runtime detection<br/>team sizing · circuit breaker · handoff protocol</sub>"]:::entry
196: 
197:     subgraph contracts["📜 contracts/ — 2 skills"]
198:         direction TB
199:         ca[contract-author]
200:         cau[contract-auditor]
201:     end
202: 
203:     subgraph roles["🤖 roles/ — 10 agents · exclusive file ownership"]
204:         direction TB
205:         be[backend]
206:         fe[frontend]
207:         infra[infrastructure]
208:         qe["qe<br/><sub>(QA gate)</sub>"]:::gate
209:         sec[security]
210:         rdocs[docs]
211:         obs[observability]
212:         dbm[db-migration]
213:         perf[performance]
214:         cra[code-review]
215:     end
216: 
217:     subgraph meta["🧠 meta/ — 7 skills"]
218:         direction TB
219:         md["madness<br/><sub>(front door)</sub>"]:::entry
220:         sx[skill-explorer]
221:         sw[skill-writer]
222:         sr[skill-review]
223:         su[skill-update]
224:         mda[model-adaptation]
225:         sc[skill-catalog]
226:     end
227: 
228:     subgraph gitcat["🔁 git/ — 4 skills"]
229:         direction TB
230:         gcm[git-commit]
231:         gpr[git-pr]
232:         gpf[git-pr-feedback]
233:         gpmc[git-post-merge-cleanup]
234:     end
235: 
236:     subgraph workflows["⚙️ workflows/ — 39 skills"]
237:         direction TB
238:         pb[plan-builder]
239:         cm[context-manager]
240:         dc[dependency-coordinator]
241:         dch[deployment-checklist]
242:         pp[project-profiler]
243:         wr[wiki-research]
244:         rdd[repo-deep-dive]
245:         lw[llm-wiki]
246:         ub[ui-brief]
247:         cdb[claude-design-brief]
248:         mc[mermaid-charts]
249:         rs[render-sanity]
250:         dl[diagnose-loop]
251:         more["+ 26 more"]
252:     end
253: 
254:     subgraph loops["🔁 loops/ — 13 skills"]
255:         direction TB
256:         lc[loop-controller]
257:         fug[fix-until-green]
258:         otl[orchestrator-task-loop]
259:         ccl[contract-conformance-loop]
260:         bs[babysit]
261:         cov[coverage-loop]
262:         pfl[perf-loop]
263:         shl[self-healing-loop]
264:         mig[migration-loop]
265:         ndc[nightly-docs-and-changelog]
266:         dhl[dependency-health-loop]
267:         cel[codebase-exploration-loop]
268:         rcl[repo-cleanup-loop]
269:     end
270: 
271:     orch --> contracts
272:     orch --> roles
273:     orch --> meta
274:     orch --> gitcat
275:     orch --> workflows
276:     orch --> loops
277: 
278:     classDef entry fill:#7c3aed,stroke:#4c1d95,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px
279:     classDef gate fill:#dc2626,stroke:#7f1d1d,color:#fff,stroke-width:1.5px
280: ```
281: 
282: ### How a build flows
283: 
284: A one-line build request enters the orchestrator and exits as a shipped artifact only after the `qe-agent` writes a passing `qa-report.json`. Every other path blocks the merge.
285: 
286: ```mermaid
287: flowchart TB
288:     user(["🧑 You"]) -->|"build X — use an agent team"| orch[👑 orchestrator]
289:     orch --> detect[/"detect runtime<br/><sub>Agent Teams · subagents · sequential</sub>"/]
290:     detect --> size[/"size team"/]
291:     size --> contracts["📜 <b>contract-author</b><br/><sub>OpenAPI · Pydantic · TypeScript<br/>written BEFORE any implementation</sub>"]:::contract
292: 
293:     contracts --> dispatch{{"⚡ parallel dispatch<br/><sub>exclusive file ownership · no overlapping writes</sub>"}}
294: 
295:     subgraph parallel["isolated worktrees"]
296:         direction LR
297:         be["🛠 backend<br/><sub>owns api/</sub>"]
298:         fe["🎨 frontend<br/><sub>owns web/</sub>"]
299:         infra["📦 infrastructure<br/><sub>owns infra/</sub>"]
300:         docs["📚 docs<br/><sub>owns docs/</sub>"]
301:     end
302: 
303:     dispatch --> be
304:     dispatch --> fe
305:     dispatch --> infra
306:     dispatch --> docs
307: 
308:     be --> validate
309:     fe --> validate
310:     infra --> validate
311:     docs --> validate
312: 
313:     validate["🔬 contract-auditor<br/>+ qe-agent + security-agent"]:::validate
314:     validate --> gate{"🚦 qa-report.json<br/>gate"}:::gate
315:     gate -- "CRITICAL findings" --> block[/"⛔ block merge"/]:::fail
316:     gate -- "all checks pass" --> ship[/"🚀 ship"/]:::pass
317: 
318:     classDef contract fill:#1e40af,stroke:#1e3a8a,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px
319:     classDef validate fill:#7c3aed,stroke:#5b21b6,color:#fff
320:     classDef gate fill:#f59e0b,stroke:#b45309,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px
321:     classDef fail fill:#dc2626,stroke:#7f1d1d,color:#fff
322:     classDef pass fill:#16a34a,stroke:#14532d,color:#fff
323: ```
324: 
325: ---
326: 
327: ## 🔁 Autonomous loops
328: 
329: The newest part of Skill Madness — and the fastest way to feel why it exists. A loop points Claude at a goal with a **verifiable stop condition** and a **mandatory guardrail stack**, then lets it run until the goal is provably met. No more babysitting "still failing, try again."
330: 
331: Every loop is a configuration of **`loop-controller`**, the foundation harness that picks the right primitive for the job — `/goal`, `/loop`, a Stop-hook, a bash "Ralph" loop, or a dynamic workflow — and wires in the guardrails so the loop *converges* instead of thrashing or running up the bill.
332: 
333: **🟢 Build & verify — run until it's green:**
334: 
335: | Loop | What it drives to "done" | Kick it off with |
336: |------|--------------------------|------------------|
337: | `fix-until-green` | tests + lint + typecheck all passing — and it can't cheat the gate | *"fix until the tests are green"* |
338: | `contract-conformance-loop` | every authored-contract criterion holds, graded by a fresh-context evaluator | *"build until the contract is met"* |
339: | `coverage-loop` | a coverage target, without gaming the metric | *"get coverage to 85%"* |
340: | `perf-loop` | a metric under its budget, with no functional regression | *"optimize until p95 < 200ms"* |
341: | `migration-loop` | an enumerated target set migrated, suite green, no legacy pattern left | *"migrate every file off the old API"* |
342: 
343: **🗓️ Operate & maintain — run on a schedule:**
344: 
345: | Loop | What it keeps healthy | Kick it off with |
346: |------|-----------------------|------------------|
347: | `babysit` | a PR rebased + green, auto-addressing review comments (HITL on anything irreversible) | `/loop 5m /babysit` |
348: | `self-healing-loop` | logs/CI watched → root-caused → fixed → verified → PR (HITL before prod) | *"watch CI and self-heal"* |
349: | `nightly-docs-and-changelog` | docs + changelog that don't rot after a release | `/schedule` nightly |
350: | `dependency-health-loop` | dependencies audited + one gated bump per pass (HITL on majors) | `/schedule` weekly |
351: | `repo-cleanup-loop` | branches / PRs / worktrees tidy — recover valuable work *before* deleting | `/schedule` weekly |
352: 
353: **🔎 Understand & coordinate:**
354: 
355: | Loop | What it produces | Kick it off with |
356: |------|------------------|------------------|
357: | `codebase-exploration-loop` | a written architecture summary that answers every seed question | *"map this codebase for me"* |
358: | `orchestrator-task-loop` | the Agent Teams shared task board drained until every task passes its gate *(experimental)* | *"drain the task board"* |
359: 
360: > 🛡️ **Loops are gated by design.** Every loop inherits `loop-controller`'s guardrails: a hard iteration cap, a token budget, a no-progress circuit breaker, and a stop condition checked by a *separate* fresh-context evaluator so the worker can't grade its own homework. Anything irreversible — pushing to prod, force-deleting work — pauses for a human. Read the harness: [`skills/loops/loop-controller/SKILL.md`](skills/loops/loop-controller/SKILL.md).
361: 
362: ---

454: ## 📂 Project structure
455: 
456: ```
457: .
458: ├── README.md                         # this file
459: ├── CLAUDE.md                         # project guidance for Claude Code
460: ├── AGENTS.md                         # shared instructions for AI agents
461: │
462: ├── skills/                           # the canonical skill library (76)
463: │   ├── orchestrator/                 # 1 — entry point
464: │   ├── roles/                        # 10 — implementation agents
465: │   ├── contracts/                    # 2 — contract-author / contract-auditor
466: │   ├── meta/                         # 7 — skills that manage skills (incl. madness, model-adaptation)
467: │   ├── git/                          # 4 — git workflow conventions
468: │   ├── workflows/                    # 34 — cross-cutting process skills
469: │   └── loops/                        # 13 — autonomous-loop skills
470: │
471: ├── scripts/                          # multi-tool installer
472: │   ├── convert.sh                    # SKILL.md → 11 host-native formats
473: │   ├── install.sh                    # integrations/ → host install dirs
474: │   ├── lint-skills.sh                # frontmatter + cross-skill validation
475: │   ├── lib/                          # frontmatter / platform / slug / term helpers
476: │   └── README.md                     # per-host destinations and flags
477: │
478: ├── hooks/                            # lifecycle-hook layer (Claude Code only, 6 hooks)
479: │   ├── hooks.manifest.json           # registry: hook id, event, profiles, blocking
480: │   ├── run-with-flags.sh             # single entrypoint; dispatches scripts/<hook-id>.sh
481: │   ├── scripts/                      # per-hook implementations (qa-gate, catalog-sync, …)
482: │   └── lib/                          # shared hook helpers
483: │
484: ├── integrations/                     # generated outputs (one dir per host)
485: │   ├── claude-code/  copilot/  cursor/  aider/  windsurf/
486: │   ├── opencode/  qwen/  openclaw/  gemini-cli/  antigravity/  kimi/
487: │
488: ├── contracts/installer/              # installer specs
489: │   ├── skill-source-format.md        # canonical SKILL.md schema
490: │   ├── per-tool-output-spec.md       # per-host fidelity matrix
491: │   ├── install-locations.md          # where each host expects skills
492: │   └── lint-rules.md                 # what the linter enforces
493: │
494: ├── spec/                             # the portable frontmatter standard
495: │   ├── PSFS.md                       # Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec v1.1.0
496: │   └── frontmatter.schema.json       # JSON Schema 2020-12 reference validator
497: │
498: ├── tests/installer/                  # bats-core tests for convert/install/lint
499: └── .github/workflows/lint-skills.yml # Ubuntu gate + macOS smoke CI
500: ```
501: 
502: ---
503: 
504: ## 🎁 Also works on ten other hosts
505: 
506: The orchestrator and the multi-agent QA gate are Claude-Code-native — that's the headline feature, and it stays home: skills whose contract *is* Claude Code's runtime (the orchestrator, the 10 role agents, all 13 loops, and the workflows bound to the Artifact tool, subagent dispatch, or `~/.claude` config) are marked `requires_claude_code: true` and are never converted. The canonical `SKILL.md` *format* is platform-agnostic, though, so the rest of the library — **39 of the 76 skills** today: the git conventions and the planning, docs, review, debugging, and contract-authoring workflows — converts to ten other AI coding tools. Broadening that subset is tracked as F1 in [`docs/FUTURE.md`](docs/FUTURE.md).
507: 
508: The single-source model is deliberate, and there's a concrete counter-example for why: microsoft/SkillOpt shipped the opposite design — five bespoke per-host integrations (claude-code, codex, copilot, devin, openclaw) — and was already drifting within months of release: backend-enum mismatches between plugins, and an openclaw adapter broken-by-design against its own engine. One canonical `SKILL.md` plus converters means a fix lands once instead of five times.
509: 
510: Two scripts handle it:
511: 
512: ```bash
513: ./scripts/convert.sh   # skills/**/SKILL.md  →  integrations/<host>/...
514: ./scripts/install.sh   # integrations/<host> →  ~/.<host>/, .cursor/rules/, etc.
515: ```
516: 
517: `install.sh` is interactive when run in a TTY and auto-detects from environment variables in CI. See `scripts/README.md` for per-host destinations, scopes, and flags.
518: 
519: | Host | Scope | Output format | Source strategy |
520: |------|-------|---------------|-----------------|
521: | 🟣 **Claude Code** | user | `SKILL.md` (passthrough) | direct symlink |
522: | 🐙 **GitHub Copilot** | user | `.md` (passthrough) | direct copy |
523: | 🌀 **Cursor** | project | `.mdc` with metadata | generated |
524: | 🤝 **Aider** | project | single `CONVENTIONS.md` | accumulated |
525: | 🪁 **Windsurf** | project | single `.windsurfrules` | accumulated |
526: | 🧱 **OpenCode** | project | `.md` with `mode` field | generated |
527: | 🧮 **Qwen Code** | project | `.md` with optional `tools` | generated |
528: | 🦾 **OpenClaw** | user | 3-file split (SOUL / AGENTS / IDENTITY) | generated |
529: | 💎 **Gemini CLI** | user | extension manifest + `SKILL.md` | generated |
530: | 🛰️  **Antigravity** | user | community-skill `SKILL.md` | generated |
531: | 🌙 **Kimi Code** | user | YAML config + `system.md` | generated |
532: 
533: **Lossy by design.** Claude-Code-specific orchestration fields (`allowed-tools`, `owns`, `composes_with`, `spawned_by`, `requires_agent_teams`) are stripped on conversion to the other ten hosts — those hosts don't run multi-agent dispatch with file-ownership exclusivity, so the metadata would be noise. You'll see one `[host] stripped allowed-tools/owns from <slug>` line per affected skill on stderr. Skills marked `requires_claude_code: true` are skipped entirely for non-Claude-Code targets.
534: 
535: > **Credit where it's due.** The eleven-host installer pattern, the `detect_<tool>()` probes, the interactive selection UI, the slug pipeline, and the OpenClaw soul/agents split are all adapted from [`msitarzewski/agency-agents`](https://github.com/msitarzewski/agency-agents) (MIT). Full attribution and the list of pieces that came across vs. were rewritten lives in [`ACKNOWLEDGMENTS.md`](ACKNOWLEDGMENTS.md). The orchestrator, role agents, contracts, QA gate, and the rest of the skill library are independent.
536: 
537: ---
538: 
539: ## 💻 CLI / scripts reference
540: 
541: <details>
542: <summary><b>📖 Full script reference</b> (click to expand)</summary>
543: 
544: ### `scripts/convert.sh`
545: 
546: Reads `skills/**/SKILL.md` and writes host-specific artifacts to `integrations/<host>/`. Idempotent. Lossy-by-design — orchestration metadata is stripped per the per-tool spec, with stderr warnings on every strip.
547: 
548: ```bash
549: ./scripts/convert.sh                     # all hosts
550: ./scripts/convert.sh --tool cursor       # one host
551: ./scripts/convert.sh --parallel --jobs 8 # all hosts, concurrently
552: ```
553: 
554: ### `scripts/install.sh`
555: 
556: Reads `integrations/<host>/` and copies into the host's expected install location. Detects which hosts are present on the machine (presence of `~/.cursor`, `~/.config/aider`, etc.). Interactive when run in a TTY; auto-confirms in CI.
557: 
558: ```bash
559: ./scripts/install.sh                     # interactive TUI (TTY) or detected hosts (non-TTY)
560: ./scripts/install.sh --tool claude-code  # one host
561: ./scripts/install.sh --dry-run           # show what would happen
562: ```
563: 
564: ### `scripts/lint-skills.sh`
565: 
566: Runs on every push to every branch (Ubuntu gating; macOS as a non-blocking smoke) via `.github/workflows/lint-skills.yml`. Validates:
567: 
568: - Frontmatter schema (required fields, valid `version` semver, kebab-case `name`)
569: - Body length under 500 lines
570: - Cross-skill invariants (no ownership conflicts in `owns.directories` / `owns.files`)
571: - Reference link integrity within each skill's `references/`
572: 
573: Outputs JUnit XML for GitHub Actions test results.
574: 
575: ```bash
576: ./scripts/lint-skills.sh                          # full lint
577: ./scripts/lint-skills.sh skills/orchestrator/     # one skill (positional path, not a flag)
578: ./scripts/lint-skills.sh --standard               # report the frontmatter standard it validates
579: ```
580: 
581: ### Frontmatter standard — PSFS v1.1.0
582: 
583: The frontmatter convention is published as a named, versioned standard: the **Portable Skill Frontmatter Spec (PSFS) v1.1.0** at [`spec/PSFS.md`](spec/PSFS.md). It defines two conformance tiers — **Core** (Anthropic-aligned, vendor-neutral; the tier any collection can adopt) and **Extended** (Core plus the multi-agent fields `owns` / `composes_with` / `spawned_by` / `requires_*`). The portable, tool-agnostic reference validator is [`spec/frontmatter.schema.json`](spec/frontmatter.schema.json) (JSON Schema 2020-12 — runnable in any language); `scripts/lint-skills.sh` is the bash reference implementation and adds the cross-file checks (name uniqueness, name-matches-directory, `owns` non-overlap) that a per-file schema can't express.
584: 
585: ### `/sync-skills` (Claude Code slash command)
586: 
587: The recommended path on Claude Code. Symlinks `skills/<category>/<skill>/` to `~/.claude/skills/<skill>/` so Claude Code sees them globally and edits stay live.
588: 
589: </details>
590: 
591: ---
592: 
593: ## 🪝 Hooks
594: 
595: Skill Madness ships a small **hooks layer** under `hooks/` that turns the orchestrator's doctrine — the QA gate, formatting, profile injection — into enforcement. Hooks are modeled on the gated-hook design but built in the repo's own bash + `python3` (stdlib-only) idiom. Today only **Claude Code** has a native lifecycle-hook runtime, so hooks are emitted for Claude Code and skipped (logged `unsupported`) for the other ten hosts.
596: 
597: ### The six hooks
598: 
599: | Hook | Event | Profiles | Blocks? | What it does |
600: |------|-------|----------|---------|--------------|
601: | `qa-gate` | `Stop` | all | **yes** | Validates `qa-report.json` against the QE schema and applies the orchestrator gate rules; blocks the Stop on a gate failure. **(marquee)** |
602: | `post-edit-format` | `PostToolUse(Edit\|Write)` | standard, strict | no | Formats the just-edited file if a matching formatter (prettier/black/shfmt/gofmt/rustfmt) is on `PATH`. |
603: | `session-start-profile` | `SessionStart` | standard, strict | no | Injects a ≤8 KB summary of `CLAUDE.md` / `.claude/profile.yaml` into the session. |
604: | `catalog-sync` | `PreToolUse(Bash git commit)` | standard, strict | no | If the skill catalog (README/CLAUDE/PLAN/START-HERE/plugin.json/marketplace.json counts) has drifted from disk, runs `catalog.sh --sync` and re-stages the corrected files; acts only on real drift, never blocks. |
605: | `skill-usage` | `PostToolUse(Skill)` | standard, strict | no | Appends a coarse, best-effort usage-telemetry event via `skill-health.sh record`; degrades silently, never blocks. |
606: | `pre-commit-lint` | `PreToolUse(Bash git commit)` | strict | no (warn) | Runs `lint-skills.sh` on staged skills and warns; never blocks the commit. |
607: 
608: Everything routes through one entrypoint — `hooks/run-with-flags.sh <hook-id>` — which reads the gating env vars and `hooks.manifest.json`, then dispatches `hooks/scripts/<hook-id>.sh` (or no-ops `exit 0` if disabled).
609: 
610: ### Profiles & disabling
611: 
612: Two env vars tune the whole graph:
613: 
614: - `ATS_HOOK_PROFILE` ∈ `{minimal, standard, strict}` (default `standard`).
615:   - `minimal` → `qa-gate` only (1 hook).
616:   - `standard` → `qa-gate` + `post-edit-format` + `session-start-profile` + `catalog-sync` + `skill-usage` (5 hooks).
617:   - `strict` → all six; `qa-gate` also treats a **missing** report as a block.
618: - `ATS_DISABLED_HOOKS` → comma-separated hook ids to force-off, e.g. `post-edit-format,pre-commit-lint`.
619: 
620: Only `qa-gate` ever blocks, and only on a real gate failure. It signals a block the Claude Code way — printing `{"decision":"block","reason":"…"}` to stdout and exiting `0`.
621: 
622: ### Install & enable
623: 
624: `scripts/convert.sh --tool claude-code` copies `hooks/` into `integrations/claude-code/hooks/` and generates `integrations/claude-code/hooks.json` (Claude Code settings-hook format). `scripts/install.sh` then copies that tree to a **namespaced** target — `~/.claude/ats-hooks/` — and writes `hooks.json` there. Install is **non-destructive**: it never auto-merges your `~/.claude/settings.json`. Instead it prints the exact snippet to merge into the `"hooks"` key. The wrapper is invoked as:
625: 
626: ```text
627: "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR"/.claude/ats-hooks/run-with-flags.sh <hook-id>
628: ```
629: 
630: ### Lint & test
631: 
632: ```bash
633: ./scripts/lint-hooks.sh          # validate the hooks tree (manifest, exec bits, headers, profiles)
634: bash tests/hooks/run-tests.sh    # the bats suite (wrapper gating, qa-gate, convert, lint)
635: ```
636: 
637: Both run in CI via the `Hooks Layer (Ubuntu)` job in `.github/workflows/lint-skills.yml`.
638: 
639: ---
640: 
641: ## 🛠️ Development
642: 
643: ### Run the lint locally
644: 
645: ```bash
646: ./scripts/lint-skills.sh
647: ```
648: 
649: That's the same command the gating Ubuntu CI job runs. If it's green locally on Linux, the PR will be green — the macOS job is a non-blocking compatibility smoke (continue-on-error).
650: 
651: ### Add a new skill
652: 
653: 1. Use the `skill-writer` skill: `"Generate a new skill for X."` — it scaffolds frontmatter + body in the right category dir.
654: 2. Or copy `skills/meta/skill-writer/references/body-template.md` and fill in by hand.
655: 3. Run `./scripts/lint-skills.sh skills/<category>/<your-skill>/` until clean.
656: 4. Run `/sync-skills` (or `./scripts/install.sh --tool claude-code`) so Claude Code picks it up.
657: 5. PR — CI gates on full-ecosystem lint.
658: 
659: ### Edit an existing skill
660: 
661: If you used `/sync-skills`, the symlinks make edits in `skills/` live in `~/.claude/skills/` immediately — no resync needed.
662: 
663: > **Keep skill bodies under 500 lines.** When detail spills over, move it to `references/` — that's what progressive disclosure is for.
664: 
665: ---
666: 
667: ## 🩺 Troubleshooting
668: 
669: <details>
670: <summary><b>"<code>/sync-skills</code> says skills already exist"</b></summary>
671: 
672: Existing files in `~/.claude/skills/<name>/` block the symlink. Either delete the existing dir or run with `--force` to overwrite. The `sync-skills` skill body documents the safe paths.
673: </details>
674: 
675: <details>
676: <summary><b>"Linter fails on macOS but passes on Linux (or vice versa)"</b></summary>
677: 
678: Almost always a `pyyaml` version skew. CI installs `pyyaml` explicitly on macOS — replicate locally with `python3 -m pip install pyyaml`. Linux runners include it preinstalled.
679: </details>
680: 
681: <details>
682: <summary><b>"My non-Claude-Code host doesn't see all 76 skills"</b></summary>
683: 
684: Expected. Skills with `requires_claude_code: true` — the `orchestrator`, all of `roles/`, all of `loops/`, and the workflows bound to Claude Code's runtime or `~/.claude` config — are skipped for the other hosts; 39 of the 76 skills convert today. `./scripts/convert.sh` prints one `[convert] skipping <category>/<slug> for <tool> (requires_claude_code: true)` line to stderr per skipped skill — no extra flag needed.
685: </details>
686: 
687: <details>
688: <summary><b>"Stderr is full of <code>stripped allowed-tools/owns</code> lines"</b></summary>
689: 
690: That's by design — every Claude-Code-only frontmatter field that gets stripped on conversion to another host is announced. It's not an error; it's the installer being honest. Pipe stderr to a log file if it's noisy: `./scripts/convert.sh 2> convert.log`.
691: </details>
692: 
693: <details>
694: <summary><b>"Orchestrator complains about file-ownership conflicts"</b></summary>
695: 
696: Two skills declare `owns.directories` or `owns.files` on overlapping paths. The lint output prints the offending pair — open both SKILL.md files and pick which one truly owns it. The orchestrator's canonical ownership map (in `skills/orchestrator/SKILL.md`) is the tiebreaker.
697: </details>
698: 
699: <details>
700: <summary><b>"<code>install.sh</code> can't find my host's install directory"</b></summary>
701: 
702: Set the override env var documented in `scripts/README.md` (e.g. `CURSOR_RULES_DIR=...`, `AIDER_CONVENTIONS_PATH=...`) before running. The defaults assume each host's standard location.
703: </details>
704: 
705: ---
706: 
707: ## 🗺️ Roadmap
708: 
709: - [x] **Skill library** — 76 skills, seven categories, all linted
710: - [x] **Multi-tool installer** — convert / install / lint, eleven host adapters
711: - [x] **CI matrix** — Ubuntu lint gate + macOS smoke on every push
712: - [x] **Contract-first specs** — OpenAPI / AsyncAPI / Pydantic / TypeScript / JSON Schema templates
713: - [x] **QA gate** — `qa-report.json` schema with critical / high / medium / low blockers
714: - [x] **Two-runtime degradation** — Agent Teams → subagents → sequential, host-detected
715: - [x] **Autonomous-loop library** — 13 loops on one `loop-controller` guardrail harness
716: - [x] **`/madness` front door** — one router that reads intent and launches the right skill
717: - [ ] **Image assets** — README hero, architecture diagram, host matrix illustration ⏳
718: - [ ] **End-to-end multi-agent verification on non-Claude-Code hosts** ⏳
719: - [ ] **Skill marketplace / registry** — discoverable installs, version pinning ⏳
720: - [ ] **Per-host CI smoke tests** — actually exercise each adapter's converted skills against a sample host ⏳
721: 
722: ---
723: 
724: ## 🧭 Key design decisions
725: 
726: - 📜 **One canonical format** — `SKILL.md` with YAML frontmatter is the source of truth; everything else is generated.
727: - 🚫 **Exclusive file ownership** — no two role agents own the same file. Conflicts resolved before spawn, not after.
728: - 🚦 **QA gate blocks** — critical issues or sub-threshold contract / security scores in `qa-report.json` fail the build. Self-declared "done" is not accepted.
729: - 🪜 **Progressive disclosure** — frontmatter (always loaded) → body (loaded on trigger) → references (loaded on demand). Big libraries, small contexts.
730: - 🪞 **Lossy conversion is announced** — every stripped field gets a stderr line per skill per host. The installer never silently drops content.
731: - 🛡️ **Fail loud, fail early** — frontmatter schema, ownership conflicts, broken cross-refs are all CI errors, not warnings.
732: - 🔁 **Symlinks > copies** — `/sync-skills` defaults to symlinking so edits in the repo are instantly live in every Claude Code session.
733: - 📣 **Pushy descriptions** — skill `description` fields intentionally over-enumerate trigger contexts. Under-triggering is a worse failure than over-triggering.
734: 
735: ---
736: 
737: ## 🤝 Contributing
738: 
739: PRs welcome — new skills, host adapter improvements, lint rules, examples, bug reports.
740: 
741: - New skill? Use `skill-writer` to scaffold it, then run `./scripts/lint-skills.sh` until clean.
742: - New host adapter? Add the converter to `scripts/convert.sh`, the installer destination to `scripts/install.sh`, and a fidelity row to `contracts/installer/per-tool-output-spec.md`.
743: - Bug report? Include the failing skill name and the lint output verbatim.

=== CLAUDE.md ===
1: # CLAUDE.md
2: 
3: > **Lost? Start at [`START-HERE.md`](START-HERE.md)** — status at a glance + which doc is canonical vs frozen vs archived.
4: > **Also read `AGENTS.md`** — it contains shared instructions for all AI agents working in this repo.
5: 
6: This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
7: 
8: ## What This Is
9: 
10: A multi-agent orchestration toolkit for Claude Code — 76 OSS-publishable skills in `skills/`. Skills are symlinked to `~/.claude/skills/` for global availability.
11: 
12: The toolkit targets Claude Code as the primary host but the SKILL.md format is platform-agnostic — Claude.ai, Copilot CLI, Codex, and Gemini CLI all consume it. Skills should describe work in terms of capabilities ("read the file", "run the command") rather than Claude-Code-specific tool names where reasonable, so the same skill body works across hosts.
13: 
14: ## Install / Sync
15: 
16: Use the `/sync-skills` command to create symlinks from `~/.claude/skills/` back to this repo. This keeps skills always in sync — edits in either location are reflected immediately.
17: 
18: ```bash
19: # Via slash command (recommended)
20: /sync-skills
21: 
22: # Manual: create category symlinks + flattened discovery symlinks
23: # See skills/workflows/sync-skills/SKILL.md for details
24: ```
25: 
26: ## Skill Anatomy
27: 
28: Every skill follows this structure:
29: 
30: ```text
31: skill-name/
32: ├── SKILL.md              # YAML frontmatter + markdown instructions (≤5,000 words; warn at 500 lines)
33: └── references/           # On-demand reference files (unlimited size)
34: ```
35: 
36: All SKILL.md files use the frontmatter convention defined in `skills/meta/skill-writer/references/frontmatter-spec.md`. This spec aligns with Anthropic's official Agent Skills standard. Required fields: `name` (kebab-case; `claude-*`/`anthropic-*` prefixes discouraged but allowed as documented exceptions), `version` (semver, top-level), `description` (trigger text, ≤1024 chars, no `<` or `>` in field values). Optional Anthropic fields: `compatibility`, `license`, `allowed-tools` (hyphen canonical; `allowed_tools` accepted as alias), `metadata`. Agent roles also declare `owns`, `composes_with`, `spawned_by`.
37: 
38: ## Skill Categories
39: 
40: - **`skills/orchestrator/`** (1) — Entry point. 14-phase build playbook, runtime detection, contract-first coordination. References: phase-guide, team-sizing, circuit-breaker, handoff-protocol.
41: - **`skills/roles/`** (10) — Implementation agents (backend, frontend, infrastructure, qe, security, performance, observability, docs, db-migration, code-review). Each has a SKILL.md + reference files with validation checklists.
42: - **`skills/contracts/`** (2) — contract-author (generates contracts from templates) and contract-auditor (verifies implementations match). Templates: OpenAPI, AsyncAPI, Pydantic, TypeScript, JSON Schema.
43: - **`skills/meta/`** (7) — skill-writer, skill-review, skill-update, skill-explorer, skill-catalog, madness (the front-door router: reads intent, picks the right starting skill — orchestrator / plan-builder / a loop / a role or workflow / skill-explorer — and launches it, confirming before anything expensive; the active counterpart to skill-explorer), model-adaptation (the model-migration reference: what to prune, what now triggers a `reasoning_extraction` refusal, and the long-run/effort scaffolding to add when the underlying Claude model changes — Fable 5 / Mythos 5 today; enforced via skill-review, loop-controller, and orchestrator).
44: - **`skills/git/`** (4) — Git workflow conventions: git-commit, git-pr, git-pr-feedback, git-post-merge-cleanup.
45: - **`skills/workflows/`** (39) — plan-builder, plan-intake, living-plan, context-manager, deployment-checklist, dependency-coordinator, project-profiler, wiki-research, interactive-doc, settings-consolidator, sync-skills, ui-brief, claude-design-brief, mermaid-charts, nano-banana, artifact-publish, playwright, render-sanity, design-token-guard, class-extraction-guard, prose-slop-guard, repo-deep-dive, llm-wiki, railway-deploy, use-freellmapi, architecture-rescue, caveman, diagnose-loop, grill-me, find-unknowns, maintain-context, zoom-out, setup-project-skills, work-item-brief, website-walkthrough-video, use-pxpipe, yagni-gate, payload-cms.
46: - **`skills/loops/`** (13) — Autonomous-loop skills. loop-controller (the foundation harness: the 5-part loop contract, primitive selection — `/goal` vs `/loop` vs Stop-hook vs bash Ralph vs dynamic workflows, the mandatory guardrail stack, and the fresh-context evaluator), fix-until-green (drive tests+lint+typecheck to passing without cheating the gate), orchestrator-task-loop (the outer loop over the Agent Teams shared task list — drain the board until every task is completed + passing its TaskCompleted gate, feeding idle workers via TeammateIdle; experimental, behind CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1), contract-conformance-loop (build-until-spec: implement until every authored-contract criterion holds, graded by a fresh-context evaluator subagent the builder can't self-grade — the loop form of the contract-author/contract-auditor pair), babysit (scheduled review-and-revise: keep a PR rebased and green by auto-addressing review comments via `/loop 5m /babysit`, with HITL on anything irreversible — the scheduled loop around git-pr-feedback), coverage-loop (grow the test suite to a coverage target without gaming the metric), perf-loop (profile → optimize → re-benchmark until a metric is under its budget under repeatable conditions, with no functional regression), self-healing-loop (watch logs/CI → root-cause → fix → verify → PR on a poll cadence, with HITL before any prod-touching fix), migration-loop (transform an enumerated target set one file per iteration until all are migrated, the suite is green, and no legacy pattern remains), nightly-docs-and-changelog (a `/schedule` routine that keeps an already-shipped repo's docs + changelog from rotting, surviving laptop-off), dependency-health-loop (scheduled audit + one-gated-bump-per-pass + green gate, HITL on major bumps), codebase-exploration-loop (fan-out read-only mappers until a written architecture summary answers every seed question — the loop form of repo onboarding), and repo-cleanup-loop (weekly evidence-gated branch/PR/worktree hygiene that recovers valuable work before deleting). Every concrete loop is a configuration of loop-controller.
47: 
48: ## Key Design Decisions
49: 
50: - **File ownership is exclusive** — no two agent roles can own the same file. The orchestrator resolves conflicts before spawning. The canonical ownership map lives in the orchestrator skill.
51: - **QE gates the build** — the qe-agent outputs `qa-report.json` per `skills/roles/qe-agent/references/qa-report-schema.json`. Build blocks on CRITICAL blockers or contract_conformance/security scores < 3.
52: - **Two-runtime degradation** — Agent Teams → subagents → sequential. Only the orchestrator needs this logic; role skills work standalone *of the runtime* (the same skill body works whether dispatched as an Agent Teams teammate, a subagent, or run sequentially) — they are still orchestrator-dispatched, not user-invocable.
53: - **Progressive disclosure** — frontmatter (~100 tokens) always loaded, SKILL.md body loaded on trigger, references loaded on demand.
54: - **Descriptions are "pushy"** — skill descriptions intentionally over-enumerate trigger contexts to combat under-triggering.
55: 
56: ## Editing Skills
57: 
58: When modifying a skill:
59: 
60: - Keep SKILL.md body ≤5,000 words (Anthropic guideline); soft warning past 500 lines. Move detail to `references/`. Heavy skills may exceed when the length is load-bearing.
61: - Description field is the primary trigger mechanism — include action verbs, specific contexts, and keyword variations
62: - `owns.directories` must not overlap with other agent roles
63: - Maintain the frontmatter convention (see `skills/meta/skill-writer/references/frontmatter-spec.md`)
64: - If using symlinks (default), edits are automatically reflected in both locations

19: 
20: ## Closed 2026-08-03 — deep-dive intake (`DV-1`–`DV-5`)
21: 
22: The 2026-08-03 **deep-dive vendor review** (a Freebuff/deepseek session — the first full cross-vendor pass since `model-adaptation`'s "never cross-vendor" doctrine landed; the irony is noted) was intaken via the repo's own `plan-intake` skill: 5 rows proposed with fresh `DV` IDs, explicitly approved ("let's implement"), and **implemented + closed same-session** on the Freebuff worktree branch. All five findings were verified live before proposal and re-verified after: hooks-suite TTY hang reproduced (hang forever → 45/45 in 21s), `--changed` guard confirmed wired-but-unenforced (SRQA-4's never-landed follow-up), README macOS overstatement confirmed at 5 spots vs `continue-on-error: true`, scaffold `0.1.0` confirmed in both plugin manifests, and the >950 band re-populated to 7 skills (SR12 trimmed only the orchestrator). Gate after the pass: lint 0 errors (116 warnings — unchanged, 0 new), catalog clean (73), hooks suite bare 45/45 in 21s, all fast + installer suites green (~346 tests), `--changed origin/main` 0 drifts. Sources: `[DV]` + per-row in-repo evidence (`.github/workflows/lint-skills.yml`, `hooks/scripts/qa-gate.sh`, `tests/run-all.sh`, `README.md`, `.claude-plugin/*.json`, `skill-review-report.md` M10).
23: 
24: - **DV-1** `[done · P1]` — **The hooks bats suite hangs forever on a TTY**, making `tests/run-all.sh` dead-on-arrival in a terminal — the repo's "if it's green locally, the PR will be green" story only held because CI runners close stdin. `qa-gate.sh` and `session-start-profile.sh` drain stdin (`cat >/dev/null`) and bats tests inherit open stdin, so every `qa-gate.sh` invocation blocked until EOF (the suite died at its first one). Fix: drain only when stdin is a pipe (`[[ ! -t 0 ]]`) in both hook scripts, and run bats with `< /dev/null` in `tests/run-all.sh` + `tests/hooks/run-tests.sh`. Observable change: the full suite completes in a terminal (~3 min). Source: `[DV]`. *(Closed: hooks suite 45/45 in 21s running bare with open stdin; root-cause guard also fixes a human running a hook script interactively.)*
25: - **DV-2** `[done · P2]` — **The SR13 version-drift guard (`lint-skills.sh --changed`) is not wired into CI**, so a SKILL.md bumped without a version bump sails through PRs. The sr-mt QA report recorded exactly this as **SRQA-4** ("add a PR-only CI step… needs fetch-depth to see the base ref") and it never landed. Fix: `fetch-depth: 0` on the lint-ubuntu checkout + a `Check version-drift guard` step running `scripts/lint-skills.sh --changed origin/main`; the tool's "not wired into CI" comment trued. Observable change: version-drift regressions fail the PR gate. Source: `[DV]` / `coordination/sr-mt-qa-report.json` SRQA-4. *(Closed: guard exits 0 on this tree — 7 changed SKILL.md, 0 drifts; the same command is now the CI step.)*
26: - **DV-3** `[done · P2]` — **README overstates macOS CI as a gating matrix** (lines 87, 496, 563, 646, 708) while the macOS job is `continue-on-error: true` — the yaml itself says "Not a merge gate". `scripts/README.md` was already honest (SR2-era); the root README was not. Fix: all five spots reworded to "Ubuntu gate + non-blocking macOS smoke". Observable change: nobody plans around a gate that doesn't exist. Source: `[DV]`. *(Closed: zero residual "Ubuntu + macOS matrix" phrasing; the FAQ pyyaml + bash-3.2 lines verified accurate and left.)*
27: - **DV-4** `[done · P3]` — **Plugin manifests stuck at the scaffold version 0.1.0** while the corpus ships PSFS v1.1.0 and skills at v1–2.x. Fix: bumped `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (metadata + plugins entry) to 1.0.0. Observable change: the plugin's marketplace display reflects maturity, not pre-release. Source: `[DV]`. *(Closed: all three version fields at 1.0.0; catalog.sh --check unaffected — it guards counts, not versions.)*
28: - **DV-5** `[done · P3]` — **Seven descriptions re-populated the >950 danger band** — SR12 shipped the warn band but trimmed only the orchestrator (1023→935), and by 2026-08-03 seven skills sat within 7–60 chars of the 1024 hard ceiling (model-adaptation 1017, living-plan 1012, find-unknowns 1001, website-walkthrough-video 991, class-extraction-guard 984, artifact-publish 979, design-token-guard 964). Fix: trimmed each under 950 — prose/detail cut (e.g. capability-handoff example wording, the "like a real person scrolling" simile), every trigger phrase kept — two model-adaptation triggers (fable handoff, model leaving plan) were relocated into the skill body's Capability-handoff section rather than dropped, so the strings stay greppable without re-inflating the description — and each bumped per the `--changed` law (model-adaptation 1.4.1, living-plan 1.2.1, find-unknowns 1.0.1, class-extraction-guard 1.0.2, artifact-publish 1.0.1, website-walkthrough-video 1.1.2, design-token-guard 1.0.2). Observable change: no routine description edit can unexpectedly hard-fail the schema check. === docs/FUTURE.md ===
1: # Future — Frontier (out of scope)
2: 
3: **Last updated:** 2026-07-21 (WC-3 parked as an F1 sub-note + PX-4 parked from the pxpipe dive)
4: **Companions:** [`PLAN.md`](../PLAN.md), [`docs/REMAINING-WORK.md`](REMAINING-WORK.md)
5: 
6: Items explicitly out of scope for the current plan. Kept here so they aren't lost; pulled into the ledger only if a future cycle prioritizes them.
7: 
8: ## F1 — Multi-host installer reach ("convergence frontier")
9: The `agency-agents` comparison (184 agents, 11-tool install reach) sketches a ~10-day roadmap to broaden where skills can install (Copilot, Cursor-native, and other hosts) while preserving Skill-Madness's moats (contract layer, QA gate). Aspirational, not committed.
10: Source: `../DeepResearch/AllTheSkills/agency-agents_deepdive/source-material/11-convergence-frontier.md`.
11: Sub-note (2026-07-21, `[WCT]`, parked WC-3): if/when host #12 lands, consider the ai-website-cloner-template pattern — a declarative `hosts.yaml` data file driving the converter instead of one shell function per host. Refactoring a working generator for an aspirational need fails the YAGNI ladder today.
12: 
13: ## F2 — Skill marketplace / registry
14: A discoverable registry for publishing/pulling skills with remote version pinning (the `source` field reserved in `skills-lock.json` hints at this). No design committed.
15: 
16: ## F3 — Per-host CI smoke tests
17: End-to-end verification that converted skills actually load and run on each non-Claude-Code host (not just that conversion produces a file). Today CI validates frontmatter, lint, catalog, hooks, and scan — but not live execution per host.
18: 
19: ## F4 — README / docs image assets
20: Hero image, architecture diagram, and host-fidelity matrix graphic for the README. Cosmetic; deferred until the doc-polish backlog (M4) clears.
21: 
22: ---
23: 
24: F5–F18 below are the new-skill ideas from the **2026-07-03 full-library review** (two independent ideation agents — a Claude Code platform-surface audit and an external-ecosystem/lifecycle scan — deduped against all 71 skills). Full pitches with triggers, DoD, and difficulty live in the report's appendix: [`skill-review-report.md`](../skill-review-report.md) § "Fresh Build Ideas". Suggested first wave: F5 + F8 + F13; identity-defining second wave: F6 + F7 + F9. The review's idea #20 (`model-router`) is absorbed by ledger entry MT-1, not listed here.
25: 
26: ## F5 — `skill-eval` (meta, M/L)
27: Measured trigger-precision/recall harness for the library's own skills: generated should-trigger / should-NOT-trigger prompt sets run in clean-context subagents, rubric-scored behavior, description A/B, threshold gate. Both ideation agents' independent #1 — skill-review currently punts evals to external `/skill-creator` "if available", so the "pushy descriptions" value prop is never measured. Closes the loop: skill-writer authors → skill-eval scores → skill-update fixes.
28: 
29: **Efficacy extension — `skill-optimize` (SkillOpt / `[SO]`, intaken 2026-07-21).** F5 as pitched measures *triggerability* (does a skill fire on the right prompts). SkillOpt (microsoft/SkillOpt) adds the orthogonal axis — *efficacy*: does the skill's content actually make the agent score higher on a held-out task set — plus an optimizer that edits the skill and keeps a change **only on strict measured improvement** (a held-out validation gate, SkillOpt's `evaluate_gate`; ungated self-improvement produced a −52.8pt collapse, see `SO-5`). Fold this in as F5's second mode: author → score *triggerability AND efficacy* → gate edits on measured improvement → `skill-update` fixes. Requires the split-hygiene standard (`SO-4`, shipped as [`docs/standards/eval-split-hygiene.md`](standards/eval-split-hygiene.md)) and a checkable signal per skill — free for build-category skills (tests/lint/bats), a rubric-judge for softer ones (so it lands cleanly on loops/roles/git, not design/narrative skills). Source: `[SO]` = the 2026-07-20 SkillOpt deep dive (`../DeepResearch/skillopt_deepdive/source-material/`, esp. `13-convergence-analysis.md` §C1 + `14-frontier-assessment.md` SO-1).
30: 
31: **Grading discipline — acceptance vs cost (harness-engineering / `[HE]`, intaken 2026-07-22).** The two axes above say *what* to measure (triggerability, efficacy); this says *how to grade so the number means something*. From harness-engineering's `evals/` method, four rules F5 should adopt: **(1) grade four results separately** — Outcome (did the artifact satisfy the behavioral claim), Proof (does the evidence establish it *in the real environment*, not via an evaluator-only check), Architecture (did it preserve the owning invariant / `owns` boundary), and Trajectory diagnostics (latency, retries, tokens, human attention) — where **acceptance = outcome + proof + architecture** and trajectory cost only *explains* the result; never blend them into one score. **(2) Pre-register the falsifying result** before running, alongside the expected effect. **(3) Apply the nine disqualifiers** that invalidate a run: treatment never retrieved or invoked; an out-of-band instruction/tool/authority/state difference; a single rollout treated as representative; token/line/activity counts standing in for outcomes; evaluator-only checks reported as worker proof; worker changed mid-comparison; grader needing an undisclosed reference implementation; a corpus agreeing with its own shadow authority; opaque targets. **(4) Separate calibration from measurement** — a case whose answer is already disclosed in-repo (or plausibly in training data) can tune fixtures and graders but **cannot support a capability claim**; that needs a held-out target with a documented exclusion scan. Directly addresses the failure the functional audit already found — skills claiming QE scores that don't exist — which is the corpus's `proof` thesis verbatim: *a green check proves only its own assertion*. Source: `[HE]` = the 2026-07-22 harness-engineering deep dive (`../DeepResearch/harness-engineering_deepdive/source-material/`, esp. `05-playbooks-and-evals.md` + `09-frontier-assessment.md` §1).
32: 
33: ## F6 — `mock-from-contract` (contracts, M)
34: Serve a runnable, schema-faithful mock server (deterministic seeded responses) from an authored OpenAPI/AsyncAPI contract, so consumer agents build against a live fake before the backend exists. Completes the owned pipeline: contract-author → mock → parallel build → contract-conformance-loop. Would be `contracts/`' first addition since v1.
35: 
36: ## F7 — `hook-forge` (meta, L)
37: Author, install, and test hooks across ALL event types (PreToolUse/PostToolUse/Stop/SessionStart/Teammate/Task): matcher + script, dry-run, settings.json wiring, verify-it-fires. Every gate in the library today is advisory/skippable — this is the deterministic-enforcement layer ("make render-sanity a non-bypassable Stop hook"). Also fixes SR3's class of problem (hooks that exist but never get wired).
38: 
39: ## F8 — `release-cut` (git, M)
40: Infer the semver bump from conventional commits, bump version fields everywhere (including skill frontmatter + manifests), generate release notes, tag, open the GitHub release. Triple-evidenced internally: RV8 + the three new drift instances in SR13 + the recurring-pain rule. Root-cause companion to SR13's lint check.
41: 
42: ## F9 — `autonomy-profile` (loops, M)
43: Design the scoped permission allowlist + sandbox profile a given loop needs to run unattended: enumerate exact tools/paths, generate the settings block, verify a dry pass never prompts. All 13 loops currently hand-wave this inline; the single biggest unblock for the loops category's walk-away promise.
44: 
45: ## F10 — `mcp-server-author` (workflows, M/L)
46: Scaffold, implement, and smoke-test a stdio MCP server exposing a project's own capabilities to Claude Code, then register it. The toolkit consumes a dozen MCPs and can't build one; natural companion to contract-author (a tool schema is a contract).
47: 
48: ## F11 — `incident-response` + `postmortem` (workflows, M + S)
49: The operate-end pair: a HITL live-incident harness (severity, running timeline, mitigate-before-RCA, approval before prod-touching actions) and the blameless retro whose action items feed plan-intake. Today the only operate-stage skill is autonomous (self-healing-loop) — no human coordination layer, no retro artifact.
50: 
51: ## F12 — `memory-curator` (meta, M)
52: Set up + maintain the auto-memory convention (one-fact-per-file, MEMORY.md index, frontmatter types): dedupe, lint stale entries, verify referenced files/flags still exist. The maintainer already runs exactly this workflow by hand with zero tooling.
53: 
54: ## F13 — `notify-on-event` (workflows, S)
55: Wire PushNotification / SendMessage so long builds and loops ping the user on completion or at HITL gates. Zero PushNotification references exist in the library, yet the loop identity depends on reaching you when it matters. Smallest idea on the list; best effort-to-value ratio.
56: 
57: ## F14 — `worktree-fanout` (workflows, M)
58: Per-agent worktree isolation on the Agent-Teams build path (+ merge-back protocol), upgrading the orchestrator's exclusive-file-ownership from convention to physical impossibility. (`/batch` auto-fanout + repo-cleanup teardown exist; the gap is the manual build path.)
59: 
60: ## F15 — `headless-runner` (workflows, M/L)
61: Package a loop as a non-interactive job: `claude -p` streaming-JSON, exit codes, result parsing, or a thin Agent SDK app — so fix-until-green/self-healing-loop can run in GitHub Actions/cron instead of an open terminal.
62: 
63: ## F16 — `routine-manager` (loops, M)
64: Author/list/monitor scheduled cloud agents generally: routine catalog, "did last night's run actually fire", failure drift. nightly-docs, dependency-health, and self-healing each re-derive scheduling ad hoc; this is the shared substrate.
65: 
66: ## F17 — Quality-gates family (bundle)
67: Seven sibling gate/audit skills, each S–M, pick by appetite: `a11y-audit` (WCAG 2.2/axe — note ux-review/playwright *mention* "accessibility audit" but never run one), `secret-scan-gate` (staged-diff secrets before commit/PR), `license-audit` (SBOM + copyleft + declared-license check — RV2-evidenced), `flake-hunter` (N reruns under jitter; a gate that protects the gates), `threat-model` (pre-build STRIDE), `adr-author` (write/supersede/index ADRs), `load-test` (k6/Locust vs an SLO).
68: 
69: ## F18 — Remaining tier-3 ideas (bundle)
70: `browser-authed-flows` (claude-in-chrome for logged-in testing — all current browser skills are unauthed Playwright), `plugin-packager` (concrete packaging step under F2), `cache-optimizer` (prompt-cache-aware agent prompts + measured cost delta), `feature-flag`, `seed-data-forge`, `human-onboarding`, `i18n-audit`.
